# Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā 2000

## Introduction by Rañjita dāsa Adhikārī

The Vyāsa-pūjā book is the annual tribute to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda from his disciples and grand-disciples around the world. It is an offering to him and carries the prayers and hopes of those who serve his mission. It is a pilgrimage of the pen to a personality who can purify even the holy places. It is a great good fortune for us that the Vaiṣṇava tradition demands that at least once a year we are obligated to undertake this meditation on his person and teachings.

Śrīla Prabhupāda taught that we are at the beginning of a golden age of Kali-yuga that will last for ten thousand years. It is reported that in the course of a conversation he said that his books would be the law books for those ten thousand years. What a breathtaking, bold statement! Practically the oldest human remains still existing today are the pyramids of Egypt, which are only five thousand years old. They are in considerable disrepair, and the culture that created them has been extinct more than two thousand years. No empire in modern history has lasted even a thousand years.

In his preface to the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* Śrīla Prabhupāda describes the work as “a cultural presentation for the respiritualization of the entire human society.” This again is as striking and brave a statement as one will find by any philosopher or conqueror. Śrīla Prabhupāda clearly intended his work to have the greatest impact on history that one can imagine!

This being the year 2000, the dawn of a new millennium, it is perhaps appropriate to look at the long-term prospects for the teachings of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, founder-*ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness and the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust.

There is a theory that people become more skeptical about the actual existence of a real person as time goes on. In his 17th-century work *Theologiæ Christianæ Principia Mathematica,* a mathematician and theologian by the name of Craig calculated that if the Gospels of the New Testament had not been written, people would have considered Jesus a myth by about the year 800. But the existence of these works, he estimated, will hold off the disbelief in Jesus till about 3,150. It is interesting to note that today, about 5,000 years after Śrī Kṛṣṇa left the earth planet, many people, even in India, think He was not a real person.

The preaching strategy of Śrīla Prabhupāda and his ISKCON society dovetails extremely well with a good preservation strategy. This strategy is *saṅkīrtana,* mainly through book distribution. No organization emphasizes book distribution as much as ISKCON and the BBT. It is this book production and distribution to as many people as possible, with translation into as many languages as possible, that will be the most potent force for the preservation of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s message. But book distribution alone may not be enough to preserve the understanding that Śrīla Prabhupāda was a flesh-and-blood person. To hark back to our point concerning “the mathematics of mythology,” even the author of the *Vedas,* Vyāsadeva, is considered mythological. After all, people say, how could only one man produce so many books? One day the same may well be said of Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Fortunately, the Vyāsa-pūjā book, with its pictures, is there to show the “real” man. And the pictures show him surrounded by other “real” men. We who were in the presence of such a great personality have a responsibility to preserve his legacy. Increasingly, the news of his followers is that this one or that one has passed away, this one or that one just had by-pass heart surgery, this one or that one has fallen to some life-threatening disease. The more evidence that can be accumulated regarding the actual historicity of Śrīla Prabhupāda, the more chance there is for his acceptance as the principal *ācārya* for the next ten thousand years. The followers of Śrīla Prabhupāda now on the planet are the first two generations of disciples. If we don’t record these things, who will? If not now, when? We are indebted to such authors as Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami, Hari Śauri Prabhu, and Śrutakīrti Prabhu, who have written wonderful biographical books on Śrīla Prabhupāda, and we eagerly await the publications of such authors as Mukunda Goswami and Govinda-devī dāsī, who also had the mercy of lots of his personal association.

On a personal note, for several years it fell to me to choose the pictures for the Vyāsa-pūjā book from the 18,000 slides and 13,000 negatives of Śrīla Prabhupāda in the Bhaktivedanta Archives. Around 150 pictures have to be selected. They have to be “good” (i.e., in focus with reasonable lighting), and preferably they should not have appeared in the book before, or at least not recently. Some should be serious, some smiling, some should show interaction with devotees or guests, some at programs, some solitary. This service is a wonderful meditation. It has to be one of the more nectarean engagements in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and the nectar is even sweeter when the book comes out and you actually see the printed result.

Although when we see a picture of Śrīla Prabhupāda we sometimes think we can tell what his mood was at that moment, we can never truly know what the pure devotee was thinking. There is one particular set of photos in which Śrīla Prabhupāda seems to be very deep in serious meditation. The focus is perfect, the lighting good, and the best pictures in the series have been used in many publications. I have admired these pictures for years, and frequently picked one or more of them for the Vyāsa-pūjā book. One day the devotee-photographer who shot them paid a visit to the Bhaktivedanta Archives and happened to notice them. He explained to me that at the time Śrīla Prabhupāda was listening to one of his *sannyāsī* disciples criticize other disciples and their management of the society’s affairs. When I realized that Śrīla Prabhupāda’s superintense expression in those pictures had arisen out of his concern for his society of devotees, I was moved to meditate on how great was his care for us and how much we owe him.

Savior of the whole world Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!

Rañjita dāsa adhikārī  
Bhaktivedanta Archives

## The Meaning of Vyasa Puja by Nārāyaṇī-devī dāsī

Śrīla Vyāsadeva, a literary incarnation of God, begins his glorification of Lord Kṛṣṇa by saying *oṁ namo bhagavate vāsudevāya*. After offering worship to Lord Kṛṣṇa, he gave this benediction to all: Lord Kṛṣṇa will be captured within the heart of anyone who hears *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* attentively and submissively. This is the process of *paramparā*. The enlightened souls worship their spiritual masters and Kṛṣṇa, receive the mercy flowing down, and then distribute it to those who would accept it. At the same time, the spiritual masters receive our offerings of love and deliver them to Lord Kṛṣṇa through this chain of disciplic succession. While the worship flows upward toward Kṛṣṇa, the mercy flows downward to us. Śrīla Prabhupāda, as the representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva, also worshiped Lord Kṛṣṇa, while simultaneously giving benediction to all members of suffering humanity who would listen to his messages and follow him.

Śrīla Vyāsadeva concludes his first prayer with the word *dhīmahi*. In 1971, in Gorakhpur, India, Śrīla Prabhupāda explained this word on the Vyāsa-pūjā day of his spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī. Śrīla Prabhupāda explained that by using this word Śrīla Vyāsadeva is joining with all his disciples in worshiping Lord Kṛṣṇa:

And we shall together offer flowers. Actually, the worship is..., that is my duty. But *sagoṣṭhī*. Now I’ll have to offer my respect with my spiritual family members, *sagoṣṭhī*. That is called *sagoṣṭhī*. Just like Vyāsadeva says, *dhīmahi*. He’s offering prayer *sagoṣṭhī*, with all his followers and disciples. That is the process.

In our temples in India we often see an Indian woman come to the temple holding one child on her hip, another by the hand, and perhaps still another in her abdomen, yet to be born. When she bows down to offer obeisances, all her children bow also. Similarly, Śrīla Prabhupāda saw all living entities as part of Kṛṣṇa’s family, and he desired to induce them all to surrender at Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet. Śrīla Prabhupāda’s satisfaction was to see everyone happy by chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, taking *prasādam,* and reading Kṛṣṇa’s books.

On this auspicious day we celebrate and glorify Śrīla Prabhupāda’s powerful connection with Śrīla Vyāsadeva and the whole *paramparā*. We celebrate Śrīla Prabhupāda’s extraordinary qualities and his unique mission of delivering Kṛṣṇa consciousness to all countries of the world. As Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura writes in his *Gurv-aṣṭaka, saṁsāra-dāvānala-līḍha-loka:* the whole world is burning in the fire of unending distress, and there is no escape by material means. Śrīla Prabhupāda is receiving benediction from the ocean of mercy flowing down from Lord Kṛṣṇa to Brahmā, to Nārada, to Vyāsa, and to Lord Caitanya and his followers, and Śrīla Prabhupāda is distributing this mercy to refresh us and liberate us from the fire of material desire, which is burning our hearts. Śrīla Prabhupāda gave us the message of Lord Kṛṣṇa without change, exactly as it was spoken thousands of years before. Therefore we can experience the full potency of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s personal presence even today. Yet Śrīla Prabhupāda did not simply repeat Kṛṣṇa’s words like a parrot. As Vyāsa said about Śukadeva Gosvāmī, *śuka-mukhād amṛta-drava-saṁyutam*. Because Śukadeva touched it with his lips, the *Bhāgavatam* became even sweeter and more easily understood. In the same way, Śrīla Prabhupāda’s purports have changed the hearts of people worldwide and effected “a revolution in the impious lives of this world’s misdirected civilization.”

Many Indian swamis went to the Western countries before Śrīla Prabhupāda, but no Westerners became Vaiṣṇavas. Once one such swami gave him some advice. Śrīla Prabhupāda relates,

\[The swami said,\] “You are going to a foreign country and you do not know which hand to take this fork and knife.” So I told him, “I am not going to learn all these things. I am going to teach them something else, to forget it.”

Although preaching boldly, Śrīla Prabhupāda remained humble. Whenever he was praised he would give all credit to his spiritual master and sometimes even to his own disciples. In 1970 Śrīla Prabhupāda wrote in a letter:

Yes, if it is any credit for me, that is what you have written, that I tried to give aural reception to the words of my Spiritual Master, Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Goswami Maharaja, and thus I tried to engage my tongue in repeating the same words without any change. Actually I am the most unworthy servant of His Divine Grace because I delayed to execute His order by so many years. But it was better I began late than never, and therefore He has kindly sent to me so many young hearts to cooperate with me in this great Movement. Please therefore continue your good cooperation, and I am sure great things will happen without delay.

Śrīla Prabhupāda was the embodiment of compassion. Just as Śrīla Vyāsadeva, feeling compassion, wrote *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* to give the world light in this dark age, Śrīla Prabhupāda carried this torchlight of knowledge to all corners of the world to illuminate everyone’s heart. Just as Advaita Ācārya cried loudly for Lord Kṛṣṇa to descend in Navadvīpa, Śrīla Prabhupāda called Kṛṣṇa to appear in temples worldwide and also as *harināma* on the streets of the world’s major cities, from New York to Paris to Sydney to Tokyo. And just as Lord Caitanya chased the conditioned souls all over India to bring them back to Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet, Śrīla Prabhupāda traveled all over the world to pick up the *mlecchas* and convert them into Vaiṣṇavas.

When glorified for his amazing achievements, Śrīla Prabhupāda said (in paraphrase),

Sometimes people give me so much honor. “Swamijī, you have created something wonderful.” I do not feel that I have created something wonderful. What have I done? I say that I’m not a magician. I am simply presenting *Bhagavad-gītā* as it is, that’s all. If there is any credit, this is the only credit. Anyone can do it. The *Bhagavad-gītā* is there, and anyone can present *Bhagavad-gītā* as it is. So it will act wonderfully. I am not a magician. I do not know the tricks of magic or the *yoga-siddhis;* I have no such power. So, my only credit is that I do not want to mix this pure *Bhagavad-gītā* teaching with any rascaldom, that’s all. That is my credit. And whatever little miracle has been done, it is only on this principle.

On this Vyāsa-pūjā day we pray to rededicate ourselves to pleasing Śrīla Prabhupāda, to expanding his mission, to helping others understand the compassion, the humor, the humility, the determination of this great soul who walked among us. We pray to reaffirm our vows to be faithful to him and thus strengthen our connection with the *paramparā* by worshiping him, knowing that our worship will flow up through Śrīla Prabhupāda and the disciplic succession to Lord Kṛṣṇa. At the same time, let us be receptive to the mercy flowing down through Śrīla Prabhupāda to us. As members of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s family, we pray to carry on the family tradition and keep the *paramparā* alive by distributing the legacy of mercy we have received so that Kṛṣṇa’s family members will increase. We pray to bring as our offering to Śrīla Prabhupāda people from every country of the world to offer themselves in surrender at his lotus feet.

Nārāyaṇī-devī dāsī  
Vṛndāvana, India

## “Adore, Adore Ye All the Happy Day”

Adore adore ye all,  
The happy day.  
Blessed than Heaven,  
Sweeter than May.  
When He appeared at Puri,  
The holy place,  
My Lord and Master  
His Divine Grace.

Oh! my Master  
The evangelic angel.  
Give us thy light,  
Light up thy candle.  
Struggle for existence  
A Human race,  
The only hope  
His Divine Grace.

Misled we are  
All going astray,  
Save us Lord  
Our fervent pray,  
Wonder thy ways  
To turn our face  
Adore thy feet  
Your Divine Grace.

Forgotten Krishna  
We fallen soul.  
Paying most heavy  
The illusion's toll.  
Darkness around  
All untrace,  
The only hope  
His Divine Grace.

Message of service  
Thou has brought.  
A healthful life  
As Chaitanya wrought.  
Unknown to all  
It's full of brace.  
That's your gift  
Your Divine Grace.

Absolute is sentient  
Thou hast proved.  
Impersonal calamity  
Thou hast moved.  
This gives us a life  
Anew and fresh.  
Worship thy feet  
Your Divine Grace.

Had you not come  
Who had told,  
The message of Krishna  
Forceful and bold,  
That's your right  
You have the mace.  
Save me a fallen  
Your Divine Grace.

The line of service  
As drawn by you,  
Is pleasing and healthy  
Like morning dew.  
The oldest of all  
But in new dress  
Miracle done  
Your Divine Grace.

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from the GBC

## Anuttama dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances.

Before becoming your disciple, I read several versions of the *Bhagavad-gītā*. All of them seemed very mystical, yet none gave me a clear sense of understanding God, nor any practical solutions to my problems.

When I read your purports to the *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is,* however, I was struck by the cohesion of the Vaiṣṇava philosophy, and I was convinced to seek a Kṛṣṇa conscious solution to the conflicted world I faced. On this auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, I meditate on three of your purports that are among my favorites. By remembering your *vāṇī* I am inspired, and I appreciate more and more the gifts you have given us.

*Bhagavad-gītā As It Is,* verse 3.29, purport:

Men who are ignorant cannot appreciate activities in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and therefore Lord Kṛṣṇa advises us not to disturb them and simply waste valuable time. *But the devotees of the Lord are more kind than the Lord because they understand the purpose of the Lord*. Consequently they undertake all kinds of risks, even to the point of approaching ignorant men to try to engage them in the acts of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, which are absolutely necessary for the human being. \[emphasis added\]

*Bhagavad-gītā As It Is,* verse 12.11, purport:

It is also found that when one who is not even interested in the activities of Kṛṣṇa consciousness gives charity to some hospital or some other social institution, he gives up the hard-earned results of his activities. This is also recommended here because by the practice of giving up the fruits of one’s activities one is sure to purify his mind gradually, and in that purified stage of mind one becomes able to understand Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Of course, Kṛṣṇa consciousness is not dependent on any other experience, because Kṛṣṇa consciousness itself can purify one’s mind, but if there are impediments to accepting Kṛṣṇa consciousness, one may try to give up the results of his actions. In that respect, social service, community service, national service, sacrifice for one’s country, etc., may be accepted so that some day one may come to the stage of pure devotional service to the Lord.

*Bhagavad-gītā As It Is,* verse 9.30, purport:

On the other hand, one should not misunderstand that a devotee in transcendental devotional service can act in all kinds of abominable ways; this verse only refers to an accident due to the strong power of material connections. Devotional service is more or less a declaration of war against the illusory energy. As long as one is not strong enough to fight the illusory energy, there may be accidental falldowns. But when one is strong enough, he is no longer subjected to such falldowns, as previously explained. *No one should take advantage of this verse and commit nonsense and think that he is still a devotee. If he does not improve in his character by devotional service, then it is to be understood that he is not a high devotee*. \[emphasis added\]

Śrīla Prabhupāda, here you outline essential concepts of living a Kṛṣṇa conscious life. On your Vyāsa-pūjā day, protect us so that we don’t simply remember your teachings and pastimes as historical concepts. Please let the reality of your divine instructions enter our hearts to guide us throughout the year.

To grow your movement, we must remember your instruction that devotees of the Lord must become more merciful than the Lord because we know the purpose of the Lord. Please inspire us to sacrifice our own comfort for the well-being of others, just as you did. Please rid us of complacency and arrogance, two great enemies of the neophytes.

To grow your movement, we must encourage all endeavors toward Kṛṣṇa, be they big or small. Please free us from envy, that we might see every pious act of the conditioned soul as a small step toward Kṛṣṇa. Please bless your society so that we can be a place of encouragement for sincere seekers of God. We are not meant to be isolated islands of practitioners, but kind and humble communities reaching out with compassion to others, whatever their spiritual status and needs might be.

To grow your movement, we must set examples of piety and God conscious behavior. Let us give up the ignorant idea that we are special for any reason other than the mercy you have granted us. Let us remember that while Kṛṣṇa forgives our shortcomings, we must strive with vigilance to uphold the principles of truthfulness, cleanliness, mercy, and austerity. Without exemplifying these Vaiṣṇava qualities, both individually and in our communities, our efforts to preach to others will ring hollow and be of little impact.

Your servant,  
Anuttama dāsa

## Badrinārāyaṇ dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

When standing on a mountain, one does not realize the mass or height of the peak. One is too close and sees only the trees, rocks, and other scenery. By traveling to the base of the mountain, one begins to get a sense of the scale, but only from a distant vantage point out on the plains can one understand the mountain’s true prominence.

With the passage of each year, I have greater respect for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Not only did you expertly present the most profound and sublime philosophy, you personally lived it at every moment and in your every action. You simultaneously crafted the ways and means via your Bhaktivedanta purports and your ISKCON for even us to realize it, if we would but follow you.

You were expert at timely *sūtras* (known in the West as “one-liners”). Asked why the death rate was so high in India, you replied, “The death rate is the same everywhere, 100%.” You were asked if the world was flat and replied, “Everywhere I walk it is,” and then coyly added in an aside, “except for New Vrindāban.” One man stood up and challenged you during a temple room lecture before the Deities, “Can you see God?” You replied strongly, “Yes, but you are standing in the way.”

You related how you stood on the deck of the *Jaladuta* in Boston Harbor and wondered how you would preach to these Western people immersed in the modes of passion and ignorance. Would any of them be able to take to the path of devotional service? You prayed for some indication and then saw a sign advertising “Unalloyed Steel.” You took it as a confirmation that you should preach boldly and without compromise.

I was thinking about your wonderful qualities and, rapt in thought, I almost bumped into a sidewalk advertising sign. It read “Often imitated, never duplicated.” You have often been imitated, but you will never be duplicated. May I forever feel indebted to you and engage in your service.

Badrinārāyaṇ dāsa

## Bhakti Caitanya Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to your divine self!

Having to accept more responsibility within your ISKCON movement has helped me appreciate the efforts and sacrifices you made to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Actually I cannot understand how you did it. For me to do such a thing would be completely inconceivable. But somehow you were driven on to do so much, and you were able to do it so effectively that now your ISKCON is spread all over the world, and into the hearts of so many millions of people.

What were you driven by? Nothing other than unalloyed love for the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Kṛṣṇa, and for your spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. That is the difference between you and me, between you and your contemporaries, and between you and everyone else. You were absolutely focused on the lotus feet of your worshipable superiors, and as a result you received their absolute mercy in all respects.

Your example teaches me that in Kṛṣṇa consciousness there are no clever tricks or shortcuts. There is only one way which is the right way, and there are no alternative routes, but only diversions presented by the illusory energy, Māyā.

Your example teaches me that love is not a cheap thing, but a matter of all-out endeavor to serve the instructions and mission of one’s spiritual master and Kṛṣṇa. You put it so nicely in a *Bhagavad-gītā* lecture: “So this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement means how to become dear to Kṛṣṇa: ‘How Kṛṣṇa will love me.’”

Your example teaches me that there is no compromise in the matter of chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa without offenses. I remember being present when you were giving *darśana* in Bhaktivedanta Manor once, and a devotee referred to a passage you wrote in the eighth chapter of *The Nectar of Devotion:* “The chanting of Hare Kṛṣṇa is beneficial for eradicating all sins, but if one becomes an offender to the holy names of the Lord, then he has no chance of being delivered.” The devotee appealed to you, that “isn’t there some way of becoming free from these offenses?” You looked at him very gravely and replied, “You are asking this question because you want to offend. Then you want to do something to escape those reactions. But you must stop your offenses. No more offenses.”

Your example teaches me that our lives, to the very end, must be dedicated to preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Even though you mentioned sometimes about a spiritual master retiring from preaching and performing *nirjana-bhajana,* still your personal example is vividly clear. It seems that *nirjana-bhajana* may not be for us, really, but rather we should consider ourselves fortunate if, in time, we can “die with our boots on,” actively engaged on the transcendental battlefield of preaching.

Your example teaches me that compassion for the fallen souls is the emotion of the advanced devotees. When that appears in our hearts, then we can perhaps consider that we have made substantial progress in our devotional service. Until then, let humble surrender at the lotus feet of you in the form of your instructions be our hallmark, and let us run far from the witch of pretentiousness. And when we come to that advanced stage, that humble surrender will be our all in all.

These are just a few lessons I have learned from your example. They are just a drop in an ocean. You are the best, and your example is the best, and those who follow your example will become the best among their peers and will reflect you and your mercy into the hearts of followers in the generations to come. I am praying at your lotus feet to please allow me to associate with such wonderful devotees, eternally.

Your humble servant,  
Bhakti Caitanya Swami

## Bhakti Cāru Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

On this auspicious day, while trying to glorify Your Divine Grace, I became painfully aware of my miserable inability to comprehend your actual greatness and real glory. I often intellectually try to measure them; however, I soon become aware of my inability. Your incredible achievements are standing in front of our eyes with all there splendor and vividness. The entire world today is receiving the benefit from your most precious spiritual gift; nevertheless, often our ungrateful mind, inebriated with false ego, fails to register them.

Your greatness becomes so obviously manifest when we see how within the short span of eleven years you fulfilled the prediction of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Yet today, due to your absence, the spiritual empire you created is crumbling. So many devotees have left the movement, and for various reasons the GBC body has not been able to cope effectively with the attacks that are causing countless casualties. Nevertheless, due to your inconceivable spiritual potency and causeless mercy, ISKCON is still standing with its full glory—so many people are becoming inspired by your books, and so many of them want to offer themselves to your mission. The festivals organized by your devotees successfully attract so many people and so effectively inject Kṛṣṇa consciousness into their hearts. On the other hand, it becomes obvious that those who have left ISKCON are not really happy. Though they may even rally around some great spiritual personalities, still, they are not really able to effectively preach the message of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. At most they just manage to steal a few devotees from your ISKCON and increase the number of their clan.

My mind is not able to comprehend your greatness, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but I have implicit faith that one day the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement will spread all over the world, to every town and village. That will happen through your ISKCON. And in that ISKCON your position as the founder-*ācārya* will be established just like Jesus Christ’s position in Christianity and Mohammed’s in Islam. Then the entire world will recognize how Kṛṣṇa wants to glorify His pure devotee. From the way He glorifies you He will show how great a devotee you are and how dear you are to Him.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu empowered you to achieve something that is greater even than His own achievement, for which He is glorified as “the most munificent.” That empowerment alone is the greatest proof of your inconceivable glory. Although I do not know how Kṛṣṇa consciousness will literally spread to every town and village, I do not have the slightest doubt about the eventual fulfillment of the Lord’s prediction. I am convinced that that will be the way Kṛṣṇa will make the entire world recognize your real greatness. I sincerely pray to Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu that I can become an insignificant instrument in executing that plan.

Your humble servant eternally,  
Bhakti Cāru Swami

## Bhaktimārga Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances.

Your Divine Grace, in 1976 you came to Toronto and resided with us in our present building for a portion of a week. We tried our best to provide a comfortable stay in the small bedroom on the top floor. We are reflecting on that space.

**Room at 243**

I sit in a room  
Of a very different kind,  
With pictures all around,  
Which soothe the vile mind.

The tones are muted grays,  
Calming and relaxed.  
There’s a coolness like the moon  
Complete and fully waxed.

In this very room  
You came to rest awhile,  
Eased your divine form  
On a bed-linen pile.

In the dead of night  
Your sleep was in divide.  
A tooth was aching,  
Causing pain to one side.

To add to all of this,  
A caravan did roar  
Outside the tiny room  
Till the one o’clock score.

The night was over.  
The birds made their song.  
Your *bhaktas* did as well.  
Nearby they did all throng.

The window facing east  
Drew in Sūrya’s ray,  
Welcomed in the dawn  
And devotions for the day.

Here you did then sport  
A *dhotī* and did bear  
*Tilak* on the brow—  
A Vaiṣṇav’s style of wear.

Your thought was on the Lord,  
And also was on us,  
Concern for the world  
With all its muss and fuss.

Adjoining to this room  
Was another full of guests,  
With Shivaraman, O’Connell,  
Waiting to get blessed.

Your day was very full,  
With outings and the talks.  
You made exits from that place  
To take to morning walks.

You sat in that room,  
It was a time in June,  
Proud to get this church,  
Like conquering the moon.

In this special space  
You left a *sādhu’s* dust,  
You left your many thoughts,  
You left us feeling trust.

I sit in this room,  
The Avenue Road lot.  
“Auspicious” is the word,  
My fortune—I know naught.

Your servant,  
Bhaktimārga Swami

## Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Most worshipable Śrīla Prabhupāda,

According to your divine instructions, preparations for the opening of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Krishnachandra Manimandir temple, the centerpiece of the University of Bhagavata Culture, ISKCON Manipur, are progressing well. The university will be a center for the cultivation of human values according to the teachings of the *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*.

Some important features of this university are: (i) the College of Sankirtan, (ii) the Varnasrama College (College of Brahmacārīs, etc.), (iii) the Ranganiketan School of Performing Arts, (iv) the Acintyabhedabheda Niketan, or Caitanya School of Vaisnava Vedanta Philosophy, (v) the College of Science and Religion, (vi) the College of Vedanta and Science, (vii) the School of Interreligious Studies, (viii) the School of Vedic Mantra Recitation, (ix) the school of Bhagavata Science and Philosophy, (x) the Chitrangada Women’s College of Vocational Training, (xi) the Babhruvahana School of Martial Arts and Vedic Administration, (xii) the School of Vaisnava Theology, (xiii) the School of Theobiology and Biomedical Ethics, (xiv) the School of Languages (Sanskrit, etc.), (xv) the School of Bhagavata Arts and Crafts (sculpture, instrument making, etc.), and (xvi) the School of Naturopathy, Yogic Science, and the Ayurvedic System of Health Care.

On this most auspicious day I humbly beg your divine blessings so that all these schools will manifest timely and fulfill our humble attempt to glorify Your Divine Grace.

Your most insignificant servant,  
Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

## Bhakti-Tīrtha Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories unto you, our eternal father! Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī writes:

*anāsaktasya viṣayān yathārham upayuñjataḥ*  
*nirbandhaḥ kṛṣṇa-sambandhe yuktaṁ vairāgyam ucyate*  
*(Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu 2.255)*

**In the** ***Bhagavad-gītā*** **you explain this: “Rūpa Gosvāmī says that as long as we are in this material world we have to act; we cannot cease acting. Therefore if actions are performed and the fruits are given to Kṛṣṇa, then that is called** ***yukta-vairāgya*****.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were the perfect example of how to use things favorable for Kṛṣṇa’s service, and while you were doing this you were so fatherly. On this auspicious day of your appearance I would like to reflect more on you as our most wonderful and merciful spiritual father.

F is for *fantastic*. It is so fantastic how you empowered so many young men and women to assist you in printing and distributing millions of your books about the Absolute Truth in many languages.

A is for your name *Abhay Charan*, meaning “one who is fearless” because of being fully surrendered to Kṛṣṇa. You fearlessly exposed the illusion of the materialists, voidists, impersonalists, scientists, and cheating religionists.

T is for *tolerant*. You were so extremely tolerant in bringing the highest philosophy down to such a common audience. We were so unqualified and naive, but you were and are determined to raise our consciousness despite our often cheating and ungrateful nature.

H is for *humility*. You are the *jagat-guru,* spiritual master of the universe. Although you were endowed with so many spiritual and material opulences, you remained so humble and detached.

E is for *every*. You were so fully in the mood of Lord Caitanya, desiring that all people in every town and every village get a chance to know that Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

R is for *retire*. At the age when people normally retire from active, strenuous, demanding occupations, you began a new career to respiritualize the world. Even during your last days on the planet, as you lay confined to your bed, you still vigorously gave out the mercy by providing material for your books. You were such a protective, concerned father who literally gave us so much guidance, good examples, and love.

Please continue to help us, dear eternal father. Please especially help your leaders to become more fatherly, to provide more competent guidance, good examples, and love. Thank you once again for the spiritual father you are. We thank you for the love you continuously extend to us.

Your son and servant,  
*with love,*  
Bhakti-Tīrtha Swami

## Bhaktivaibhava Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, who has opened my eyes, which were blinded by the darkness of ignorance, with the torchlight of knowledge.”**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa on this earth, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.”**

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**“Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.”**

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble and prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet!

You are my shelter, my only hope.  
No more reservations, I yearn to surrender.  
I fall at your feet, there is no other scope.  
My lord and master, filled with divine splendor.

Kindly hear my prayer, and show me the path.  
You have the mace, Your Divine Grace.  
Fussing and fighting, blinded by wrath,  
Lost in illusion, the human race.

Give me the strength to serve your instructions  
With full conviction, faith, and devotion.  
Everything you’ve given, no need for concoctions.  
Remembering you, my heart filled with emotions.

You are my life and soul, I belong to you.  
I lay my heart in your hands, I have nothing at all.  
Eternally indebted, I can never pay my due.  
Out of your own grace, you mercifully saved us all.

Your eternal servant,  
Dāsa A.C. Bhaktivaibhava Swami

## Bhūrijana dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa on this earth, having taken shelter at His lotus feet. Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.”**

Only the pure can deeply inspire conditioned souls with *kṛṣṇa-bhakti,* the full desire to serve Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa in Vṛndāvana, for that realm is far beyond a conditioned soul’s ability to experience. Surely, Śrīla Prabhupāda, all will agree that your success in doing so was incredible. One could convincingly argue that succeeding in preaching Kṛṣṇa’s names throughout the world was more than incredible—it was impossible. The obstacles to your success are well known: you were alone and unaided, penniless, advanced in age, and a “foreigner.” But these obstacles—and I have only listed a few of many—are in my opinion minor compared to the cultural and conceptual differences that separated your Kṛṣṇa conscious message from Western minds. After all, “East is East and West is West, and never the twain shall meet.” Deities, demigods, giant interstellar eagles, the universal structure, the role of women, Sanskrit verses, uniformity of dress and hairstyle, poverty, austere living, and strict morality. The list could forever expand.

Yet, you somehow succeeded, and that was certainly evidence of Kṛṣṇa’s empowerment. Certainly, it was also the times, for in those days many youths were seekers attracted to esoteric teachings as well as to the fragrant Indian spices in your cooking. Certainly, it was your *kīrtanas* as well as your presentation of a coherent and authoritative philosophy. It was these, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but it was also more. Śrīla Prabhupāda, it was you.

A sweetness emanated from your presence that captivated us. That sweetness offered us a hope that we too could become sweet. That sweetness caused us to offer our futures to you; it caused us to be fearless; and it even caused us to cooperate.

From where did you obtain that sweetness? Was it natural charisma? Cultivated charisma? No, it was neither. It was more. It was beyond this nature; it was supernatural.

After having traveled with you for several weeks in 1976, I, for the first time, stepped up onto an altar with fully installed Deities, Rādhā-Kālacandjī. I immediately felt a shiver of remembrance: that atmosphere, that sweetness, that subtle spirituality—brilliant and fragrant—was clearly familiar. It was the same atmosphere that accompanied you! More correctly, the rarefied sweetness of Rādhā-Kālacandjī was also continuously experienced in your presence.

That sweetness also explains to me the source of your tireless, determined desire to preach, which was more a roaring blaze than a desire. Wherever you traveled, you at all times carried that desire, as well as Śrī Vṛndāvana-dhāma, Śrī Śrī Nitāi and Gaura, and Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. You compassionately opened your heart and allowed us to experience its contents. It was They who caused your power, your preaching, and your sweetness; it was you who caused Their presence.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, although I now and then catch a hint of the fragrance of your presence, sadly, it so quickly vanishes. Offering my prostrated obeisances to you, your Lords, and your sweetness and feeling quite humbled by my life, I, although quite unqualified to do so, wish to submit to you a petition in the form of these words from Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura’s *Prema-bhakti-candrikā:*

O Lord, You are an ocean of mercy. You are the friend of the fallen souls. Please notice me. I have become like a nondevotee, swallowed by the *timiṅgila* fish of lust. O Lord, please protect me.

In every birth I repeatedly offended You. In every birth I failed to worship You sincerely. No one is as fallen as I. Nevertheless, You are the only goal of my life. O Lord of my life, please do not abandon me.

O Lord, You are known as Patita-pāvana, the deliverer of the fallen, and You are also known as Śyāmasundara. Please do not ignore my appeal. Even if I am an offender, I think that You are the real goal of life. That is the truth.

O Supreme Personality of Godhead, please do not neglect me. O master of my life, please hear my words. Even if I have committed offenses to you, I always think of You as my master. Please allow me to serve You as Your associate and follower.

Lust has destroyed my mind. I do not know what is actually beneficial for me. O Lord, You glance mercifully on all living entities, and You are like a desire tree fulfilling the desires of everyone. Please accept me as Your servant.

O Lord, You will not see anyone in these three planetary systems who is as fallen as I. O Lord, please accept the name “Narottama Pāvana, the deliverer of Narottama,” and let that name be broadcast throughout the material world. O Lord Śyāmasundara, O lifter of Govardhana Hill, O deliverer of the fallen, please accept me as Your servant.

Narottama dasa is very unhappy. O Lord, please engage him in your *saṅkīrtana* movement and make him happy. O Lord, I fear that I will not be able to overcome the many obstacles that stop me from advancing in devotional service. O Lord, at every moment I beg You to remove all those obstacles.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I offer these words to you knowing full well that you, as my Guru Mahārāja, will accept them and offer them on my behalf to Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, and Śrī Vṛndāvana-dhāma.

Your lowly servant,  
Bhūrijana dāsa

## Bīr Krishna dās Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept my obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Rāma-navamī is next week, and I am meditating on the glorious pastimes of Lord Rāma in preparation for the celebration. By your grace I am feeling great happiness in these thoughts. Whatever attraction I have for the Lord’s pastimes and devotees is due to your mercy.

If I had not been blessed by you, instead of meditating on the heroic deeds of Hanumān, the great monkey servant of Lord Rāma, I would still be acting like a mundane monkey.

*jogyatā-vicāre, kichu nāhi pāi,*  
*tomāra karuṇā—sāra*  
*karuṇā nā hoile, kāndiyā kāndiyā,*  
*prāṇa nā rākhibo āra*

**“When I examine myself, I find nothing of value. Therefore your mercy is essential to me. If you are not merciful, I shall simply weep and weep, and I shall not maintain my life.” (*****Gurudeva,*** **by Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura)**

I pray that you allow me to engage eternally in expressing my gratitude for your mercy, though I understand it is impossible to ever pay you back for all you have given me. As you state in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (4.22.47, purport):

A joker’s activities simply arouse laughter, and a person who tries to repay the spiritual master or teacher of the transcendental message of Kṛṣṇa becomes a laughingstock just like a joker because it is not possible to repay such a debt. The best friend and benefactor of all people is one who awakens humanity to its original Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

And in the 10th Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (10.80.41) Sāndīpani Muni tells Kṛṣṇa and Sudāmā: “This indeed is the duty of all true disciples: to repay the debt to their spiritual master by offering him, with pure hearts, their wealth and even their very lives.”

And in letters to your dear disciples:

Actually you are correct, it is not possible for the disciple to repay the debt to the spiritual master. Therefore the disciple remains eternally indebted to the spiritual master and continually works in such a way that the spiritual master may become pleased upon him for such sincere services rendered. \[Letter to Mantrinī—Paris, 29 July 1976\]

A Vaisnava always feels himself incapable to repay his debt to his spiritual master; therefore he works very hard in order to try and repay that debt. He knows that without the mercy of the spiritual master one cannot preach this Kṛṣṇa consciousness and therefore he always tries to act in such a way that he may please his guru. \[Letter to Catur Mūrti—Tehran, 9 August 1976\]

Let me please you by preaching. I understand that this means being determined to set a first-class example (“example is better than precept”) and repeating your teachings purely according to time, place, and circumstance. Let the following words be my focus in life:

If you feel at all indebted to me then you should preach vigorously like me. That is the proper way to repay me. Of course, no one can repay the debt to the spiritual master, but the spiritual master is very much pleased by such an attitude by the disciple. In the *Bhagavad-gita* it is said: *vyavasayatmika buddhir ekeha kuru-nandana,* “Those who are on this path are resolute in purpose, and their aim is one.” Our only business is to be fixed up in devotional service by pleasing the spiritual master. \[Letter to the residents of New Dvārakā—Bombay, 14 August 1976\]

Aspiring to be your servant,  
Bīr Krishna dāsa Goswami

## Giridhārī Swami

**Bhagavad-gītā As It Is**

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace on this auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā day!

Recently, while reading one of your purports to the Fifth Canto, I was reminded of your limitless compassion for the conditioned souls:

*vāñchā-kalpa-tarubhyaś ca kṛpā-sindhubhya eva ca*  
*patitānāṁ pāvanebhyo vaiṣṇavebhyo namo namaḥ*

**Just like a desire tree, a Vaiṣṇava can fulfill all the desires of anyone who takes shelter of his lotus feet. Prahlāda Mahārāja is a typical Vaiṣṇava. He prays not for himself but for all living entities—the gentle, the envious and the mischievous. He always thought of the welfare of mischievous persons like his father, Hiraṇyakaśipu. Prahlāda Mahārāja did not ask for anything for himself; rather, he prayed for the Lord to excuse his demoniac father. This is the attitude of a Vaiṣṇava, who always thinks of the welfare of the entire universe. \[*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **5.18.9\]**

Like Prahlāda Mahārāja, you always thought of the welfare of others and never your own. Driven by the order of your Guru Mahārāja, you traveled constantly, freely giving your association to the most fallen. Sometimes your association came in the form of *vapu* but more often in the form of *vāṇī*. That powerful association uplifted thousands, if not millions, and by some inconceivable mercy, I had the great fortune to be one of them.

I still remember that cold, snowy morning when my friend John and I visited a second-hand bookstore. As we looked around, I thought about the previous evening. We had come to the conclusion that our lives were shallow and unhappy because we had never seriously followed a spiritual path. Both of us had read about many of the great religions of the world and had even dabbled in various spiritual practices, but we were still dissatisfied. We wanted to find something new—a philosophy or a religion that would give us the spiritual experience we were looking for.

Suddenly, John’s voice broke my reverie. “I found it! I found it!” he exclaimed.

“Found what?” I asked.

“Found the *I Ching,*” he said.

I thumbed through the hexagrams describing nature and human endeavor in terms of the interaction of yin and yang. I was intrigued. Minutes later, my interest waned, and much to the dismay of my friend, I handed the book back to him.

“Not for me,” I mumbled and continued my search.

As I turned the corner, from one aisle to another, I saw a rack with a sign that said “75% Off.” I quickly looked the rack over. It had mostly worn books with faded covers. I was about to move on when suddenly one book seemed to stand out, beckoning me to look more closely. I picked the book up and studied the painting on the cover. From my cursory study of Hinduism, I recognized the “god” Kṛṣṇa, sitting in a chariot with reigns and whip in hand, driving the horses. His passenger was a warrior holding a bow with his left hand, reaching with his right hand to pull an arrow from his quiver.

The painting was exotic and struck me as beautiful. But what did it mean? My eyes fell upon the title, *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is*. Oh, I had heard of *Bhagavad-gītā,* but I had never read it. I thumbed through the pages and understood it to be a dialog between Kṛṣṇa and someone named Arjuna. The book talked about many of the same questions I wanted answers for.

“This is it!” I said, half to myself, half out loud.

“What?” said John.

I showed him the *Bhagavad-gītā*.

“Hinduism?” he said, still clutching the *I Ching*. “Not for me, thanks.”

John bought the *I Ching,* and I bought the *Bhagavad-gītā*.

We raced back to my apartment and began to read our new books. I was so absorbed that I lost all track of time and ended up reading the whole night. By the time I looked up, John had fallen asleep on the couch trying to understand the *I Ching* and was snoring like anything.

As the sun rose, ushering in a new day, the soothing rays of your *Bhagavad-gītā* were ushering in a new life for me. I took your advice and soon began chanting and following the principles. I read as many of your books as I could get my hands on. Later, I had the good fortune to meet your disciples. By their mercy, you accepted me as one of them.

Your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* transformed my life as it has transformed the lives of thousands of others. There is no way to repay you for this great gift, but you taught us that the disciple attempts to repay his spiritual master by giving the gift to others.

On this holy day, I beg three boons from you: The first is that I may realize the purport of your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is;* the second, that I may distribute your *Bhagavad-gītā* to as many people as possible during my lifetime; the third, that I may inspire and teach others to do the same.

Your humble servant,  
Giridhārī Swami

## Girirāja Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

In December of 1969 you returned to Boston for the first time after I had met you and joined the temple in April. I stayed behind to help in the kitchen, while most of the other devotees went to receive you at the airport.

In the kitchen we listened to a tape of a lecture you had given the previous April in New York:

We are worshiping *govindam ādi-puruṣaṁ,* the supreme original Personality of Godhead, who is known as Hari....*Antar bahir yadi haris tapasā tataḥ kim*. If you always see Hari within yourself and if you see Hari always outside, inside and outside...*Tad v antike*...Hari is present everywhere, so one who sees Hari, antike, near, or distant place, within, outside, he does not see anything except Hari. How it becomes possible? *Premāñjana-cchurita-bhakti-vilocanena*. When one is merged into the love of God, he does not see anything in the world except Hari. That is his vision. So...inside and outside, if you always see Hari, Kṛṣṇa, *tapasā tataḥ kim,* then what is the use of your other austerities and penances? You are on the topmost level. That is wanted. *Nāntar bahir yadi haris tapasā tataḥ kim*. And if you do not see within and outside Hari always, then what is the value of your austerities? Therefore...we chant this mantra, *govindam ādi-puruṣaṁ tam ahaṁ bhajāmi*. We have no other business. Simply we have to satisfy Govinda, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa. Then everything is complete. He is complete and His worship is complete, His devotee is complete.

My heart softened as I contemplated your heart—always melted and filled with love for Kṛṣṇa. I just knew you were always seeing Kṛṣṇa. And I was eager to see you.

Finally you arrived. You climbed the stairs to the temple room—the floor there was freshly tiled and the walls freshly painted by the hands of the devotees—and took in the sight of the Deities of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa on Their new altar.

You were pleased. You said when we polish the Deities’ paraphernalia we polish our hearts. You said the sanctified atmosphere had infused your heart. And you praised Satsvarūpa for the improvements in the center.

You took your place on the *vyāsāsana*:

I am so pleased that you are doing things just to my satisfaction. Stick to this principle and Kṛṣṇa will bless you, surely. Our line of action is not difficult: chanting sixteen rounds regularly, following the four regulative principles, taking *prasādam,* reading books. We have got so many books. Speak, discuss about the subject matter amongst yourselves. This is the process.

Then you spoke from *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*.

Later some devotees accompanied you to the private house that had been arranged for you some distance from the temple. Only devotees with specific approved business were allowed to meet you there.

Yet we were glad you were free to rest and recuperate—and write.

One day you invited the staff of BTG to come and discuss. We, of course, were eager to hear about their meeting with you, anything and everything about you, and when they returned they seemed eager enough to share your instructions, you, with us.

They said you had told them that when Mother Yaśodā stockpiled butter, Kṛṣṇa came and stole it. Somehow or other we must distribute the BTGs. Otherwise, if we keep them in stock, Kṛṣṇa will come and steal them.

Then you discussed the order in which the articles should appear. You said first any article by Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, then by your Guru Mahārāja, then by you, and then by your disciples. And then you quipped: “Actually, your articles are also written by me. They are just submitted under different names.”

Now, on the auspicious occasion of your appearance day, I pray for your mercy, that whatever books and articles I write may actually be written by you, and that you may appear within them.

Then, if you do the writing—and we do the following—everything will be all right, complete. Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Girirāja Swami

## Gopāl Krishna Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet on the auspicious occasion of your 104th Vyāsa-pūjā celebration. All glories to your lotus feet, which are and will continue to lead humanity in Kali-yuga on the path back to Godhead.

Before offering this brief homage, I must admit that I feel totally unqualified to do so. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the Vaikuṇṭha man who appeared in order to help realize Lord Caitanya’s prediction that the holy name will spread to every town and village of the world. I want to thank you a million times for giving me the opportunity to serve the Supreme Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have offered the entire world a process by which they can live peacefully and happily by becoming Kṛṣṇa conscious. You have done what no one else ever did in history—converted thousands of *mlecchas* into aspiring devotees of Śrī Kṛṣṇa. You have single-handedly launched a powerful movement that can revolutionize the entire universe.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are and will remain the pre-eminent *śikṣā-guru* for the next ten thousand years for everyone who comes in contact with ISKCON. Your glories are spreading unlimitedly as time passes. Positive results are being realized through your *vāṇī,* present in your books. All over the world people are becoming Kṛṣṇa conscious due to reading your books or coming in contact with someone speaking from your books. In your purports there are no speculative theories. Every word in your purports has been carefully conceived and has great significance. The whole of ISKCON is going on only because of you. Only by taking shelter of the instructions contained in your books, lectures, letters, and conversations can we hope to make spiritual advancement. Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī has explained in *The Nectar of Devotion* that the sum and substance of all the rules and regulations is to come to the point that we always remember Kṛṣṇa and never forget Him. You wanted us to be conscious of Kṛṣṇa twenty-four hours a day. You bring about this remembrance because you are always thinking of Kṛṣṇa. You are the touchstone that makes people Kṛṣṇa conscious.

After establishing the Kṛṣṇa conscious movement in the West, you returned to India and personally supervised the execution of major projects in Bombay, Vṛndāvana, and Māyāpur. These three projects plus the other big projects that have opened since then, along with your books, have helped to establish your institution as the leading exponent of the Vedic message. Due to your purity and hard work, ISKCON has now become one of the most respected institutions in India and in many other parts of the world. Due to your causeless mercy, the temple of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Pārtha-sārathi was formally opened by the prime minister on Rāma-navamī in 1998. Last Janmāṣṭamī, the Governor of Delhi formally inaugurated the animatronics presentation of your *Bhagavad-gītā* experience. In this presentation the audience sees you giving a lecture, lifting your hands, and moving your face. Along with this animatronics presentation is shown a video presentation about your divine activities. At one point in your lecture you state that we should not be misled by bogus incarnations of God, and at this point Kṛṣṇa begins to speak to Arjuna and ultimately reveals His universal form. This animatronics presentation is very much appreciated by all visitors, and it gives the viewers a chance to hear you speak and to appreciate your transcendental personality. In January we also inaugurated Govinda’s Restaurant, which is gaining popularity. We are now working on the final phase of the museum in the Delhi project, which we hope to complete over the next year. In Bombay, due to the efforts of a few devotees and a steering committee consisting of sincere life members, an agreement has been reached to clear Hare Krishna Land of all tenants by July of this year. Everything you say always comes true. We are about to launch a major project in Bombay, which will help realize “The Heaven on Earth” that you wanted in Juhu. During your two visits to Kurukṣetra you spoke about having a Kṛṣṇa-Arjuna temple at Jyotisar, where the Lord spoke the *Bhagavad-gītā,* and we are hoping to start the execution of this project soon.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the *patita-pāvana*, savior of the fallen. Our only real business should be to follow your instructions and work for your pleasure and Kṛṣṇa’s. We should be exemplary in our behavior and not act in a manner that may spoil the good image ISKCON has achieved due to your hard work. On this auspicious day I want to beg you to please bless me with a tiny fraction of your determination and enthusiasm to serve Lord Kṛṣṇa and the spiritual master. My only desire is to be engaged in your service, birth after birth.

Your insignificant servant,  
Gopāl Krishna Goswami

## Guru Prasād Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to your perennial vigilance, the main factor that has kept ISKCON active and dynamic in spite of so many challenges.

By your all-perfect vision you injected the seed of success into your society: the main heritage, your books, and so many pertinent instructions that we are just beginning to understand. As we pass through many experiences in our effort to further your cause, it is becoming apparent that you took everything into account and expected us to faithfully fulfill your indications and at the same time use our intelligence to expand and refine that which you left us.

*tvaṁ naḥ sandarśito dhātrā dustaraṁ nistitīrṣatām*  
*kaliṁ sattva-haraṁ puṁsāṁ karṇa-dhāra ivārṇavam*

**“We think that we have met Your Goodness by the will of providence, just so that we may accept you as captain of the ship for those who desire to cross the difficult ocean of Kali, which deteriorates all the good qualities of a human being.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.1.22)**

Kali-yuga is full of surprises, which mostly demonstrate that just to follow the basic program you gave us is a great achievement. Previously I would think to myself that it is very simple, but now, after seeing so many who have not been able to maintain, I pray for your constant guidance and safekeeping. The sages at Naimiṣāraṇya prayed for the saving grace of the spiritual master as their only protection against Māyā’s onslaught in Kali-yuga. They were self-controlled, visionary, and fixed in spiritual culture from birth—yet they still prayed for the mercy of their spiritual master. Then what to speak of myself, wandering in the midst of Kali-yuga with you as my only beacon, and relying on the help of your devotees who are resolute in following you.

*jīve sākṣāt nāhi tāte guru caittya-rūpe*  
*śikṣā-guru haya kṛṣṇa-mahānta-svarūpe*

**“Since one cannot visually experience the presence of the Supersoul, He appears before us as a liberated devotee. Such a spiritual master is none other than Kṛṣṇa Himself.” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta*****,** ***Ādi-līlā*** **1.58)**

Although I know that the Supersoul dwells in my heart and gives me direction when my own limited powers of perception fail, I can directly feel and perceive your protection. Your purely spiritual desires are all around, gradually pulling me to the shore of devotional service. Despite my constant resistance, you have not allowed me to stray away.

Many perceive that these are difficult times. Certainly from an external point of view they are. But as I stand behind you, my vision is transformed, and I see a world of opportunity. I beg you to help me take advantage of these wonderful opportunities that Kṛṣṇa is offering, and one day I hope, by taking advantage of them fearlessly, that I may be allowed to become your unwavering and trustworthy servitor.

Your fallen and aspiring servant,  
Guru Prasād Swami

## Hridayānanda dās Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Again and again I offer my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. By your blessings, we gradually advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and thus we gradually advance in our ability to appreciate your perfection as an extraordinary pure devotee of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. When we begin the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, we understand you imperfectly, indeed immaturely.

Of course, even when we were young practitioners, you blessed us so we could grasp certain basic truths about *viṣṇu-tattva, guru-tattva,* etc. But a young beginner tends to think and act with a zealous enthusiasm that protects his or her tender young creeper. As one’s faith grows stronger, one becomes more thoughtful and wise.

I have often experienced that as we mature in spiritual life we transcend our immature notions of the Guru and Kṛṣṇa. As in all stages of *bhakti-yoga,* there is danger here. One may foolishly believe that one’s own immature vision of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, was your actual nature, and thus in rejecting one’s own youthful fanaticism, one may tragically, disastrously criticize you.

I do not hesitate in declaring that you were, and are, a perfect loving servant of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. All your actions are perfect because they are all performed in perfect Kṛṣṇa consciousness. My humble prayer on this glorious day is that you give us the knowledge and strength to serve you properly, to understand you well enough to represent you as you would be represented.

In the hope of one day transcending my glaring imperfections, I remain  
Your servant,  
Hridayānanda dās Goswami

## Jayapatāka Swami

My dear spiritual father Śrīla A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrate obeisances offered again and again at your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace! I am most unqualified to honor Your Divine Grace on the auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā celebration, yet I am trying to do so, begging for your blessings.

Your Divine Grace is the ocean of mercy. You have took Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s causeless mercy around the world; otherwise that mercy would have remained hidden. Without Your Divine Grace’s mercy we would not have had the opportunity to get Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Your Divine Grace has purely presented the message of Lord Kṛṣṇa as revealed by the previous *ācāryas,* without alteration. Out of Your Divine Grace’s causeless mercy, you desire the deliverance of all conditioned souls. Your Divine Grace has unlimited good qualities, and simply by our remembering them we become awestruck and purified.

Originally your disciples casually addressed you as “Swamiji,” but you advised them that this wasn’t an appropriate way to address the spiritual master. When Your Divine Grace was asked what *was* appropriate, master you advised them that “Śrīla Prabhupāda” was a proper address, along with other names. Thus your disciples began to address you as Śrīla Prabhupāda. Later we asked if we could address you as “Ṭhākura,” as we do for Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura or Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. You replied that this was not proper during your lifetime. Now that your *aprakaṭa-līlā* (unmanifested pastimes) are going on, it seems appropriate to now address Your Divine Grace as Śrīla Bhaktivedanta Swami Ṭhākura or Śrīla Bhaktivedanta Ṭhākura. You are truly an exalted Vaiṣṇava and worthy of being honored as a Ṭhākura.

In your presence some members of the Gauḍīya Maṭh complained about us, your disciples, addressing you as Śrīla Prabhupāda, since they had been addressing Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura exclusively as Śrīla Prabhupāda. Your Divine Grace later instructed us that the name Śrīla Prabhupāda couldn’t be limited to one person. It is a general name for any exalted spiritual master who achieves great preaching results. Your Divine Grace also mentioned how in Navadvīpa so many local Gosāis are addressed as “Prabhupāda,” since it is one of the names for spiritual master.

From all these exchanges we can understand that the honorific Ṭhākura is more exalted than Śrīla Prabhupāda, and therefore you deserve this more exalted form of address. You are truly a glorious representative of the previous *ācāryas* and have manifested their will in this world. All glories to Śrīla Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda Ṭhākura!!

May all of Your Divine Grace’s spiritual grandchildren and future followers experience a close and direct relationship with you. In ISKCON we want to achieve a stage in which everyone feels a real and vital connection with Your Divine Grace through your books, your teachings, remembrance of you, and the transcendental help your representative spiritual masters and other Vaiṣṇavas are giving them. May everyone feel Your Divine Grace’s mercy coming down to him or her through their devotional service! Since you are a pure representative of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu and Vyāsadeva, in this way everyone will also feel closely connected with Lord Kṛṣṇa.

In Śrī Māyāpur Candrodaya Mandir we are preparing to break ground for your grand project of the Temple of the Vedic Planetarium. We are ever anxious for Your Divine Grace’s causeless mercy for the successful completion of this glorious project.

I have personally realized how much there is still for me to do to fulfill the many instructions you have given me. I need your special mercy to accomplish all that you desire me to do. I am very weak and inefficient, and only by Your Divine Grace’s causeless mercy can I do anything. Although I am not very qualified to do anything, you have kindly sent some devotees to help me fulfill your desires. Please have mercy upon them as well as upon me. Please have mercy on all ISKCON devotees and the ISKCON GBC so that they can be empowered to manifest your will in this world. Please forgive me for any errors or offenses I have made.

Your most insignificant servant,  
Jayapatāka Swami

## Kavicandra Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my obeisances.

We should always be absorbed in thinking of your glorious pastimes and qualities, but at this Vyāsa-pūjā time we should make a special effort.

You said that the spiritual master lives forever and the follower lives with him. I have been trying for many years to be your follower, but I will probably require many lifetimes to achieve such an exalted status. So far the only devotional activity that I am even a little bit proficient at is conchshell blowing.

We are fortunate that many of your followers are writing their memoirs so that those of us who are not able to remember you on our own are able to relish your association by hearing. And of course we have your books and lecture tapes and the opportunity to one day hear attentively.

Recently I have heard a lot of the *līlā* and instructions of your Guru Mahārāja, His Divine Grace Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. The more I hear, the more it becomes clear how you perfectly imbibed and followed his instructions, especially the instruction to preach in the West. I am still trying to pretend to be a devotee and your follower. Although a few are fooled, I know that I have not begun to even understand your orders. I hope that someday I can say, as you said, that “I simply followed the order of my spiritual master.”

When I was first on traveling book distribution, I had only one tape of yours, a tape of you chanting *kīrtana*. At one point on that tape you chanted a *śloka* that was unknown to me. You chanted with such deep emotion, with a voice like thunder, that I was always overwhelmed and would wonder, “What does that *śloka* mean?”

I don’t recall exactly when, but I finally learned about the *śloka,* which you often quoted. It was one of the prayers of the great devotee King Kulaśekhara:

*kṛṣṇa tvadīya-pada-paṅkaja-pañjarāntam*  
*adyaiva me viśatu mānasa-rāja-haṁsaḥ*  
*prāṇa-prayāṇa-samaye kapha-vāta-pittaiḥ*  
*kaṇṭhāvarodhana-vidhau smaraṇaṁ kutas te*

**“My Lord Kṛṣṇa, I pray that the swan of my mind may immediately sink down to the stems of the lotus feet of Your Lordship and be locked in their network; otherwise, at the time of my final breath, when my throat is choked up with cough, how will it be possible to think of You?”**

As old age overwhelms us, I see more and more the deep import of this *śloka*. In order to remember Kṛṣṇa at the time of death, we have to be fixed in our devotional practices. And if we do many things but forget Kṛṣṇa, then we are the losers.

Remembering Kṛṣṇa at the time of death is given importance in many places in the *śāstra*. In the *Bhagavad-gītā* (8.2) Arjuna asked Kṛṣṇa:

*adhiyajñaḥ kathaṁ ko ’tra dehe ’smin madhusūdana*  
*prayāṇa-kāle ca kathaṁ jñeyo ’si niyatātmabhiḥ*

**“Who is the Lord of sacrifice, and how does He live in the body, O Madhusūdana? And how can those engaged in devotional service know You at the time of death?”**

You kindly explain in the purport:

Now the word *prayāṇa-kāle* in this verse is very significant because whatever we do in life will be tested at the time of death. Arjuna is very anxious to know of those who are constantly engaged in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. What should be their position at that final moment?

Except for trying to serve you, I cannot report any great successes in my life. I have not done very much, and the list of failures would be too long for this occasion. I can only hope against hope that I can become an open-hearted recipient of your mercy and continue to try until the time of death. I know that if at the time of death I can still be trying to remember you and to follow your instructions, that will glorify you.

You have explained:

In India there is a common saying. They say, *bhajan koro pūjān koro murte jānle haya*. The meaning is that however you may meditate upon...You may be very great meditator, or you may be a great religionist or yogi or a very learned scholar or whatever you may be, but everything will be tested at the time of your death. \[Lecture on *Bhagavad-gītā* 4.9–11, 25 July 1966, New York City\]

*Bhajan koro sādhan koro murte jānle haya:* “Whatever you do, spiritual activities, they will be tested at the time of death.” All your chants that you are practicing, this will be tested at the time of death. If you can remember Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, *hari-kīrtana,* at the time of death, then you are successful. \[Lecture on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 6.1.27–34, 17 December 1970, Surat\]

Kṛṣṇa is always testing us, and our apparent failures may be preparing us to be determined to pass the final examination. You showed us a perfect life, right up until the last moment. You never became proud of your accomplishments, which were unprecedented. I have done nothing, but am always harassed by pride, thinking that I am the doer. Only by remembering you and your teachings will I be able to overcome such foolishness.

At the passing away of one of your Godbrothers you confirmed that a glorious life is certified by a glorious death.

I am so much grieved to learn the news of our revered god-brother, Swami Bhakti Sadhak Niskincana Maharaja, and I am so glad to learn that he passed away in full sense chanting “Krishna, Krishna.” This is a glorious death. As it is said in the *Srimad-Bhagavatam: Ante Narayana Smriti*—this is the successful termination of our material existence. We do not know what will happen to us, but there is a Bengali proverb that everything will be tested at the time of death. So it is glorious for Swami Niskincana Maharaja that he has so gloriously passed away. \[Letter to Śraman Mahārāja, 15 January 1969\]

You have shown us everything, and I pray that someday I will give up my false ego and simply learn to love Kṛṣṇa.

Your aspiring servant,  
Kavicandra Swami

## Madhusevita dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa on this earth, having taken shelter at His lotus feet. Our respectful obeisances unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Goswami. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.**

**Endurance**

Nowadays the fashion is  
to be “sensitive” to all,  
never mind what is their goal;  
you’ll get fame by their disease.

Be “realistic” to your needs,  
even they be numberless;  
then peace, love, and happiness  
will cut off hypocrisies !

Prabhupād’s own written words  
you can take this or that way;  
Anthropo-socio-divyam  
is the science of the day.

Subtle influence, Māyāvāda,  
it’s like AIDS, gives Kali joy;  
dreadful ever-changing virus,  
hard to locate and destroy.

From Kṛṣṇa’s *auśadhalāya,*  
the most powerful vaccine:  
real attachment and love for  
Prabhupād’s sweet lotus feet.

And if taken regularly  
with the diet of honesty,  
this vaccine will give you endurance  
and destroy your heart’s disease.

Leopards, as we all well know,  
never change their dear black spots,  
and belonging to that species,  
I can testify it is so.

But somehow I got some taste  
for that wonderful vaccine.  
Śrīla Prabhupād, allow me  
to stay always at your lotus feet.

*Your faltering humble servant,*  
Madhusevita dāsa

## Mālatī-devī dāsī

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

I am falling at your lotus feet with great awe and reverence, begging you to please accept these most humbly offered obeisances. All glories to you, O greatest of great souls, for you have taken the time and concern to save the life of this illusioned nobody!

All the Vedic scriptures sing of your character. May I also learn these prayers, and may they enter the core of my heart. May I serve you in truth, birth after birth. May my mouth sing only your praises and reject everything else.

It has been intimated by unscrupulous persons that perhaps you “did not give us everything; that there is still more to be learned.” O dearest lord and master of my heart and soul, how these foolish immature speculations pierce my mind! Perhaps it is not these persons’ fault that they are blinded by something other than the truth.

It was out of uninhibited love of Kṛṣṇa that the *gopīs* freely gave the dust of their own lotus feet to relieve their beloved’s headache. It was their total commitment and dedication to Him that caused them to behave lawlessly, running into the depths of the night to seek out the sound of His flute, losing all respectability and access to their families by doing so. He tricked them by His cunning, as you too tricked us into following you and giving up our faulty misapplied lives, and even our families. We just didn’t care...we loved you and you loved us. That we were able to perform miraculous adventures of sacrifice and devotion was only possible through you.

Reflecting upon this, I can see that you not only gave us the highest form of Kṛṣṇa’s mercy at all levels and at all times, but you actually caused us to act spontaneously on such platforms. We, your “dancing white elephants,” your very own collared-by-devotion “dogs,” the most fallen on the face of this Kali-yuga earth, were given your absolute mercy and benediction to carry forward the torch of Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s *saṅkīrtana* movement, the prime benediction for humanity at large.

What is it that we were “not given”? What was “left behind” for “someone else” to give (to us)? If there is a leftover thing, it’s only my own inability to rise from my fallen state. On this, your most sacred Vyāsa-pūjā day, please allow me to remain your forever grateful and loving dependent. What you have given to us, and to the entire world, cannot be described by anyone with a mortal tongue and an impure mind. In order to chant your glories, I will need millions of births, and your glories would make such births bearable.

A fallen soul begging your shelter forever,  
Mālatī-devī dāsī

## Mukunda Goswami

**The Poet**
