“Every \[devotee\] is a poet. Without being *kavi* \[poet\], one cannot become devotee. There are twenty-six qualifications of a devotee. One of them is to become *kavi*.”—Śrīla Prabhupāda morning walk, 25 March 1974, Bombay.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your feet. All glories to you!

*“We cannot make this institution or this center as a brothel!”* That’s how you ended your lecture at the Manor one Sunday morning in August, 1973. I’d been sitting erect on the temple floor, eyes closed, brows knit, affecting my best pseudo-yogi listening posture. But I was sound asleep. How I managed to stay upright remains one of the great unsolved mysteries of my life.

It had been a late night. That wake-up call was the end of your lecture. It was over! But wait a minute! I thought you were speaking on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. How did *that* sentence creep in? Devotees were bowing down, and you stood up and headed toward the temple room door. You were climbing the stairs to your rooms.

I thought back to the evening before. What had happened that night probably infuriated the neighbors against us more than anything else. There was the stage, with its 250-watt amplifiers, huge speakers, and blasting rock music in the tiny village of Letchmore Heath—Letchmore Heath, population 250, the coveted bucolic hideaway where frog-and-cricket choruses can keep you awake at night.

During our “environmentally unfriendly” promotional event, guests puffed on reefers, and some couples walked arm-in-arm, occasionally stealing into the bushes. Apparently none of this had escaped your watchful eye. From the vantage point of your second-story quarters, you had scanned the grounds, clearly observing the evening’s happenings under an English summer sun that didn’t set until 9.30 p.m.

Your startling statement the morning after, about the temple becoming a brothel, brought our miscalculation into sharp focus. We’d gone way too far along the track of *yukta-vairāgya,* or engaging matter in the service of spirit.

Warnings like this one were becoming legendary to many of your followers. You reached out in ways that no Indian Swami or “holy man” had done before. You said what your audiences should hear. You found the words, phrases, and statements that penetrated. “My Godbrothers used to call me ‘poet,’” you once told us. On one occasion, you referred to Vyāsadeva as the “perfect poet.”

On his appearance day in 1935, your beloved *gurudeva* gave premier endorsement to your poetic status. He had read your verses that begin:

Adore adore ye all  
The happy day,  
Blessed than heaven,  
Sweeter than May.

He was moved, especially by the stanza,

Absolute is sentient,  
Thou hast proved,  
Impersonal calamity  
Thou hast moved.

In fact, according to your official biography, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta saw this as proof that you knew the mind of your master. You were in ecstasy when you heard this. Your *gurudeva* showed this couplet to many guests and ordered that whatever you wrote should be published in his journal, the *Harmonist*.

During your sojourn in the Western world, you gave uncommon meaning to *gorgeous, wildfire, rot, loafer, glowing, liberation, rascal, big, superexcellent, simply wonderful, zero,* and *kick out*. When you said “damn” and “go to hell” in public—words sometimes thought unfit for holy men to utter—you gave deeper substance to these terms.

Generally you reserved *gorgeous* for the beauty of Kṛṣṇa and his intimate connections. You spoke of Kṛṣṇa’s gorgeous body, gorgeous Rukmiṇī, gorgeous females of the spiritual world, gorgeous Deities, Their gorgeous luster, gorgeous temples, gorgeous sacrifices, gorgeous altars, gorgeous worship, gorgeous garments, gorgeous Deity garlands, gorgeous Deity thrones, King Yudhiṣṭhira’s gorgeous chariot, gorgeous festivals and ceremonies, gorgeous preaching, and gorgeous propaganda for the movement.

But you also referred to “gorgeous arrangements” for sense gratification, “rotting” in the material world, and “fools and rascals” to whom we should be compassionate. You spoke of lizards who could scamper up “polished” walls, vultures who could see dead bodies from “miles away,” US freeways as smooth as “velvet,” the “pulse of the universal body,” “stray” yogis, the “emperor yoga” of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and tiny fish who sport in waves where powerful elephants can’t even keep their balance.

You cited passages from poets past—Vyāsadeva, Vidyāpati, Caṇḍidāsa, Jayadeva, Narottama, Bhaktivinoda, Viśvanātha Cakravartī, Christ, Cowper, Tennyson, Wordsworth, Shah, and Shakespeare.

Occasionally invoking Western proverbs, you incorporated the power of poets to deepen understandings and realizations of spiritual truths. When you explained the importance of association, you mentioned that “a man is known by his company,” referring to the famous saying by Euripides. In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta (Antya* 3.11), you say that one should not be unnecessarily critical of devotees, and you wrote “Caesar’s wife must be above suspicion,” from a line in Shakespeare’s *Julius Caesar*.

During your ten years of world travel, your English changed so much that “Indian English”—which included phrases like “liking it too much” and “doing his level best”—faded almost into oblivion. You possessed, however, amazing flexibility to communicate with your audience. If time, place, and circumstance dictated a reversion to earlier, ethnic modes of speech, you were ready. Example: in 1976 in Durban, when you ended your “proper English” lecture, an Indian guest asked, “Whence did we come, why are we here, and what is the true reason for our existence, and where are we going?” Expecting to elicit an immediate and obvious reply, you asked, “Wherefrom just now you are coming?” Before he could reply, and after a beat of silence, the audience roared with laughter. The questioner, probably intimidated, fell silent. You then asked anyone to answer your question. After a long, excruciating silence, you explained to the questioner—and the audience—that although he was coming from “home,” his real home was not South Africa or India but the spiritual world.

But what does becoming a poet have to do with Kṛṣṇa consciousness? In one sense, nothing, in another sense everything. To communicate effectively according to time, place, and circumstance means being “poetic.” And even though the twenty-six qualifications of a devotee are said to develop “automatically” in one who takes up devotional service (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 5.18.12), these qualities are also to be acquired separately.

You thought the list of qualifications important enough to tack up on a wall at 26 Second Avenue, “where it all began.” In 1966 you told your early followers, “As soon as you become devotee, the test is that all those twenty-six qualities will develop. You have to test yourself.” (lecture on *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, *Madhya-līlā* 20.119–121). Apparently Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja, the author of *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, thought so too. In verse 76 of the 22nd chapter of the *Madhya-līlā* he cites the Fifth-Canto verse of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* that says all the good qualities of the demigods “automatically” develop in a devotee. But only two Bengali couplets away, beginning with text 78, he enumerates each of the twenty-six characteristics, including the twenty-fifth, being a *kavi,* or “poet.”

Often your poetry took the form of common-sense similes. On one occasion an Indian asked one of the standard queries: Why did Kṛṣṇa incite Arjuna to violence, even against his own family? Doesn’t the Hindu tradition advocate *ahiṁsā,* nonviolence? This was your down-to-earth answer: “If you can question the high-court judge why he is ordering somebody to be hanged, then what will be the answer? The high-court judge orders somebody to be hanged and somebody to take degree for one *lakh* \[100,000\] of rupees. Is there injustice? It is the law. The Supreme Lord has to execute the law. So there is no mistake....In order to keep whole thing in balance, sometimes violence is required, and that is not to our whims but at the decision of the Supreme Lord.”

Your genius for repartee and your gift of language is one reason so many of your answers and words have been repeated, revered, memorialized, and even made into slogans, mottos, and mission statements. Your consummate poetry, even that of your early “Indian English,” will be an increasingly important part of the world’s literary heritage.

Ben Johnson once said that poetry “speaketh somewhat above a mortal mouth.”

Aligned with and faithful to the predecessors of your great spiritual heritage, you pushed the envelope and reached into the deeper realms of consciousness to reestablish a truth beyond mortality.

Begging to remain your servant,  
Mukunda Goswami

## Nirañjana Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Please accept my prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this auspicious day of your divine appearance in this material world, I would like to express my gratitude for all that you have done for me, for all that you are doing for me, and for all that you will continue to do for me (of this I have no doubt). I cannot possibly imagine which direction my life would have gone had you not intervened. I do not even want to imagine. A life without your association would be like a body with no breath.

*jīvañ chavo bhāgavatāṅghri-reṇuṁ*  
*na jātu martyo ‘bhilabheta yas tu*  
*śrī-viṣṇu-padyā manujas tulasyāḥ*  
*śvasañ chavo yas tu na veda gandham*

**“The person who has not at any time received the dust of the feet of the Lord’s pure devotee upon his head is certainly a dead body. And the person who has never experienced the aroma of the Tulasī leaves from the lotus feet of the Lord is also a dead body, although breathing.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **2.3.23)**

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have given me a reason to exist.

When I think of how fortunate I was to meet you and to have the opportunity to serve you, it overwhelms me—not just by comparing my life, according to what I’ve been given, to the lives of less fortunate souls who never came in contact with you or with your instructions—but mainly because there is no doubt in my mind that anything I have of any value in this life has been given to me by you. How can I adequately thank someone who has given me the most valuable gift and who continues to increase the value of that gift without limit? It is not possible for me, nor for any other living being, to reach the limit of gratitude one must offer in return for this gift.

What makes it so difficult for me, however, is that even though you are giving me everything, being the indebted beggar that I am, I have neither the qualification nor the assets to offer anything significant back to you in return. This is a dilemma for me, and oftentimes causes me great pain. This dilemma has been similarly raised by Pṛthu Mahārāja to the four Kumāras:

*yair īdṛśī bhagavato gatir ātma-vāda*  
*ekāntato nigamibhiḥ pratipāditā naḥ*  
*tuṣyantv adabhra-karuṇāḥ sva-kṛtena nityaṁ*  
*ko nāma tat pratikaroti vinoda-pātram*

**“Pṛthu Mahārāja continued: How can such persons, who have rendered unlimited service by explaining the path of self-realization in relation to the Supreme Personality of Godhead, and whose explanations are given for our enlightenment with complete conviction and Vedic evidence, be repaid except by folded palms containing water for their satisfaction? Such great personalities can be satisfied only by their own activities, which are distributed amongst human society out of their unlimited mercy.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **4.22.47)**

And you have tried to ease my pain in your purport to this verse:

Knowledge of Kṛṣṇa is such a great gift that it is impossible to repay the benefactor. Therefore Pṛthu Mahārāja requested the Kumāras to be satisfied by their own benevolent activities in delivering souls from the clutches of *māyā*. The King saw that there was no other way to satisfy them for their exalted activities. The word *vinoda-pātram* can be divided into two words, *vinā* and *uda-pātram,* or can be understood as one word, *vinoda-pātram,* which means “joker.” A joker’s activities simply arouse laughter, and a person who tries to repay the spiritual master or teacher of the transcendental message of Kṛṣṇa becomes a laughingstock just like a joker because it is not possible to repay such a debt. The best friend and benefactor of all people is one who awakens humanity to its original Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

You are so kind to this wretched soul, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that, anticipating my plight, you tried to alleviate me from this ever-increasing burden of having nothing to offer in return. This is another perfect example of the extent of your compassion upon someone as fallen as I am. The problem, however, is that when I think of this kindness, it also increases my gratitude, and thus the pain increases again and again for failing to offer anything of value to you in return. There is no relief to this pain, Śrīla Prabhupāda—except from time to time, by my very tiny and insignificant efforts to give a small sample of what you have given me to those less fortunate souls who are in need of your association. However, even then, when the pain temporarily subsides, the inexhaustible limit of the debt still remains. What am I to do? You’ve made it clear that I cannot ever pay back this debt, and I’m a laughingstock for even trying.

The only resolution I’ve been able to come to in this dilemma is that I should prepare myself to be laughed at by the whole world. If that’s what it will take to ease the pain from this debt, then the pain of being laughed at for trying will be nothing in comparison. It’s clear to me now. I can’t pay back the debt, but I *must* try. And if I am to judge by the results of my efforts to distribute what you have given me, then my conclusion is that all of my efforts to do this must be focused on giving everything I have to serving your order. Only then will I be able to have hope that I’ve begun.

It’s your order to preach, Śrīla Prabhupāda, which gives me the hope that someday I will become a fit carrier of your boundless mercy. It’s your order to strictly follow in your footsteps, Śrīla Prabhupāda, which gives me the hope that someday I may be able to make others as fortunate as you have made me. And it’s your example, Śrīla Prabhupāda, which has made an indelible impact in my heart, and which forces me to go on trying until my last breath to do something which may someday bring forth your smile of approval that I’ve become a worthy servant of Your Divine Grace.

Along with all that you’ve given me, perhaps it is this eternal debt which gives me the hope that someday I will even more fully appreciate everything else. Therefore, thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving me this debt, the most valuable of debts. It is a debt that can never be repaid but that enables one who possesses it to become the holder of the greatest wealth—your lotus feet in the core of his heart.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please forgive me, ungrateful as I am, for asking something more from you than what you have already given, but I have one more humble petition to place at your lotus feet.

Please make me an eternal servant in the dust of your lotus feet and don’t ever kick me away.

Your eternal debtor,  
Nirañjana Swami

## Paraṁ Gati Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

In the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (11.23.57) it is said:

*etāṁ sa āsthāya parātma-niṣṭhām*  
*adhyāsitāṁ pūrvatamair maharṣibhiḥ*  
*ahaṁ tariṣyāmi duranta-pāraṁ*  
*tamo mukundāṅghri-niṣevayaiva*

**“I shall cross over the insurmountable ocean of nescience by being firmly fixed in the service of the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa. This was approved by the previous** ***ācāryas,*** **who were fixed in firm devotion to the Lord, Paramātmā, the Supreme Personality of Godhead.”**

My dear master, your life was naturally the very personification of truthfulness because you were always dependent on Kṛṣṇa, who is Himself the embodiment of the Absolute Truth, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. By your personal example you showed that our only friend in this world is the Lord. In your heart you always meditated on Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa and Their mercy. In your life you never deviated from truthfulness; therefore your character was always sublime and free from material contamination.

Your mission was completely successful because of your absolute dedication to the Supreme Personality of Godhead. You taught us that our preaching of Kṛṣṇa consciousness should be in the mood of complete surrender to the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Since the Lord knows and controls everything, the result of our endeavors depends on His good will.

We cannot bring this message to the people in general and make them accept the science of *bhakti* unless we are ourselves properly acquainted with it and successfully immersed in it, and therefore free from the tendency to cheat others.

In your practical life you showed how it is crucial to be free from duplicity and the tendency to cheat others and to be completely fixed in truthfulness. Therefore your sublime character and behavior are to be glorified by all, whether in human society or the realm of the demigods.

I humble pray to you to give me a drop of determination and mercy by which I can be always fixed at your lotus feet and never deviate from your instructions.

Your humble servant,  
Paraṁ Gati Swami

## Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances! All glories to Your Divine Grace!

I wonder where you are just now. Of course, wherever that may be, you are eternally engaged in the service of your dearmost Lord Kṛṣṇa. You may be residing with Him in the spiritual realm, or gone somewhere else in the material world to preach His glories. In fact, as a pure devotee and eternal associate of the Lord you can do both simultaneously. Just as many eternal associates of Lord Kṛṣṇa appeared in order to assist Lord Caitanya in His pastimes, while simultaneously retaining their original identities, so it must be the same in your case. Your love for Kṛṣṇa is so profound that you are always firmly and consciously situated in your eternal loving relationship with Him, even while you are fully engaged in preaching in this material world. That is why you were able to penetrate so deeply and effectively into the hearts of so many illusioned, conditioned souls, who had long since forgotten their connection to Kṛṣṇa, and very expertly revive that connection, like a veritable spiritual touchstone. In fact, you are still doing this on the planet today through your ISKCON movement, with its devotees and temples all over the world, as well as through your books. So even though you have physically left this earth planet to serve Kṛṣṇa in other realms, you are still very much present here in so many ways. You live forever by your divine instructions, and your follower lives with you. You planted the seed of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in so many hearts and so many places, and we are continually witnessing how these seeds are fructifying and blossoming in the form of enthusiastic devotees and vibrant temples.

Just now I am at our ISKCON temple in Tirupati, Andhra Pradesh, South India. Early this morning we joined thousands of pilgrims at the temple on top of the Tirumala Hills and had *darśana* of Bālajī, Lord Veṅkateśvara. This reminded me that you also visited this place as the special guest of the government of Andhra Pradesh and had *darśana* of Bālajī. You were very pleased to see that hundreds of thousands of pilgrims were visiting this holy place every day. At that time, you recommended to the Endowments Minister of Andhra Pradesh that the TTD (Tirupati-Tirumala Devasthanam) should work out a program to co-operate with ISKCON to spread the message of Lord Kṛṣṇa for the benefit of all.

Some years later the TTD responded to your wish by donating a spacious piece of land at the foot of the Tirumala Hills in the town of Tirupati. Today there is a thriving temple here with beautiful Rādhā-Govinda Deities. Every day a dozen or so devotees go up to the top of the hills to distribute books at the temple of Bālajī, and they report that your books are selling like hot cakes. From the ample proceeds of this book distribution they are constructing a large temple for Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda, and the construction is already well under way. Over thirty devotees live in the *āśrama,* and there is an expanding congregation of *gṛhastha* devotees. There is also a school with one hundred children and a *gośāla* with fifteen cows. It has been more than five years since I have been there, so it is very encouraging to see the progress that has been made in the interim. Book distribution, devotee-making, and temple construction, etc.—these were the preaching programs you established, and by faithfully following your instructions, under the guidance of their senior authorities, the devotees here are making steady progress in fulfilling your vision for spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

At the temple bookstall I could see your books in many regional languages of India. Since this ISKCON temple is also listed in the Tirupati tourist brochures, many visitors come here from all over India and take home your books in their regional language, and these books will no doubt also be read by many friends and family members. In this way the preaching is expanding far and wide from this one temple. Your vision was to have hundreds and thousands of such temples throughout the world, in order to re-spiritualize the lives of the entire humanity. It is a great privilege to be able to assist you in this most important mission, and I pray that, despite my innumerable faults and foolish mistakes, you will allow me to continue to serve your ISKCON movement, even though my contribution is most paltry and insignificant.

Your lowly servant,  
Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

## Prithu dāsa Adhikārī

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories unto Your Divine Grace!

I am always in awe of your preaching spirit. It is ever enlivening me.

Incredible:

While the whole world was engaged in worshiping mundane fools and rascals—*śva-viḍ-varāhoṣṭra-kharaiḥ saṁstutaḥ puruṣaḥ paśuḥ/ na yat-karṇa-pathopeto jātu nāma gadāgrajaḥ* (“Men who are like dogs, hogs, camels, and asses praise those men who never listen to the transcendental pastimes of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the deliverer from evils”)—you came to the West to sing:

*jaya rādhā-mādhava kuñja-bihārī*  
*gopī-jana-vallabha giri-vara-dhārī*  
*jaśodā-nandana, braja-jana-rañjana,*  
*jāmuna-tīra-vana-cārī*

**“Kṛṣṇa, the lover of Rādhā, displays many amorous pastimes in the groves of Vṛndāvana. He is the lover of the cowherd maidens of Vraja, the holder of the great hill named Govardhana, the beloved son of Mother Yaśodā, and the delighter of the inhabitants of Vraja. He wanders in the forests along the banks of the river Yamunā.”**

Regardless of whether people would accept your message or not, you were determined to preach and glorify Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa.

As you said:

The people might not understand our message.  
But Kṛṣṇa will be pleased.  
And that is our mission.

They thought Jesus Christ’s mission was stopped.  
They killed him.  
But his mission was attained.

He preached three years only, but so many followers.  
He pleased Kṛṣṇa.

We must not be disappointed that no one is hearing Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

We will say it to the moon and stars and all directions.  
We will cry in the wilderness,  
because Kṛṣṇa is everywhere.

We want to have a certificate from Kṛṣṇa that “This man has done something for Me.”

Not popularity.  
If a pack of asses says you are good,  
what is that ?  
We have to please Kṛṣṇa’s senses with purified senses.

Your determination to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness has affected the hearts of us all. And no matter how difficult it has been at times, no matter how many failures we have witnessed, no matter how inadequate we have acted on various occasions, unable to carry out your orders, ultimately your glorification of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, your ISKCON, will succeed and the planet will be flooded by your message.

Please allow me to continue my service to your lotus feet.

Your servant,  
Prithu dāsa Adhikārī

## Rādhānāth Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my respectful obeisances.

Today I am reflecting upon your causeless mercy. You have roamed the world collecting the broken glass of our worthless lives. Lord Caitanya has commissioned you to miraculously transform this glass into the rarest jewel—the jewel of pure devotion unto Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Vrindābanchandra.

O most merciful Guru Mahārāja, you are repeatedly impressing upon us the intense urgency for Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You clearly see the tragic waste of human lives as the world’s population rushes into the jaws of death.

At times you humbly appeal to us with the tearful pleas of a loving mother. At another time you may expose our ignorance with the roar of a lion. You have shown us how to utilize all that Lord Kṛṣṇa has created, to awaken sleeping souls from the slumber of illusion. The world is addicted to drinking the poison of material enjoyment. You have given us the opportunity to realize, in this very life, Kṛṣṇa, the embodiment of all *rasas*.

When will we wholeheartedly respond to your urgent call? Life is passing quite rapidly. How intensely are we calling out the holy names? How faithfully are we studying your sacred books? How determined are we to preach your mission to suffering humanity? How lovingly are we serving your beloved servants?

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have entrusted us with the integrity of your divine legacy. I pray with all my heart that in a spirit of genuine humility we may be united in serving your sweet will. In pleasing you we will find shelter in the dust of your lotus feet. Let that dust be our ultimate aspiration, birth after birth.

Praying to become your faithful servant,  
Rādhānāth Swami

## Ramāi Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*kṛpā-sindhuḥ su-sampūrṇaḥ sarva-sattvopakārakaḥ*  
*nispṛhaḥ sarvataḥ siddhaḥ sarva-vidyā-viśāradaḥ*  
*sarva-saṁśaya-sañcchettā-nalaso gurur āhṛtaḥ*

**“One who is an ocean of mercy, who is fulfilled in all respects, who has all good qualities, who works for the benefit of all souls, who is free from lust, who is perfect in all respects, who is well-versed in the scriptures, who knows the science of Kṛṣṇa, who can remove all the doubts of his disciples, and who is always alert in the service of Kṛṣṇa is known as a genuine guru.” (*****Hari-bhakti-vilāsa*** **1.45, 46, quoted from the** ***Viṣṇu-smṛti Vacana*****)**

Many years ago the devotees made up a poster which read “PRABHUPADA, the master at whose feet all masters sit.” How incredibly true! At the time there may have been some other spiritual teachers around who were genuine spiritual masters, but of course most spiritual teachers were not, and mainly they were cheaters. I once read a statement by Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura in which he said that the instructions of the recent *ācāryas* are more pertinent and therefore more important than even those of the predecessor *ācāryas*. That is not to say that the predecessor *ācāryas’* instructions should be disrespected or disregarded, but for us, your disciples, and for other people of the modern age, your *śikṣā* is more relevant and, as such, more essential. Śrīla Prabhupāda, because of you, some of us are able to help spread Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement in different parts of the world.

When you first arrived in America you wrote back to someone in India, “They \[the priests\] thought that the students may not be converted into Hindu ideas, as it is quite natural for any religious sect. But they do not know that the devotional service of the Lord (Shree Krishna) is the common religion for every one, including the aborigines and the cannibals in the jungles.” In some places we are indeed preaching to the aborigines and islanders, perhaps even cannibals, and we find that they very much appreciate the devotees presenting Kṛṣṇa consciousness, especially when we chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and dance in ecstasy, and then feed them nice *prasādam*. In the same letter you wrote, “The Bhagavata cult is preached also through the art of music and dance, as it was done by Lord Caitanya.” Only by your mercy are we trying to follow in the footsteps of yourself and Lord Caitanya and all His associates.

The Christian and Muslim missionary people are backed by huge resources, and they preach all over the world with great success. Why don’t the people of India who know *bhāgavata-dharma* do the same? Nevertheless, we, as your disciples and as devotees of Lord Caitanya, pray that we all combine in a missionary spirit and preach all over the world; with or without resources, we follow your example and depend on your mercy.

On another occasion you wrote, “We are not always successful in our attempts in preaching work, but such failures are not certainly ludicrous. In the Absolute field both success and failures are glorious. Even Lord Nityānanda pretended to be a failure to convert Jagāi and Mādhāi in the first attempt.” We are trying as much as possible, Śrīla Prabhupāda. The road is not always smooth in our preaching work, and sometimes it is even bumpy in our own personal life, but we are living simply to try somehow or other to please you. If you are satisfied just a little bit, then we consider ourselves successful.

Your insignificant servant,  
Ramāi Swami

## Ravīndra Svarūpa dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my *daṇḍavats* at your lotus feet again and again. All glories to your service, which continues here on earth, increasing and expanding, even though you yourself have withdrawn from us, having entered into your eternal pastimes in Goloka Vṛndāvana.

Therefore your servants here shall have to wait to reunite with you in *vapu-sevā,* the direct attendance upon your person. Yet for those who take shelter of you by regularly hearing your words and serving your order—by *vāṇī-sevā*—you remain close by them, and indeed they become your living instruments, your senses and your limbs, by which you continue your unceasing work. So acting, they can walk through fire and not mind, because they have the joy and solace of your companionship.

Lord Caitanya gave the *brāhmaṇa* Kūrma clear direction concerning how to retain the Lord’s association even in His absence:

Instruct everyone to follow the orders of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa as they are given in the *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. In this way become a spiritual master and try to liberate everyone in this land. If you follow this instruction, your materialistic life at home will not obstruct your spiritual advancement. Indeed, if you follow these regulative principles, we will again meet here, or, rather, you will never lose My company. \[*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya-līlā* 7.128–129\]

These clear instructions from Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu tell how to preserve association with the guru by *vāṇī-sevā*. In order to insure that your disciples would know how to associate with you after your physical departure, you imparted the same directions with equal clarity in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* as part of your elucidating the allegorical story of Purañjana.

Purañjana has been reborn as the daughter of the King of Vidarbha, and she has become the queen consort of King Malayadhvaja. Now her royal husband has passed away, and Queen Vaidarbhī laments pitiably. You reveal the allegorical meaning:

Figuratively the queen is supposed to be the disciple of the king; thus when the mortal body of the spiritual master expires, his disciples should cry exactly as the queen cries when the king leaves his body. However, the disciple and spiritual master are never separated because the spiritual master always keeps company with the disciple as long as the disciple follows strictly the instructions of the spiritual master. This is called the association of *vāṇī* (words). Physical presence is called *vapuḥ*. As long as the spiritual master is physically present, the disciple should serve the physical body of the spiritual master, and when the spiritual master is no longer physically existing, the disciple should serve the instructions of the spiritual master. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.28.47, purport\]

Then a *brāhmaṇa*, described as an “old friend of King Purañjana,” appears on the scene to console the queen, who is preparing to immolate herself on her husband’s pyre. Allegorically, the *brāhmaṇa* represents the Supersoul.

When one becomes serious to follow the mission of the spiritual master, his resolution is tantamount to seeing the Supreme Personality of Godhead. As explained before, this means meeting the Supreme Personality of Godhead in the instruction of the spiritual master. This is technically called *vāṇī-sevā*. Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura states in his *Bhagavad-gītā* commentary on the verse *vyavasāyātmikā buddhir ekeha kuru-nandana* (Bg. 2.41) that one should serve the words of the spiritual master. The disciple must stick to whatever the spiritual master orders. Simply by following on that line, one sees the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

The Supreme Personality of Godhead, Paramātmā, appeared before the queen as a *brāhmaṇa*, but why didn’t He appear in His original form as Śrī Kṛṣṇa? Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura remarks that unless one is very highly elevated in loving the Supreme Personality of Godhead, one cannot see Him as He is. Nonetheless, if one sticks to the principles enunciated by the spiritual master, somehow or other he is in association with the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Since the Lord is in the heart, He can advise a sincere disciple from within. This is also confirmed in *Bhagavad-gītā* (10.10):

teṣāṁ satata-yuktānāṁ bhajatāṁ prīti-pūrvakam  
dadāmi buddhi-yogaṁ taṁ yena mām upayānti te

“To those who are constantly devoted and worship Me with love, I give the understanding by which they can come to Me.”

In conclusion, if a disciple is very serious to execute the mission of the spiritual master, he immediately associates with the Supreme Personality of Godhead by *vāṇī* or *vapuḥ*. This is the only secret of success in seeing the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Instead of being eager to see the Lord in some bush of Vṛndāvana while at the same time engaging in sense gratification, if one instead sticks to the principle of following the words of the spiritual master, he will see the Supreme Lord without difficulty. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.28.51, purport}

How, then, will we continue to have your association and your guidance after your departure? Your answer is clear and simple: by *vāṇī-sevā*. Here you emphasize the necessity of being true to your order, and you have explained repeatedly how that is the “only secret of success.” Guidance will come, we will be illuminated with knowledge, the direction to go will be lit up like a runway at night. Therefore, your instructions are our most precious legacy.

We also engage in *vāṇī-sevā* when we dive deep into the ocean of nectar you have left in the form of your books. If we worship your books with our intellect, making ourselves fit receivers according to the instructions you have given, then you speak to us and guide us with pertinent, incisive, and infinitely wise counsel. This is also your most precious legacy to posterity, but I am grieved to confess to you, it remains an unused asset, still in storage. I pray that the day will come when we will become fit to take what you have given.

We also engage in *vāṇī-sevā* whenever we bring others together with you in your *vāṇī* aspect, introducing conditioned souls to you in the form of your books, and teach them how to receive and relish your association therein. By this most precious legacy, we serve you by assisting you in your own unending *vāṇī-sevā* to your Guru Mahārāja.

By *vāṇī-sevā* we prove our love for you. You have told us that our love for you will be tested. How? Following the order of the spiritual master is the test of love. Some object that love is not subject to testing; it is a feeling that verifies itself; it is law into itself, and is not subject to any law. But you, the greatest of lovers, knew what the demands of love are. One time I heard you say—it was as if you were issuing a challenge—“Where is the proof that you love Kṛṣṇa? If you love Kṛṣṇa, you will follow his orders!” This is how you distinguished authentic love from mere sentiment, the counterfeit of love.

Love is tested by *vāṇī-sevā*:

Similarly, when you remain fully surrendered to the supreme will, either in distress or in happiness, *that* is your happiness. That is real happiness. This condition cannot be without love. In any condition, you remain surrendered. It cannot be done without love. When there is lack of love, this kind of mentality cannot develop: “In any condition I shall remain surrendered.” Just like, you are...you are...A whole society is carrying my order, not because I am superior person. There is *love*. Without love, you cannot do so. You have got some bit of love for me, therefore you carry my order. Otherwise it is not possible. And I cannot also. You are foreigners, you are Americans; I came from another country, I have no \[bank\] account. I cannot also order you: “You must do it, otherwise I will chastise you.” Because there is love. It is a connection of love. I can also become bold enough to chastise you, but you also, in whatever condition you carry my order due to the basic principle is love. And our whole philosophy is love. We are just trying to learn how to love Kṛṣṇa, that’s all. So without the basic principle of love, these things cannot be conceived. \[Discussion on Arthur Schopenhauer\]

On May 23, 1977, you also made a well-known statement about the test of love. As recounted by Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami:

“Your love for me,” said Śrīla Prabhupāda, “will be shown by how much you cooperate to keep this institution together after I am gone.” \[*Śrīla Prabhupāda-Līlāmṛta: Uniting Two Worlds*, p. 313\]

Or, as recorded by Tamāl Krishna Goswami:

Śrīla Prabhupāda emphasized, “Your love for me will be tested how after my departure you maintain this institution. We have glamour and people are feeling our weight. This should be maintained. Not like Gauḍīya Math. After Guru Mahārāja’s departure so many *ācāryas* came up.” \[*TKG’s Diary*, p. 45\]

This important order, which sets down cooperation together to maintain ISKCON after your departure as our test of love for you, goes to the essence of your *vāṇī-sevā*. For to cooperate together to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness is the very meaning of *saṅkīrtana*. You have also explained this wonderfully:

The purport of the verse is that even Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu—He is God himself, Kṛṣṇa Himself—He felt, alone, unable to do this task. He felt. So this is the position. You are cooperating; therefore I am getting the credit. Otherwise alone what could I do? *Ekākī āmāra nāhi pāya bolo*. Caitanya Mahāprabhu Himself wanted our cooperation. He is God, Kṛṣṇa. And therefore cooperation is very important thing. Nobody should think that “I have got so great ability. I can do.” No. It is simply by cooperation we can do very big thing. “United we stand; divided we fall.” This is our....So be strong in pushing on Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and Kṛṣṇa will help. He is the strongest. Still, we must be combined together. *Saṅkīrtana*. *Saṅkīrtana* means many men combined together chanting. That is *saṅkīrtana*. Otherwise *kīrtana*. *Saṅkīrtana*. *Bahubhir militvā kīrtayatīti saṅkīrtanam*. *Bahu*: *bahu* means many, many combined together. That is Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mission, combined together. \[Room Conversation with Rādhā-Dāmodara Saṅkīrtana Party, 16 March 1976, Māyāpur\]

You have given us the greatest legacy in the multifaceted form of your *vāṇī-sevā*. When I think of how this legacy goes still so much neglected and disused, by me and by others, my sadness overwhelms me. Please therefore enliven me always to consecrate myself to your *vāṇī-sevā* in all conditions and at all times, in the face of all discouragement and reversals. You yourself have shown us this way by your own example, and I know of no higher calling than to follow you.

Please bless me to remain forever your servant,  
Ravīndra Svarūpa dāsa

## Romapāda Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble and respectful obeisances. All glories to you, the divine master of my life! Śrīla Prabhupāda, within this offering on your Vyāsa-pūjā day, I want to attempt to express to you the shelter and safety I feel in abiding by your instructions, and in engaging in your service.

In this material world, the terrain we have to cross is most treacherous! Falling prey to the beck and call of illusion, promising to deliver happiness to us in a realm built of self-deception, we have come to this strange place—Devī-dhāma, the material world. For countless lifetimes, we have made this foreign land our home.

Not even for one *moment* has the promise of happiness been fulfilled! In an endless series of mindless moronic acts, we have tried to find satisfaction, only to find ourselves staring directly in the face of *māyā’s* illusion instead.

How fortunate we are that you have entered our life! In this place of darkness, you have given us light! In this place of suffering, you have given us genuine joy, through varieties of wonderful service to you and to Lord Kṛṣṇa! In this parched desertlike realm, you have given us the sweet nectar of your divine teachings! These teachings are ever-fresh spiritual nectar, revitalizing our very life and spirit. It is such solace to know that we can always turn to your divine teachings and become completely refreshed and sheltered!

Your mood was to give this nectar to others, tirelessly. Reciprocating with your mood, Kṛṣṇa sent so many sincere souls to you to get your merciful association and become purified.

Just this morning, one graying American gentleman wearing an expensive-looking suit came to the temple before work and made a beeline for your *vyāsāsana*. After paying his prostrated obeisances before you, he sat cross-legged before your *vyāsāsana,* hands folded in prayer, offering silent appreciations with such sincerity that one could feel the vibrations pouring from his heart.

Who can count the number of such souls that your presence on earth has touched and transformed? I spoke briefly with this gentleman, who had met the devotees when there was a center in Manhattan in the 70’s. He glorified the holy name with such joy in his heart, describing how much he had benefited from chanting the *mahā-mantra* over all these years since he had met your devotees.

You came to this sanity-forsaken place, New York City, to freely distribute the most precious of gifts, *kṛṣṇa-nāma,* and, through the offenseless chanting of the holy name, *kṛṣṇa-prema*.

As the recipients of these gifts, all of us, all of your followers, are your indebted servants. We pray that you always keep us in your service and mercifully empower us to become your instruments in this same mission of distributing the holy name and thereby become eligible to taste its ripened fruits.

Because this world is so treacherous, we surely need your blessings. We are fraught with our own shortcomings; were we more purified at heart and thus transparent reflectors of your divine example, we would easily overcome personal difficulties, and our preaching efforts would naturally bear fruit.

I take the dust of the feet of your followers who are fixed in their dedication to spotlessly exemplify your instructions, and who thus carry on your tradition of unalloyed devotion. We each need to become such exemplars ourselves. To accomplish this, we need to seek out such association in one another and help one another become fully matured representatives of your true gift to the world. In this way, your teachings will come to life!

Regardless of what we do, you will live forever in your *vāṇī* form; but we are meant to exemplify those teachings! Please give us the strength, through the association of your sincere followers, to succeed in this specific sacred service to you. This is my heartfelt and earnest prayer.

Your indebted servant,  
Romapāda Swami

## Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Wise one—guru—wise one from whom we revolted, refused, found it too hard either to desert or not to. The war rages on around us. You maintain within us that lamp in a windless place because you gave us your time.

As eternal guide you taught in *paramparā*. You made it clear. I’m writing this letter to you, aware that separation brings on strange changes, and time does that too, and that we may not quite recognize each other the way we used to. But there’s a live connection, and it’s personal.

I sometimes think I’m all alone, holding up my end. I dare not ask if you have forgotten me in your *samādhi*. Where are you now, Śrīla Prabhupāda? It seems a futile question because I can never feel it quite enough. You asked a better question of your Guru Mahārāja: “How can I serve you?”

\* \* \*

You were full of dance. You sent us out to preach. You said, “If they arrest you and put you in jail, go on chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa. Don’t mind.” You were pleased and we rallied. We somehow stayed in your group and your temples, although now we have splintered, our impurities telling. Wise one, dear servant of the Lord, I write this letter to be with you. It’s a lingering, a kind of prayer. We needn’t live in confusion as long as we can hear the words you spoke, which made even Vyāsadeva’s message clearer, or more appropriate to our struggle to take up Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Your servant,  
Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

## Śivarāma Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Remembrance of you and serving your order are the two means by which I associate with you. On the auspicious event of your appearance I meditate upon Your Divine Grace with the following words. Please excuse my impudence if I appear presumptuous in any way.

Your humble servant,  
Śivarāma Swami

**The Heart of My Master**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, while crossing the Atlantic in September of 1965 you suffered two heart attacks, perhaps warnings of what an arduous mission you had undertaken. I heard from your friend Bhagatji that during your second attack Śrī Kṛṣṇa came to your bedside and personally messaged your chest. That must have been a wonderful experience for Him.

When Śrī Kṛṣṇa placed His hand upon your chest, did He take the opportunity to enter your heart, as He had done with Rūpa Gosvāmī? Did He empower you at that time to spread His mission (or was that at some bygone time unknown to me)?

Within your heart, was the Lord astounded by what He saw? Did not Śrī Kṛṣṇa, who is no stranger to transcendental beauty, who is the master of all wonder, marvel at the exquisite serenity and divine splendor of that transcendental abode?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, within your heart is the Vṛndāvana of devotional qualities—enchanting, magnanimous, and grave. Everywhere are desire trees and desire creepers catering to the welfare of all beings. These trees and creepers blossom with flowers like Tolerance, Austerity, and Simplicity, which appear in unlimited colors and a variety of fragrances. The majestic groves of your heart grow from the *cintāmaṇi* soil of Kindness, which in turn is decorated with the many colorful gems of Mildness and Peacefulness.

Within the perimeter of your heart flows the Yamunā River of Spiritual Ecstasies, and within the core of your heart stands the Govardhana Hill of Resolute Determination, casting its multicolored hues everywhere. The aromatic breeze blowing from the Hills of Wisdom is warmed by the sun of Prema and then nurtured by innumerable Bhāvas. The ceiling and background of this divine abode is the clear blue sky of Humility, where birds like Respect and Pridelessness fly while singing happy songs.

The twenty-six qualities of a Vaiṣṇava are fully manifest in a myriad of enchanting forms in the realm of your heart, and they are places of pleasure pastimes for Śrī Kṛṣṇa and His eternal associates. Did Govinda marvel to see His own footprints, to hear the echo of His own laughter, and to see the remnants of His own pastimes?

When Śrī Hari came to the hundred-petalled Yoga Pīṭha of your heart, did He wonder at the golden throne beneath the towering desire tree of your Love? While happily taking His seat, did He not run His hands over its many decorative jewels, which are in fact the tears of sweetness from your inner being?

At this place you daily worship Hari with sixty-four kinds of paraphernalia and cook the most delicious foodstuffs, which you offer with your own hands. As the Lord sat upon that *prema-āsana,* was He not enchanted by this divine land? Fully absorbed in the variegated beauty, majesty, and splendor of your heart, did Govinda not lose Himself in ecstasy?

Pleased and comfortable, did Śrī Kṛṣṇa not desire to bestow His full mercy upon you, as you stood nearby, His fully surrendered servant?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, did Śrī Kṛṣṇa cry a stream of tears as He spoke to you with the sweetest possible words, reassuring you of His protection and continuing shelter? Then, blessing you to fulfill your mission, did He enter a jeweled boat and, as He departed down the Yamunā manifesting His many forms, did he bid farewell with the words “Do not fear! Come along! Everything will be all right!” before you awoke from your “dream”?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your heart is a transcendental wonder. It is the divine realm of Vṛndāvana, which is impervious to assault by *karmīs, jñānīs,* yogis, and *sādhakas*, for the Vṛndāvana of the *gopīs* is reached only through the Vṛndāvana of *seva*.

Only you have made your heart one with the order of your Guru Mahārāja. In turn he represents a line of pure servants, whose hearts are fully aligned to their guru, and ultimately the heart of Śrī Kṛṣṇa—which is Vṛndāvana. Thus, being fully dedicated to the order of the *paramparā,* you have revealed to us the “secret” of spiritual life and the secret of Vraja—unalloyed, unimpeded service.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you brought your disciples close to you and asked them to become one with your heart (*cittete koriyā aikya*) by becoming one with your order. Then you desired we make the world Vṛndāvana through the instrument of your heart, ISKCON. Only by your mercy is this possible.

On this occasion I can only plead my helplessness. Śrīla Prabhupāda, standing as I am, looking in all directions, I can only beg you, “Please bring me to your heart. Make mine one with yours. Through your service let me realize the heart of my master. Please bring the world to your heart, and make it as you desired.”

A magnanimous person never turns away one who surrenders to him. Please accept the worthless prayers of this servant of yours.

Śivarāma Swami

## Śrīdhara Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances on this glorious day of your appearance in this world. You are my everything.

I treasure what you have taught me in so many ways through your books, your personal example, and your followers. I am weak. I am wretched and I am suffering due to so many *anarthas*.

My condition has confounded me. My liver problem, which is life-threatening and theoretically beyond cure (it is called *āsādya* in Āyurveda), is giving me such a wonderful opportunity to learn how to come closer to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and also to Kṛṣṇa.

I can’t even die and finish this miserable life. I just go on struggling and waiting for your mercy.

The greatest thing that you have taught me through this experience is how dependent I am on the goodwill and mercy of the Vaiṣṇavas. Their kind words, prayers, and blessings have had beneficial physical, emotional, and psychological effects on me and my health. I cannot believe the depth of this and why I couldn’t appreciate it before.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, if you give me more time through your divine influence, I will be happy to be sick for as long as you want and go through as much pain as you see fit to give me if it means I can associate with you and your followers in the sweet way of gathering together with my God-family and chanting your glories.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given me so much. You mean so much to me. I need your love and association always. Please save me from the pain of your separation.

Your lowly servant,  
Śrīdhara Swami

## Suhotra Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet on the glorious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā celebration in the year 2000.

**Your One Glance Was Enough**

“My weakness cohabiting with temptation...”  
These words sum up much about my life.  
Yes, much even about that first meeting with you:  
When (in weakness) I thought myself too unworthy  
To share in your presence.  
When (in temptation) I thought your awareness  
Limited by your form grasped by my limited senses.

Your one glance was enough  
To put all that to flight.  
Your one glance was enough  
To lay bare my heart.  
Your one glance was enough  
To make me want to serve you  
Again and again  
Forever.

Mist, a dragon’s breath creeping the fields,  
Scatters before the dawning sun.  
Night, a shade stalking the forest path,  
Recoils before the ascending moon.  
But the terrors that leap from mist and night  
Are sheltered within a dark behind that dark  
That the sun and moon dispel.

Your one glance was enough  
To put all that to flight.  
Your one glance was enough  
To lay bare my heart.  
Your one glance was enough  
To make me want to serve you  
Again and again  
Forever.

Your thumbtip, playing across pink rosebud fingerjoints,  
Counts the arguments of opposed schools of thought.  
My curiosity trembles to know it, but that number  
(Whatever it is) is at once erased, a mere cipher,  
By a dismissive wave of your hand.  
“Kṛṣṇa is the Absolute Truth,” your voice transmits  
Directly into my consciousness.

And so it was, yesterday. Your fiery truth, a spear of the supreme sun,  
launched at my heart. Now today the earth, a quarrel with eclipsed minds.  
I pray these minds (mine included) may tomorrow shine like the supreme moon  
That re-launches the spear of your truth from the catapult of pure reflection  
Upon the sweet Lord that Your Divine Grace reveals within the heart.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, you know best that beyond all the noise,  
We just want to be loved. But we must learn to love in return.  
Wanting love, yet not knowing how to give it, is lust.

Your one glance is enough  
To lay bare our hearts.  
Your one glance is enough  
To make us want to serve you  
Again and again  
Forever.

Your humble servant,  
Suhotra Swami

## Tamāl Krishna Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Though I mentally composed prayers to you constantly during my protracted illness last year, forgive me for not writing a formal offering for your Vyāsa-pūjā book. By Kṛṣṇa’s mercy and the intercession of Your Divine Grace and all my other well-wishers, I am once again engaged in your loving service and able to write. By extending my life you have allowed me further opportunities to glorify you in tangible ways.

You often emphasized the importance of the Bhaktivedanta Institute, which you considered to be the vanguard of the preaching mission. The institute originally included members specialized in science as well as the humanities. Though I never expected to be included in such work, now I somehow find myself engaged writing a Ph.D. dissertation presenting Your Divine Grace’s theological contribution. I pray that Kṛṣṇa and Your Divine Grace will enable me to write it in a way that will satisfy both the academic and devotional communities.

Devotees have asked what I will do once I complete my research. During my recuperation last year I came across the following letter you had written me from London, dated 23rd August, 1971:

We are making a very gorgeous plan at Mayapur and if you all together can give shape to this plan, it will be unique if not in the whole world then at least in all of India....The aim is to make it an international institute for taking perfect spiritual order of life; we shall give titles like Bachelor of Divinity, Master of Divinity, Doctor of Divinity....Our mission is solid. Our philosophy is not utopian. Our men are being trained for exemplary character. So we shall have a unique position all over the world provided we stick to the principles, namely unflinching faith in the spiritual master and Kṛṣṇa, chanting not less than sixteen rounds regularly and following the regulative principles. Then our men will conquer all over the world.

I do not know if I am a fit instrument to help carry out this grand scheme of Your Divine Grace. I am approaching my present task of writing the doctoral dissertation as your humble servant with no other desire than to present Your Divine Grace to the world in a most fitting light. Once this is complete, if you wish to engage me further in this direction, then I will work with others to give shape to your vision of ISKCON at Māyāpur as “an international institute for taking perfect spiritual order of life.” Everything depends upon your mercy: alone I am worthless, but by your divine grace anything is possible.

Your humble servant,  
Tamāl Krishna Goswami

## Vaidyanātha dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories unto you!

The years after you left us have brought experiments, errors, and finally—we hope—mature conclusions. We were moved by the question “Who is guru?” and devotees came to divergent answers. Over time, however, after a process of natural selection, it became clear that you are the candidate to meet all the criteria. We have understood that you are the instructor guru, though not initiator guru, for all devotees in ISKCON. Since our disciplic succession is based on *śikṣā,* you are thus our most excellent *jagat-guru*. It would be speculative to try to understand whether you or anyone else knew this historical outcome before you physically left us in 1977, and so, looking back, the experiments seem justified. An error is justified if one does not commit it again after having learned from the experience.

Scripture advises that one should cultivate a relationship of close friendship with the spiritual master. Advancement in spiritual life is not possible without such a connection. As such, in your physical absence it is our prayer to you that you become personally accessible to all your devotees in ISKCON through your *vāṇī*. You have often emphasized that you are nondifferent from your books. In fact, all of your own disciples have established and maintained their relationship with you mainly through your books. They did this even when you were present, and they are continuing to cultivate this relationship by clinging to your instructions as they are conserved in your books, repeating them over and over, hoping to one day internalize them.

Nowadays we receive offers for spiritual advancement from other spiritual masters and disciplic lines, and divergent philosophies are making themselves available. However, will we still have your mercy when trying the “greener grass on the other side of the hill”? I don’t think so. We may not get fed and lose the shelter of your lotus feet as well. We are fully confident that just by clinging to what you have left us we will all go back home to Lord Kṛṣṇa’s eternal abode.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, where are you? Please be present before us.

Your servant,  
Vaidyanātha dāsa

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from Sannyāsīs

## Bhakti Rāghava Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*śrī-caitanya-mano-’bhīṣṭaṁ sthāpitaṁ yena bhū-tale*  
*svayaṁ rūpaḥ kadā mahyaṁ dadāti sva-padāntikam*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at the dust of your divine lotus feet. Please allow me to attempt to glorify you on this most auspicious day of your 104th Appearance Day although I am most unqualified to do so.

All I know is that I cannot imagine myself doing anything other than trying to engage in your devotional service, even though I am not very qualified nor very expert in whatever I may try to do. Although I am not very satisfied with the service I am rendering for you now, still I know and remain convinced that this path of devotional service, wherever and in whatever circumstances I may find myself, is my one and only hope in life. I would like to see things very much different from what they are; still I will not allow my mind to become discouraged or disheartened because things are not at my level of expectation. My only desire is to try to help you make things better in whatever way that may be possible.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you yourself had to struggle so very hard, and for so many years. You were so much more qualified in ways which, compared to the qualities of a fallen person like me, are not even imaginable. Your faith in the words of your Guru Mahārāja and your staunch determination to serve his mission carried you through all the obstacles that came before you. You were all alone in your struggle most of the time. Yet you kept on striving just to please your Guru Mahārāja, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, because you were fully convinced that Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* mission was in the right hands. You knew perfectly well that by pleasing and serving your Gurudeva you would ultimately attain success; in fact, your effort to please him was in itself your success.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I know I should not be attached to results. Still, I would like to see many more spirit souls take to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Moreover, I would like to see those who have taken to Kṛṣṇa consciousness become more united in their efforts to expand your divine mission. And most of all I would like to see myself become a much better instrument in helping you make all of this happen. In these last few years of serving in the West, I have been appreciating more and more the vision that you shared with us, especially near the end of your manifest pastimes in 1977, when you expressed your strong desire to visit and remain in our farm community in Gītā-Nāgarī. You wanted to teach the devotees there how to truly develop a *varṇāśrama* community. This you wanted very dearly, very strongly, and very decisively. It seems that not many of your disciples have tried to fulfill this important part of your mission, which you stated comprised 50% of it. Indeed, this part of your mission remains unfulfilled. It seems that many are not understanding the full impact a successful *varṇāśrama* community could bring to our movement and to society at large. Yet you had that clear vision, that perfect understanding, that strong realization that we must all work to establish *varṇāśrama* to save our own families, our own children, our own society of devotees. To convince society at large that *varṇāśrama* can be established, we must first show them by our own example how it can be done, how it *must* be done.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please allow me to continue sharing and nurturing this vision. Please allow this very lowly servant of yours to assist you in whatever little way he can, to help unfold this wonderful vision and thus help bring the world closer to Kṛṣṇa consciousness by establishing *varṇāśrama* in rural areas. It is so inspiring to see young devotees become enlivened at the thought of this project. I am personally convinced we will be able to attract many sincere souls to come and live with us in such rural communities. But without your kind blessings, none of this will be possible. I am therefore sincerely praying to receive such blessings, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for without your mercy a fool like me will never be able to accomplish anything substantial for your mission. Let me not be attached to results, but still let there be more and more conditioned souls taking to Kṛṣṇa consciousness, for this will glorify you and your Guru Mahārāja, who desired so intensely that people from the Western world seriously take to the mission of Lord Caitanya.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving me this opportunity to serve although I am fumbling at every step. Thank you for the wonderful gift of devotional service, which is like an ocean of unlimited nectar. As Lord Caitanya prays and instructs in His *Śikṣāṣṭaka:* “This *saṅkīrtana* movement is the prime benediction for humanity at large because it spreads the rays of the benediction moon. It is the life of all transcendental knowledge, it increases the ocean of transcendental bliss, and it enables us to fully taste the nectar for which we are all looking for.” By the spread of Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement, may your glories be known and sung more and more throughout the three worlds! All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! Please kindly keep me engaged in your devotional service life after life.

Your servant,  
Bhakti Rāghava Swami

## Bhakti-vighna-vināśa Narasiṁha Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Thank you for giving this lowly soul an opportunity to engage in Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement. I wish I was more capable of assisting in your mission, but your ability to engage me in the Lord’s service, despite my shortcomings, is only further proof of your greatness.

In distributing love of Godhead, Caitanya Mahāprabhu and His associates did not consider who was a fit candidate and who was not, nor where such distribution should or should not take place. They made no conditions. Wherever they got the opportunity, the members of the Pañca-tattva distributed love of Godhead. \[*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi-līlā* 7.23\]

In the accompanying purport you state,

There are some rascals who dare to speak against the mission of Lord Caitanya by criticizing the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement for accepting Europeans and Americans as brāhmaṇas and offering them sannyāsa. But here is an authoritative statement that in distributing love of Godhead one should not consider whether the recipients are Europeans, Americans, Hindus, Muslims, etc. The Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement should be spread wherever possible, and one should accept those who thus become Vaiṣṇavas as being greater than *brāhmaṇas,* Hindus or Indians....We strictly follow the principles set down by the Pañca-tattva.

On the basis of this principle, you brought me to Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and now it is my duty to bring others. Therefore I fall at your lotus feet and beg for your blessings. May I always stay engaged in your service.

Your most unworthy servant,  
Bhakti-vighna-vināśa Narasiṁha Swami

## Bhakti Vikāśa Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Vyāsa-pūjā is meant for glorification. However, the past year has seen previously unthinkable offenses being made to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, from within your own house, ISKCON. Without redress of these offenses, glorification is superficial and hypocritical. Therefore I am restricting this offering to reconfirmation of my position as your eternal servant who wants nothing to do with any minimizing or questioning of your self-effulgent glories.

May those who cling to your lotus feet ever be rewarded with the nectar of your service. And may those who dare blaspheme you also get their just rewards.

Your eternal servant,  
Bhakti Vikāśa Swami

## Candramauli Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

O most merciful master. Savior of the entire world. Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories unto you!

This year I received a letter which included the following words:

The disciple is like a lotus. The spiritual master is like the water around the lotus. And Kṛṣṇa is like the sun. As long as the lotus is floating on the water, the sun will nourish the lotus. But if the water vanishes, that very sun will burn the lotus. Without the guru’s mercy the disciple is nowhere.

Your instructions are that water that is sustaining, nourishing, and ultimately transporting the disciple to the lotus feet of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Your instructions are the very life and essence of our relationship with Kṛṣṇa. When we hold tight to those instructions, the lotus of our devotion is receiving nourishment. But when deviation and speculation manifest, we find ourselves being burned by the fire of our own ignorance.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, in your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.8.71, you write:

We must very seriously act according to the instruction of the spiritual master. That is the way of perfection. There need be no anxiety over attaining perfection because if one follows the instruction given by the spiritual master he is sure to attain perfection. Our only concern should be how to execute the order of the spiritual master. A spiritual master is expert in giving special instructions to each of his disciples, and if the disciple executes the order of the spiritual master, that is the way of his perfection.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your general instructions are made available for everyone, and many disciples have the blessed good fortune of having received individual instructions. Others, like myself, are left to receive your individual instructions in a less direct way, or so it seems.

He reasons ill who tells that Vaiṣṇavas die,  
When thou art living still in sound!  
Vaiṣṇavas die to live, and living try  
To spread the holy name around!

(Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura)

On this most auspicious anniversary of your divine appearance in the world, my sincere hope and prayer is to be able to remember your instructions, guidance, and direction in every aspect of my devotional service. I feel confident that if I can always develop the mood of devotional service by your mercy, someday I may be truly worthy to be called your disciple.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are our only shelter.

Your unworthy disciple,  
Candramauli Swami

## Candraśekhara Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please look down upon this tiny servant. Grant me the wisdom to deal with the challenges of the day. Give me the desire to study your books and apply them in my daily life. Bestow upon me the hankering to hear my early morning *japa* with real attention. I want to help others; let me actually help them rather than simply becoming a burden to them. The service of management I am doing now is the most difficult I have ever undertaken. Keep me from ruining everything because of my false ego. I want to grow in Kṛṣṇa consciousness despite my own fears and bad habits. And I want to see others progress in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Let me make this a priority. Let me gain the vision to rearrange things to be conducive for others. Help me find fresh challenges for them. Help me appreciate and nurture them. Please give me the strength to deal with the fatigue of constant counseling. The fatigue of endless problems and obstacles. The material world is made of these. What should I expect? Let me become a source of inspiration. What can I say? I have no real talents or qualities that are of any use. Let me be a tool in your hands.

Your servant,  
Candraśekhara Swami

## Dānavīr Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda, most precious of all spiritual gems, kindly accept my eternal obeisances at your divine lotus feet.

Blessed Gurudeva, as good as Hari,  
Pure *vedānta* and *bhakti* grant thee.  
You are flower-bearing spring,  
Fragrant blossoming service bring.

For big theories of short-term breadth  
You are all-devouring death.  
*Pitāmaha,* saving where’er you go  
Kali’s orphans debased and low.

When foggy gloom shrouds the mind,  
Sunny Bhāgwat purports are so kind.  
Packs of jackals yelping drowned  
By your Jagannātha *rath* cart’s thund’ring sound.

Waterpot reflections should consider:  
Gold and silver are not all that glitter  
Claims to fame are only right  
If they follow your path of true sunlight.

Courts, sports, and more—latest crazes  
Produced by Māyā’s illusory mazes.  
Praying for insight to comprehend  
How disruptions will come to an end.

Unqualified leader...yes, I must confess:  
Typical victim of the decadent West.  
My only hope—your causeless kindness  
Force open my eyes chosen blindness.

By selling your books, miracles appear,  
Debts, fire, and disease soon flee in fear.  
The field is riper than ever before;  
Bless us to distribute more and more.

Gaurāṅga’s *sannyās* and Siddhānta’s threads  
Were only meant to turn social heads.  
Introducing all to Śrī Rūpa’s line,  
Vedic College at the appropriate time.  
It’s your plan with full guarantee,  
Superb Kṛṣṇa doct’rate degree.

Your insignificant servant,  
Dānavīr Goswami

## Devāmṛta Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

How do I best spend the last phase of my life—for your pleasure?  
What is the most my little self can do—to please you?

Without your blessings, I’ll never understand *bhāgavata-dharma*.  
Unless you glance upon me, Kṛṣṇa is unknown to me.  
Devoid of your favor, I’ll never even know myself.

The clock is ticking away my life.  
When will I abandon the futile attempt for my own satisfaction?  
Can my senses conform to your program—before the next death comes?

“Action in Kṛṣṇa consciousness”?  
Why can’t I suit myself—in the name of *bhakti*?  
This dilemma—ablaze in the heart—is directly extinguished by you.  
If only I will listen and obey—and remain steadfast.

You can immediately snuff out the forest fire of my material existence.  
Thus I hereby celebrate your Appearance Day.

Upon this desert known as Earth, you bring the rains of mercy from Kṛṣṇa.  
Your instructions, knowledge, and mercy can introduce a new way of life for  
this deviant planet.  
You never doubted this potency of *bhakti*.  
So why should I?

Attempting to be an unconditional servant,  
Devāmṛta Swami

## Dhanvantari Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble and respectful obeisances at your lotus feet.

Every year, with the coming of your Vyāsa-pūjā, I sit down at my desk with the special purpose of writing you an offering expressing some of my thoughts with regards to the infinite glories of your transcendental appearance in this mortal world.

Every day I think of you. I can only wish, though, that I could think of you spontaneously every moment of my life and, with unbroken attention, as if in a mystic trance, lose all reason and absorb myself uniquely in meditating on your glorious personality.

I am certain that this would be sufficient to at once take me back home, back to the Supreme, and engage me in the service of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

Everything about you fascinates me. However, today, on this, your Vyāsa-pūjā of the year 2000, I would like to write about something that shows an especially compassionate facet of your personality—your compassion for the ignorant, illusioned, and fallen people of the West.

Without a doubt I am one of these people; thus I here present my testimony as genuine proof of your kindness and compassion.

Back in ’73 I was a young, restless university student trying to go beyond the tight boundaries of the religion inherited from my family. In my search, I discovered *haṭha-yoga,* and, thus, the knowledge of the importance of a guru.

Unable to find a spiritual master in my home town, I convinced my parents to buy me a ticket to India, where I hoped to find a guru in Hrishikesh, in the Himalayas.

Everything was set for the trip. I was only awaiting the end of classes, while I also studied English.

I was imprisoned by the walls of illusion. Several different manifestations of Māyā covered this restless being with suffering.

You, being more subtle than air, passed through all these walls that isolated me from reality and reached me at the core of my ignorance, conquering my heart.

To this day, I’m surprised to note that as I recall it all, it’s as if I can still hear the voice of my brother announcing the presence of one of your emissaries at our doorstep, looking for me.

At first it was not a pleasure to receive that stranger, who told me he had gotten my address at my English School. I had to interrupt my attentive reading of a book with comments by Mahatma Gandhi—the *Bhagavad-gītā*.

The visitor introduced himself timidly and said he knew of my interest in yoga and my plan to travel to India. Surprised, I asked him to have a seat in my parents’ living room.

He then told me that I didn’t have to go to India to meet a guru. I doubted his words. He told me that I didn’t have the maturity to choose a guru and that, once in India, I would inevitably be fooled by fake gurus. I tried to argue. He, in turn, explained to me the fidelity of a genuine guru to *śāstra* and the *paramparā*. I told him I knew the *Bhagavad-gītā*.

He then pulled out of his bag a *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is,* the Macmillan edition. I was awed.

At that very moment it was as if the modest edition of the *Bhagavad-gītā* I was reading had assumed its original form and produced a fantastic and miraculous effect, opening my eyes to something that, in the privacy of my own home, in the bounds of my own city, I had never imagined could exist.

He showed me pictures of you, spoke about your coming to the West and your Hare Kṛṣṇa movement.
