I touched the book, appreciated its special characteristics, and was overcome with awe.

I asked him how I could get a book like that and what was the address of the movement in my city. He answered that he had just arrived in my country to introduce the message of *bhakti-yoga* and that he had no other book but that one, but that I could read it while I translated his lectures into Portuguese.

I accepted the proposal.

I wasn’t aware of what was actually happening at that moment, but today I realize that that was my first encounter with you. That was the moment you, only by your causeless compassion, decided to protect me in my spiritual search while I matured enough to really accept a spiritual master.

Thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I have kept in the deepest region of my heart a profound gratitude for your kindness in sending your servant, Dhruvānanda dāsa, to the intimacy of my home to save this fallen soul.

Your servant,  
Dhanvantari Swami

## Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**“‘I was born in the darkest ignorance, and my spiritual master opened my eyes with the torch of knowledge. I offer my respectful obeisances unto him.’ This gives the definition of the guru. Everyone is in the darkness of ignorance. Therefore everyone needs to be enlightened with transcendental knowledge. One who enlightens his disciple and saves him from rotting in the darkness of ignorance in this material world is a true guru.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **6.15.16, purport)**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I was not only born in the darkest ignorance, but I had further fallen into the deep abyss of Māyāvāda philosophy. You were the one who saved me from that. And you continue to save me today. Just as Advaita Ācārya was extremely grateful that Lord Caitanya’s affection for Him was so great that the Lord severely chastised Him for His impersonal explanation, may I forever deepen my appreciation for your mercy in the form of your chastening Bhaktivedanta purports, which smashed my nonsense Māyāvāda hallucinations.

I have learned how you expertly engaged modern-day impersonalists like Alan Watts, Edward Dimock, Allen Ginsberg, M. K. Gandhi, and others in the Lord’s *saṅkīrtana* movement (even if unbeknownst to them). In a similar way, you are engaging this worthless offender in some small service to your mission. Even if you decide to kick me away should I become utterly useless for your purpose, it is still an act of your boundless mercy upon this soul, who doesn’t at all deserve what you have already given him. You once said that even if Kṛṣṇa kicks someone away with His lotus foot, it is a great fortune.

It is said that the ungrateful person is the lowest of the low. This means that if I don’t reciprocate your rescue effort and heed your warning signal, and I again fall into that bottomless pit of spiritual envy, how low can I go? Certainly the proverbial mouse of *punar mūṣika bhava* would appear to have a much more auspicious destiny than mine.

I really don’t have to consider much more than this when I meditate upon Your Divine Grace. Let others speculate on your position of *nitya-* or *sādhana-siddha;* let them wrangle about whether your constitutional *bhāva* is *mādhurya*- or *sakhya-rasa,* or whether you are from the camp of Candrāvalī or Śrī Rādhikā. What difference will it make? Will it make me a more surrendered disciple? You are my special savior. You are the manifested representation of Lord Nityānanda come before me to quench the blazing fire of my material existence. Isn’t that enough reason to eternally glorify and follow you?

Attempting to reflect upon your unfathomed mercy, may I forever remain  
Your insignificant servant,  
Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

## Guṇagrāhi dāsa Goswami

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my respectful obeisances in the shade of your lotus feet. The following is my humble offering to Your Divine Grace on this most auspicious day of your appearance:

**The Most**

Yamarāja asked Yudhiṣṭhira,  
“What is the most amazing thing in the world?”

Yudhiṣṭhira replied,

*ahany ahani bhūtāni gacchantīha yamālayam*  
*śeṣāḥ sthāvaram icchanti kim āścaryam ataḥ param*

**“Day after day countless living entities in this world go to the kingdom of death. Still, those who remain aspire for a permanent situation here. What could be more amazing than this?”**

Well, in fact one thing could possibly be more amazing, Śrīla Prabhupāda. And that is that in spite of Māyā’s seemingly unbreakable spell of illusion, because of your divine intervention countless souls worldwide are now miraculously being freed from their bondage to impermanence and are taking shelter at Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet. That, I would say, has now become the most amazing thing.

And what could be said to be the most courageous thing?  
The most courageous thing was that on the order of your spiritual master you risked your life to journey to America to save ours.

The most merciful thing?  
You walked among the most fallen and freely distributed your intimate association.

The most valuable thing?  
All that you taught us by your precept and example.

The most astounding thing?  
The quantity of books your wrote, miles you traveled, disciples you initiated, difficulties you bore, letters you wrote, lectures you gave, *darśanas* you held, projects you began, and temples you opened.

The most noble thing?  
That your children and grandchildren properly care for each other.

The most depressing thing?  
To see your family quarrel and break apart.

The most magnanimous thing?  
Your inclusion of every soul in this world in your vision of *varṇāśrama*.

The most challenging thing?  
To succeed in establishing that vision.

The most relishable thing?  
Your preaching, saturated with wit, genius, clarity, humor, compassion, and conviction.

The most careless thing?  
To not take your words seriously and fall from your divine protection.

The most ironic thing?  
Although you have made Kṛṣṇa consciousness so easy, I make it so difficult.

The most frustrating thing?  
My failure to live up to your expectations.

The most lamentable thing?  
To leave my body knowing I have not done what I could to serve you.

The most frightening thing?  
To think of where I would be had you not given me your mercy.

The most powerful thing?  
The effect of your books on one’s heart.

The most purifying thing?  
To preach your message.

The most enlivening thing?  
To see people appreciate you and engage in your service.

The most regrettable thing?  
To see someone leave your shelter.

The most remarkable thing?  
How you loved us, tolerated us, forgave us, and never gave up on us.

The sweetest thing?  
To have been in your personal association.

The most pleasing thing?  
To have received your affectionate glance.

The most ecstatic thing?  
To watch you dance.

The most satisfying thing?  
When you are somehow pleased with our humble service.

The most fortunate thing?  
That Kṛṣṇa so mercifully sent you to us.

The saddest thing?  
That He took you away.

When we think of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we can only think in superlatives. It is impossible to describe you in any other manner. You are the most and have accomplished the most in every way. And you also know how to bring out the most in everyone. In my case, however, due to my stubbornness, that “most” has been a long time coming. Still I must shamelessly beg you to please continue to be patient with me and never allow me to stray from that most wonderful shade of your lotus feet.

Your insignificant servant,  
Guṇagrāhi dāsa Goswami

## Hanumatpresaka Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at you lotus feet.

I am most likely bereft of the sincerity or intelligence necessary to glorify you, but I pray that by your mercy and the mercy of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gokulānanda this offering will be something wonderful.

Today is the Rāma-navamī festival, and it is very nice to be writing about your merciful self on this day. Thank you for giving me this name, Hanumatpresaka dāsa, and allowing me some little position in helping to serve Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa in Their unlimited ecstatic *līlā*. Actually I was thinking of thanking you for the many wonderful gifts you have given us, but there are so many. So I decided to choose eight that seem especially important.

1\. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for protecting us from any moral falldown and sitting with us while we do our *japa*. This year again we were able to follow the four regulative principles strictly and chant at least sixteen nice rounds daily.

2\. Thank you for dragging us out of bed and taking us with you to Rādhārāṇī and Kṛṣṇa’s *maṅgala-ārati*.

3\. Thank you for showing us and engaging us in singing *kīrtana* and *bhajana* for Their Lordships.

4\. Thank you for giving us an immeasurable library of wonderful books. Every day we hear or give *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class, and the more we investigate your books the more we discover depth, novelty, and order in your preaching.

5\. Thank you for ISKCON, the association of sincere devotees, who do have their faults but who certainly are sincerely trying to serve Kṛṣṇa and become pure.

6\. Thank you for inspiring us with ideas to preach /teach Kṛṣṇa consciousness. The more we go out, the more we discover new ways to introduce Kṛṣṇa to others. The cowherd boys are always canvassing among the *gopīs* to engage them in Kṛṣṇa’s sports. Individually we remitted over a thousand dollars to the BBT this year. Even the most fallen devotee can do that by your mercy.

7\. Thank you for so much wonderful *prasādam*.

8\. Thank you for the opportunity to worship such beautiful Deities in ISKCON, such as Rādhā-Madana-bihāri in Peru, Rādhā-Gopāla in San Jose, and Nitāi-Gaurasundara, who took us on the Vraja-maṇḍala *parikrama*. The list is so long. The only way I can find my way around such spiritual opulence is to take your lotus feet and *hari-kīrtana* as my first worship.

Thank you, thank you, thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

And now, finally, let me close by asking for three benedictions:

1\. May this year be just as auspicious as last year: sixteen rounds, four principles, morning program, ISKCON, *saṅkīrtana,* etc., and...

2\. May our Anjana Suta Academy grow into a big institution by specifically...

3\. Distributing full sets of your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* daily.

Your humble servant,  
Hanumatpresaka Swami

## Indradyumna Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you!

Each year on this auspicious day, celebrating your appearance in this world, your disciples and grand-disciples gather together to offer special homage to you. For many of us, this day is one of mixed emotions. We are thankful that you appeared and blessed us with your presence, but we feel sadness that in these present lives we must be content with only memories of that brief association. And by the influence of material nature, even such memories seem to come and go! In the mood of Kṛṣṇa’s dearest we cry foul to the universal engineer who created these minds, which allow memories of your precious association to gradually fade away with time!

But from the transcendental point of view there is nothing to lament, for strict adherence to your instructions and continued service to your mission will one day grant us your personal association again. We all hanker for that day, Śrīla Prabhupāda. It is our constant meditation. After all, we saw you only briefly in the infancy of our spiritual lives. How wonderful it will be to see you again when we are in a purified state, devoid of any trace of material desires and able to render you the most confidential service.

While here, you were often proud of your disciples, sometimes presenting us to your contemporaries as proof that the holy names can purify even the lowest among men and women. I pray one day you will introduce us to the eternal residents of Goloka Vṛndāvana with the same pride and satisfaction. We will proudly tell them that it was by your grace alone that we attained the transcendental realm! At that time all of us, your followers, will happily gather at your lotus feet and recount for your pleasure our combined efforts to serve the mission of Your Divine Grace within this material world.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, by your mercy I have been able to give the greater portion of my life to your wonderful ISKCON movement. I only wish I could have given more. Unfortunately, the first years of my life were spent uselessly, bereft of your transcendental association. Please utilize whatever years remain in the same missionary activities you engaged me in during my youth. That vibrant youth is gone now, but it appears that there are benefits in old age as well! The mighty senses are weaker now, affording me the opportunity to cut material desires down to the root. Old age also brings closer the reality of death, and with it the urgency to achieve the ultimate goal of life—pure devotional service to Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. But most important, it reveals how little time is left to repay my debt to you—as my ever well-wisher and dearmost friend.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you planted many varieties of transcendental seeds in this world. Some took the forms of temples, farms, Deity worship, book distribution, *gurukulas,* and festivals. You expected us to water and cultivate those seeds so that such programs would develop into a worldwide society capable of giving shelter to the entire world. It has not proven to be an easy task. It seems what you did, as one individual, cannot be matched by an entire society of aspiring devotees. But we have faith that by continuing to follow your instructions we will imbibe the spiritual strength and purity required to see your plans through to completion. That is the least we can do as your immediate followers! If necessary, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I will be happy to come back to this world as many times as required to help your mission successfully spread to every town and village. I would be unable to come before you again, knowing that your desires have not been fulfilled.

Of course, the prospect of life again within the womb, and of birth, disease, old age, and death, are not at all appealing. But even now, in the midst of these very same realities, I am peaceful and happy serving your *saṅkīrtana* mission. My only request is that I will always have the association of Godbrothers and Godsisters who are faithful to your mission. Such association protects one from illusion, opens the doors to liberation, and leads one to the highest goal of *kṛṣṇa-prema*.

O Prabhupāda! How I long to see you once again! As my body slowly dwindles in the closing chapters of this life, I am left with only one desire—to be reunited with you again. In that way I will achieve perfection, for you live forever in Vṛndāvana—life’s ultimate goal. I have faith I will go there, because you said to me personally, “So much endeavor in this material world, but when I take you home, back to Godhead, everything will be easy and sublime.” Śrīla Prabhupāda, those words will never fade from my memory. They are engraved in my heart for all time. And when I finally achieve that most cherished goal, I will never have to experience your separation again. Today, your appearance day, will be forever, and life will be as you promised us—an eternal festival of bliss and knowledge in service to your lotus feet.

Your eternal servant,  
Indradyumna Swami

## Jayādvaita Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

\* \* \*

“My dear Lord, you are the ocean of mercy. With my arms placed upon my head, I am bowing down before you with all humility and sincerity. I am praying unto you, my Lord. Would you be pleased just to sprinkle a little of the water of your glance upon me? That will be a great satisfaction.”—*Kṛṣṇa-karṇāmṛta* (quoted in *The Nectar of Devotion*)

About a year back, an old Godbrother and I had just finished honoring *prasādam* at his place and were having some light chat when somehow our talk drifted and tangented the topic of gurus. One comment led to another, and in literally less than a minute he was in a furor. “Who do these people think they are!” (He meant the present gurus in ISKCON.) “They’re not Prabhupāda! They’re not *paramahaṁsas*! What makes them think they can be as good as Prabhupāda!”

And so on. He was hot on the topic, and nothing could cool him down. There was no suggesting to him that perhaps not all the present gurus—even, perhaps, not any of them—think themselves as good as Prabhupāda. He wouldn’t hear of it. And there was no suggesting to him that few if any of those Godbrothers imagine themselves *paramahaṁsas*. To have mentioned that would only have invited another verbal barrage. Such fine distinctions would get me nowhere. The man had gone ballistic, and that was that.

All I could do was get up and leave the room, adding quickly on the way out, “I’ll come back in a few minutes.”

Sure enough, after ten or fifteen minutes he had cooled down. So I was able to make a light comment, and at least for the time being we put the matter aside.

But later I was reflecting: How is it that this Godbrother, a serious senior devotee, usually thoughtful and balanced and smart, had so quickly left rationality behind and turned into a firecracker?

And then the thought dawned on me: This was a symptom of his love for Śrīla Prabhupāda. Arguments aside, he had been so fortunate that he had often sat with Śrīla Prabhupāda, walked with Śrīla Prabhupāda, talked with Śrīla Prabhupāda, and taken directions from Śrīla Prabhupāda face to face. Now Śrīla Prabhupāda was physically gone. And in his absence, we had so many problems.

Could it be that this Godbrother was right? Not in his arguments, certainly. They made little sense. But in his dedication, his loyalty, his love...yes.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the world has gone mad, and so have we.

When Kṛṣṇa was absent from Vṛndāvana, all the cowherd boys became bewildered, and having given up all kinds of activities, they appeared mad and forgot all their regular business. They were sometimes lying down on the ground, sometimes rolling in the dust, sometimes laughing and sometimes running very swiftly. All of these symptoms gave them the appearance of madmen. \[NOD\]

And so it is.

Out of the sorrow of separation all these boys forgot to take care of their cowherding and tried to forget all the melodious songs they used to sing in the pasturing ground. At last they had no desire to live anymore, being separated from Kṛṣṇa....

Being so greatly disappointed, they were aimlessly wandering on the banks of the Yamunā. \[NOD\]

Even in our madness, we’re not as mad as the cowherd boys. (Oh that we could be!) Still, we’re mad enough nonetheless.

But we can’t afford to live like madmen. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have a mission, and we have service to do. We can’t afford to float about in aimlessness. Nor be overwhelmed by anger. Nor act foolishly or pretentiously, intoxicated by pride or illusion. Taking your instructions on our heads, and in our vision, and in our hearts, we need to serve you in your service to your spiritual master, and to Kṛṣṇa, and to the world, with steady and sober minds, enlivened by your words and your example. And in this way, in your absence, live and serve in your eternal presence.

Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Prostrate at your lotus feet, always in need of your mercy, the servant of your servants,  
Jayādvaita Swami

## Keśava Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

I offer my most humble obeisances unto the lotus feet of you—His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda—on this auspicious day. I pray for the devotees’ mercy that I may be able to describe an insignificant drop of your transcendental glories.

The message of Lord Caitanya is reaching every town and village of every country. By your causeless mercy many living beings are engaged in devotional service. And your fame is spread all over the three worlds. But you never claimed any credit for yourself; you always transferred all the honor to your spiritual master.

My Guru Maharaja wanted me to come here and to pick up as many of you as is possible. And I am trying my best. By Grace of Krishna, you have joined me to help in this Krishna Consciousness propaganda and Krishna will surely be pleased upon you very much. Please try to continue this activity of cooperation and we are sure to come out successful. \[Letter to Jaya Gopāla, 11 January 1970\]

We believed in the words of our spiritual master and started in a humble way—in a helpless way—but due to the spiritual force of the order of the supreme authority, this movement has become successful. (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, *Ādi-līlā* 7.95–96, purport)

*yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo*  
*yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ’pi*

**“By the mercy of the spiritual master, one is benedicted with the mercy of Kṛṣṇa. Without the grace of the spiritual master, no one can make any spiritual advancement.”**

I am begging that you continue showering me with your unlimited mercy and compassion, so that one day a I may develop a little true taste for serving Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

Your fallen servant,  
Keśava Swami

## Lokanāth Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet on this auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā 2000.

Here in India we are getting a lot of very encouraging preaching results, and in that regard your preaching mission is being accomplished. The book distribution is ever increasing. I had the honor of handing out first prize to the leading *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* set distributor, a devotee from Māyāpur. You always encouraged standing orders and set distribution, and ISKCON Māyāpur and ISKCON Bombay have been the world champions for so many years. Even our Padayātrā India is at the top in the small-temple category of book distribution.

Having used the first Padayātrā chariot for almost sixteen years, we recently offered a new chariot to the Padayātrā Deities, Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Gaurasundara. During my lecture on the occasion, I shared my realization of how every day, on average, a hundred *Bhagavad-gītās* are distributed by *padayātrīs* in remote villages of India. The people of these villages buy a *Bhagavad-gītā* only once in their lifetime, and because to them it is a good sum of money they don’t purchase another edition. If someone proposes to sell them another one they say, “I already have a *Bhagavad-gītā*.” So this means your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* is the only one in their possession and will be in their homes and hearts for generations. *Padayātrīs* have been going, on average, to a thousand villages per year, visiting with the residents and distributing books. You can imagine the results in due course of time as the *padayātrīs* go on planting thousands and thousands of *Gītās*.

A short time ago we celebrated the 25th anniversary of the opening of the Krishna-Balarāma Temple, and on the occasion we read the following statement you made on April 20th, 1975:

The movement is undoubtedly increasing in foreign countries, why not in our country? The government, the leaders, and the guardians, they should deeply contemplate on the serious nature of this movement and help us. Now we are bringing men from the foreign countries to preach. How long I shall bring? If we want to spread this movement all over India very seriously, we invite all educated intellectual young men to come forward.

So that is happening now. Do you remember how they used to call our temple as Angreji Mandir (English temple)? The sole reason was because during those early years when you were establishing the Krishna-Balarama temple, you had only the assistance of Western devotees. Seeing that the majority of devotees running the temple were Westerners, the locals and visitors naturally thought of it as an English temple. But now things have reversed. Now 90% of the residents of the Krishna-Balarama Temple are Indians, and our temple in Vṛndāvana is becoming known as the ISKCON temple or Krishna-Balarāma Temple.

On the occasion of Ratha-yātrā in Kurukṣetra, I attended the Kurukṣetra temple’s big *pandal,* at which thousands were in the audience. Sākṣī Gopāla Prabhu, the temple president, informed me, “Mahārāja, more than half these people are chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa on beads.” I was highly pleased to hear that. Only five or ten years ago hardly anyone knew the *mahā-mantra,* but now there are thousands who are taking vows to chant a fixed number of rounds.

In Juhu, Bombay, there are many temple projects coming up. The Bombay team, headed by Bhīma and Devakīnandana Prabhus, seem to be moving forward with plans to build another huge temple complex in the back of the existing temple. I was thinking it is like your formula of “Double it!”—they are applying it to temple construction. The Delhi, Bangalore, Baroda, and Ahmedabad temples are all now fully operational, and several more temples are on the way, in particular the Māyāpur Temple of the Vedic Planetarium.

There seems to be progress on various fronts, such as book distribution, devotee-making, congregational development, and temple construction. These are the wonderful offerings made to you on this day of your appearance, the day after Kṛṣṇa’s appearance.

On another front, there are also a lot of things that are a cause for a lot of concern and anxiety. Arguments and quarrels, hypocrisy, politics, fratricidal war, loss of faith, *anarthas* and ill motives, suicide attempts, court cases, fault-finding and gossiping, confusion and bewilderment. It sounds like *kaler doṣa-nidhe rājann:* “This age of Kali is full of faults.” In 1977 in Bombay you gave a *pandal* lecture series titled “Modern-Day Civilization Is a Total Failure; the Only Solution Is Kṛṣṇa Consciousness.” We always thought the faults of this age were present only in “*karmī*” society. But currently these faults seem to have infiltrated the lives, thoughts, and actions of our own devotees. We seem to be ineffective and powerless to fight against these anti–Kṛṣṇa conscious forces and personalities.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am feeling helpless on the occasion of your appearance day, and I am rushing to your feet and begging for your mercy, your intervention, for my empowerment so that we can flush out anything and everything that is checking the progress of your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. Prabhupāda, you would never tolerate these disturbances if you were physically present. You would have fought tooth and nail. You would have not left any stone unturned. You would have spent sleepless nights to find the solutions. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are here. Please continue to direct and inspire us so that we can preserve your great movement and propagate Kṛṣṇa consciousness, the gift of the holy name. You are an empowered representative of Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the *sena-pati bhakta*. Please, O commander in chief of the *saṅkīrtana* army, is the most compassionate Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu not compassionate anymore? Of course, I can’t doubt His magnanimity, but seeing the circumstances around us, faithless persons like myself begin to wonder, Why is Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu not doing anything to crush all the nonsense that is going on in the midst of this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement? If the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement does not do *yāre dekha, tāre kaha ‘kṛṣṇa’-upadeśa,* purely and forcefully, then who else will?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you always said, “The Hare Kṛṣṇa movement is good for everyone, and it will benefit the whole world.” There is no alternative to this movement. Please, please protect and purify us, your movement. This is our desperate plea. With a straw between my teeth I beg you. Please give us intelligence and purity of purpose so we can give a befitting fight to the enemies of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. The worst enemy is the enemy that is hidden within us, hidden within the members of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. You said we couldn’t be defeated by any outside forces. You said the most dangerous thing is the internal fight. This would ruin us. Śrīla Prabhupāda, I can’t tolerate to see this happening in front of my eyes. I can’t stand it, but what can I do without your mercy? It is all that we are made of.

Begging for your mercy, I remain  
Your unworthy servant,  
Lokanāth Swami

## Nityodita Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. By your kindness, you are making the unqualified qualified.

As the clock struck twelve with the arrival of the new millennium, the *mahā-mantra* was circling the globe as we followers of yours were all holding midnight *kīrtana*! You are the one who showed us how to conduct ourselves during any event in this world by chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, be it marriages, New Year’s celebrations, birthdays, beach outings, peace vigils, world wars—even the death of a near and dear one. Your faith in the holy name is what has made you the most successful preacher in the history of the world!

Oh, to get a drop of your faith!

There is an innate desire in every living being to be part of a successful enterprise. Nobody likes to fail. That you have given us the most sure-fire way to ultimate success is undoubtedly one of your greatest gifts to us. Your method is not subject to the whims of the economy, the fragile nature of our health, the rise and fall of organizations, or even the annihilation of this planet!

Oh, to get a drop of your conviction!

You have asked about whether nuclear devastation on this planet would affect the Sankirtana Movement. No, there is nothing that can stop the Sankirtana Movement because it is the will of God Himself, Lord Caitanya, that His Holy Name be heard in every town and village. Neither can the demons devastate this planet independent of the will of Krsna. Nothing happens without His sanction.... For our parts we should just be determined to carry out our mission against all opposition, demons, nuclear war, whatever. \[Letter to Makhanlāl dāsa, 22 June 1973\]

Oh, to get a drop of your determination!

Your preaching always balances the stark reality of the material world with the vibrant reality of the spiritual world. As all spiritual masters in the Madhva-Gauḍīya-sampradāya enrich their disciples with the highest devotional perfection of feeling separation from the Lord, so you have successfully revived such spiritual desires in the hearts of countless lost souls, causing us to reach out for perfection!

Oh, to get a drop of your potency!

The *gopīs* of Vṛndāvana were so attached to Kṛṣṇa that they were not satisfied simply with the *rāsa* dance at night. They wanted to associate with Him and enjoy His company during the daytime also. When Kṛṣṇa went to the forest with His cowherd boyfriends and cows, the *gopīs* did not physically take part, but their hearts went with Him. And because their hearts went, they were able to enjoy His company through strong feelings of separation. To acquire this strong feeling of separation is the teaching of Lord Caitanya and His direct disciplic succession of Gosvāmīs. When we are not in physical contact with Kṛṣṇa, we can associate with Him like the *gopīs,* through feelings of separation....Devotional service to Kṛṣṇa in feelings of separation elevates the devotee to the highest perfectional level, to the level of the *gopīs.* \[*Kṛṣṇa* book, Chapter 35\]

Oh! To even come close to your perfectional state!

Your words are full of the power of the Lord Himself. Why can’t I hear you? You have given us everything because you have given us Kṛṣṇa. Why do I want something else? There is no future or security in this world. “This material world is a very precarious place; therefore we should always chant Hare Krishna and seek Krishna’s protection.” (Letter to Makhanlāl dāsa, 22 June 1973)

As you so poetically wrote in a homage to your Guru Mahārāja, there is a “struggle for existence, a human race, the only hope, His Divine Grace.” You have received all your knowledge from His Divine Grace Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, and as his humble servant you gave that knowledge to us. Now it is up to us to humbly serve you by giving the same knowledge to others. This is called the *paramparā* system.

It is not that you have become a student and you’ll remain student. No. One day you shall become also guru and make more students, more students, more. That is Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mission, not that perpetually...Yes, one should remain perpetually a student, but he has to act as guru....It is not that because I am acting as guru, I am no longer student. No, I am still student. Caitanya Mahāprabhu taught us this instruction that we shall always remain a foolish student before our Guru Mahārāja....That is the qualification....We should be always prepared to be controlled by the guru. \[Lecture on the Disappearance Day of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, 10 December 1976\]

Oh! To approach your humility!

Today, I am falling at your feet with a thawing heart, thanking you for another year of exciting devotional service. Please protect me so that I can serve you until my last breath!

Your struggling servant,  
Nityodita Swami

## Prahlādānanda Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

At the end of his letters Śrīla Prabhupāda often wrote, “I hope this meets you in good health.” Some people may be surprised that a transcendental personality such as Śrīla Prabhupāda would be concerned with something that might seem as mundane as a person’s health. Śrīla Prabhupāda, however, gave Kṛṣṇa conscious reasons why he was concerned about our health.

*Although the body is material, since it is being used in Lord Kṛṣṇa’s service it now belongs to Lord Kṛṣṇa and should be taken care of nicely:*

We should always remember that our body is not for sense gratification; it is for Krishna’s service only. And to render very good sound service to Krishna we should not neglect the upkeep of the body. We learn from an instance of Sanatana Goswami. He was sometimes very much sick on account of eczema, and he was therefore sometimes bleeding. But whenever Lord Caitanya met Sanatana Goswami, He used to embrace him in spite of Sanatana’s request for Him not to touch him. Because of this. Sanatana Goswami later on decided to commit suicide so Lord Caitanya would not embrace him in his bloody condition. This plan was understood by Lord Caitanya, and He called Sanatana Goswami and said to him, “You have decided to end this body, but don’t you know that this body belongs to Krishna? You have already dedicated your body to Krishna so how can you decide to end it?” So you must not neglect the upkeep of your body. This is the lesson we get from Lord Caitanya and Sanatana Goswami. Try to take care of your health in the best possible way. \[Letter to Rāyarāma dāsa, 9 February 1969\]

*Service to the body and mind may also be service to Lord Kṛṣṇa:*

Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura warns us in this connection that we should not mistakenly think that the idea of giving up everything implies the renunciation of duties necessary in relation to the body and mind. Even such duties are not sense gratification if they are undertaken in a spirit of service to Kṛṣṇa. \[*Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, *Ādi-līlā* 4.170, purport)

*By maintaining one’s health nicely one can do nice service for Lord Kṛṣṇa:*

Keep your health in good condition and work very hard for Krishna. That is our motto of life. \[Letter to Rāyarāma dāsa, 6 March 1969}

*Śrīla Prabhupāda understood that ill health could hinder one’s devotional service to Lord Kṛṣṇa:*

If you are willing to offer your medical services to my students, when it is required, that will be very nice. It is important to keep the body fit and healthy so that we will not meet the obstacle of ill health while serving Krishna. Ill health may hinder one’s service, so, we want to avoid it as much as possible. \[Letter to Dr. W.D. Currier, 19 June 1975\]

*Śrīla Prabhupāda wanted his disciples to live a long time to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness:*

Be careful about your health first. This information is not only for you but all my noble sons. I am an old man. I may live or die it does not matter. But you must live for long time to push on this Krishna Consciousness movement. \[Letter to Rāyarāma dāsa, 21 December 1967\]

*To maintain one’s health doesn’t have to be difficult:*

Regarding your question about maintaining your body nicely, I think that if you follow our regulations of diet, sufficient sleeping, and keep to the prescribed rules of cleanliness, two baths per day,\* then you will be able to keep yourself in proper health. Of course, disease will always be there at some time while there is this material body, but this we must tolerate and not be very much agitated by. Actually, the Vaisnava who knows that he is not this body, he does not therefore neglect his body, but he takes very nice care so that he may utilize his body in the service of Krishna. Just like a man may know that he is not his car, so he does not therefore neglect his car, but he will take care of it so it will be able to render service to him. So we must take sufficient care to provide our bodies with its demands, but when disease or other necessary inconveniences arrive, we do not become disturbed because such troubles are simply temporary manifestations. \[Letter to Balabhadra dāsa, 12 May 1969\]  
\*at least one

Regarding your fasting, if you are sick, then fasting is the best medicine. For disease and unwanted guests, if you do not give them food, they will go away. \[Letter to Revatīnandana Swami, 16 January 1975\]

*The ultimate protection comes from Lord Kṛṣṇa:*

Regarding Syama Dasi’s health, it is to be understood that so long we have got this material body there must be some trouble. Actually, medicine is not the remedial measures for our bodily troubles unless we are helped by Krishna. Therefore, whenever there is bodily trouble we may adopt the prescribed methods of medical science and depend upon Krishna for His Mercy. The best remedy, not only for Syama Dasi but for everyone, is to consult some approved physician. But ultimately we have to depend on the Mercy of Krishna, so we should chant regularly, pray to Krishna to give us a chance to serve Him, and, if required, we may adopt the approved method of treatment. \[Letter to Kīrtanānanda Swami, 14 February 1969\]

*Although Śrīla Prabhupāda wanted the devotees to be healthy, he also wanted them to tolerate the inevitable miseries of material life:*

So long we have got this material body, the miseries will be coming and going, simply we have to tolerate them and try to make advancement in Krishna Consciousness as best we can. \[Letter to Pradyumna dāsa, 23 March 1968\]

*The order of importance:*

In some of the Vedic literatures, it is said that Atmanan Sarvato Rakshet, that means one should give first protection to the soul. Then he should take care of his particular type of faith, then he should take care of the material things, namely this body, and anything in relation with this body, or wealth. \[Letter to Toṣaṇa Kṛṣṇa dāsa, 7 October 1968\]

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s eternal servant,  
Prahlādānanda Swami

## Rasānanda Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Your opulent form is as radiant as millions of suns. Indeed, your lustrous body is the fountainhead of light in this dark physical existence. We, the inhabitants of darkness, have not taken advantage of your radiant form. We live in our dreadful nightmare, egoistically extracting meanings from your words, trying to make our words your words. Thus we remain in darkness. Pitiful is my existence, for I do not blindly follow your teachings; rather I extract obscure meanings here and there, when your words are clear as crystal. Just as the monists cloud the meaning of the *siddhānta* with their word trickery, and just as clouds sometimes cover the beauty and radiance of the sun, so nowadays we fools cover and even doubt your empowered words with our word jugglery. Due to my blindness, I cannot see that you are the messiah, entitled to submerge us into the unfathomable ocean of service to the lotus feet or Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara. Today, on this most auspicious day of your appearance on this world, I fervently pray that I may take advantage of your radiant presence and extinguish once and for all the darkness preventing me from understanding the words that emanate from your lotus mouth.

I remain your servant,  
Rasānanda Swami

## Subhāga Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet with love and devotion.

You are a pure devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa, and His dearest one also. You saved countless fallen living entities from the burning fire of material existence by incessantly showering profuse nectarean mercy from the ocean of devotion to the Lord.

You are the embodiment of Kṛṣṇa’s mercy; therefore, though I am not qualified, still you engaged me in your service. Thus you made me one of the most fortunate souls. I don’t have any education, intelligence, or renunciation, what to speak of *bhakti,* but I have an abundance of arrogance, false ego, enviousness, and the propensity to criticize others—I am full of unwanted qualities. Following your instructions is my only means to becoming free from all these unwanted characteristics.

You said that your only qualification was that you had cent percent conviction in the words of your Guru Mahārāja and that you conveyed them without change. In the *Bhagavad-gītā* Kṛṣṇa says that to be fixed in devotion one must have *vyavasāyātmikā buddhiḥ,* and Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura explains that this means “one should accept the instruction of the bona fide spiritual master as one’s mission in life.”

Your life was dedicated to the service to your Guru Mahārāja in all respects, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You offered yourself completely to Lord Kṛṣṇa.

To achieve perfection in devotional service is a gradual process. Nearly thirty years have passed since you gave me shelter at your lotus feet. My contemporaries have done so much wonderful service for you and Lord Kṛṣṇa. I wonder when I will be able to do some service for you.

From time to time our Society faces seemingly insurmountable problems and calamities. To overcome them we must study your instructions carefully. You gave everything in your books, so we should consult them. Here is the test for us: If we genuinely love you and Kṛṣṇa and Caitanya Mahāprabhu, then we must sacrifice our personal interests and try to make you and Them happy at all costs. You have made it clear that the way to do this is to try to bring everyone to the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa and engage them in His service, beginning with the chanting of Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare. We must also refrain from offending devotees and glorify their good qualities. Then we will live in the wonderful family of Kṛṣṇa, where there is no envy or fear because everything is based on sacrificing our personal interests and loving you and Kṛṣṇa.

Your fool and fallen servant,  
Subhāga Swami

## Trivikrama Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda!

You came to me—by God—when there was little hope—the future up in smoke—what would I do—without you?

Freely you gave to one and all—association, instructions, example and more—what would we do—without you?

Now youth is gone—a dreamlike past—but hope remains—it lasts and lasts—what would I do—without you?

The struggle persists—habits die hard—“Mercy! Mercy!” I cry from the core of my heart—what would I do—without you?

A chance to repay—in token form—your “matchless gift”—is what I pray—what should I do—for you?

But how to repay—it can’t be done—eternally in debt—is what I’ve become—what should I do—for you?

If I could just pray—and surrender away—then you’d certainly say—what it is I should I do—for you.

Then I could report—that my life is OK—for pleasing you—is what I should do—for you!

Your insignificant servant,  
Trivikrama Swami

## Umāpati Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Ignorance is Bliss**

I like to think I’m smart. I guess most people are like that. I like to show it off too, but somehow, that never worked well around Śrīla Prabhupāda. Once I went to fix Śrīla Prabhupāda’s tape recorder at 26 Second Avenue. It was the old reel-to-reel kind, and I felt confident, armed with years of professional recording experience, but I was unfamiliar with that particular machine.

I put my finger on an unmarked button. “What does this button do?” I asked.

“Then you do not know how to fix this,” Śrīla Prabhupāda said. “If you have to ask, ‘What does this button do?’ then you cannot fix it.”

I was taken aback. I had thought my vast knowledge of electronics was already famous throughout the spiritual world. Someone came in the door, and Śrīla Prabhupāda pointed at me.

“He thinks he can fix this, but he has to ask ‘What does this button do?’” said Śrīla Prabhupāda, laughing.

I did not say anything more. I just sat there, burning with humiliation from this extraordinary kindness of my spiritual master.

It happened again a few years later. Aniruddha and I had to drive across Los Angeles and back to find out why Śrīla Prabhupāda’s two new transcribing machines did not work. The dealer showed me that I had threaded the tape wrong. I had not run it past the little gizmo that tells the machine to turn on. It should have been obvious. This time I did not wait for Śrīla Prabhupāda to tell me I was stupid. I was the one who mentioned it.

And what can I say about all the times I dropped things or fumbled my words in Śrīla Prabhupāda’s presence? Would I ever get smart?

Hope came one day when someone put on a tape of Śrīla Prabhupāda lecturing and I marveled at how beautifully Prabhupāda spoke. “Why can’t I speak just a little bit like that?” I wondered.

And the answer came. “It’s because I think I have something to say on my own,” I thought. “I think I know something, but I don’t know anything. Only Śrīla Prabhupāda knows, but I can borrow his knowledge.”

It is a relief to know that I am stupid. I don’t have to make people think I am smart any more. Now I just want them to see how intelligent my spiritual master is. The more I realize that I am stupid, the more I can accept Śrīla Prabhupāda’s mercy.

Now I realize that Śrīla Prabhupāda had been making exactly this point since the beginning: “The disciple’s mind should be like a blank slate upon which the spiritual master can write.”

I still cannot speak like Śrīla Prabhupāda. Well, can anyone? So now I try to let him speak through me. I have finally understood that this is what I am supposed to do. Now I have to work at understanding more and more that the only way to be smart is to borrow knowledge from my spiritual master.

I thought I knew something, but all my false knowledge was only filtering out the sweetness of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Let me not be a professional any more, nor an engineer or a mystic or a sage or all those other things I thought I was or wanted to be. Let me be a simple disciple with no knowledge of my own but with knowledge borrowed from my spiritual master.

And let me offer endless obeisances to Śrīla Prabhupāda, the only intelligent person I have ever met. How kind he is! He has taught me the value of knowing that I am stupid.

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your most humble servant,  
Umāpati Swami

## Varṣāṇā Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you!

This homage is one of gratitude,  
Coming from an inmate  
Whose life was transformed  
By your love.

I am sure you know  
What I am going to say,  
But I will speak it anyway,

Out of gratitude to you  
And for my own purification.

During the time before you appeared,  
To bring hope into my life,  
I was a prisoner  
Living in darkness.

And even though  
The door was not locked,  
I kept it closed  
Because I was afraid...

Then one blessed day,  
By divine arrangement  
You appeared at the door of my prison,  
Telling us what it was like to be free.

And even though we couldn’t conceive  
What freedom might be,  
We had faith in your words  
Because we knew that you loved us.

And by hearing your instructions  
I found the courage  
To open the door  
A little...

With that meager invitation  
You entered my life,  
Touching and transforming  
Everything in it.

You not only entered  
The door of my prison,  
You also opened  
The portals of my heart

And you taught me  
To cleanse my heart  
Because it was meant to be  
A temple.

In the decades to follow  
I still stumble and fall sometimes,  
But it’s different now  
Because I feel your hand  
Picking me up again.

And when it hurts too much,  
Sometimes I cry,

But it’s different now  
Because I feel your kind and tender touch  
Mending my heart.

Though I still struggle  
Confronting obstacles,  
It is different now  
Because I feel your guidance

Leading me by the hand,  
Lightening my burden,  
Lighting the way.

And though I still have trouble  
Trying to understand,  
It is better now  
Because I hear your words

Assuring me  
It will be all right.

And now I am sure,  
As never before,  
That whatever happens,  
Even when I die,

You will be there  
To lead me onward  
Out of my dungeon  
And back to home,

Where there are no prisons...

No longer victim of circumstance,  
Forever captive of your love.  
Do you remember?—you called us inmates,  
And we remain so to this day.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
On this most blessed event,  
When you appeared in our world  
To set us free  
From the bondage of fear.

Your servant,

Varṣāṇā Swami

## Vedavyāsapriya Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to your divine grace, which is the only reason for my being in your movement.

Well, like many of your other disciples, I have spent around twenty-five years in your movement. That is quite a considerable portion of this life. Nevertheless, I am amazed to think of the vast nescience that still covers this insignificant being in the temporary material world. This temporary material world is infinitely variegated for me. It is too difficult for me to understand the things, beings, atmospheres, and even the Vaiṣṇavas around me, especially the interplays of their subtle but complicated natures. As Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa says, *gahanā karmaṇo gatiḥ*. The material world weaves such a web around me that I cannot figure out independently how to escape from the fear produced by it. Thus I remain stuck in the web of material life due to my old habits.

But your divine grace taught me the art of living like a spider, who does not get trapped in its own web. I have been fortunate enough to obtain your divine grace through the efforts of your sincere devotees, who causelessly kept after me to join your movement. They said that it was your desire. I accepted. I have never regretted that decision. In good times or bad, in praise or disapproval, in all conditions I have always felt Your Divine Grace by my side. Many times I could see no way that I could ever became successful, certainly not by adopting such opinions as ritvikism. Thus it is only your glorious divine grace that has sustained me for the last twenty-five years. Your causeless divine grace amazes me in all conditions. Now I have the courage to live on and serve without any complaint. I will try my best to glorify your divine grace. Anybody who tasted your divine grace will give all credits to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and forget the material web. Thus your movement remains glorious and will fulfill your prophesies.

Your servant eternally,  
Vedavyāsapriya Swami

## Vipramukhya Swami

**What Kind of Person are You, Śrīla Prabhupāda?**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you!

When you boarded the cargo ship known as the *Jaladuta* and sailed from Calcutta to New York, you crossed many vast bodies of water. From the mouth of the Gaṅgā your ship slipped into the Bay of Bengal. From there you sailed south, around the southern tip of the Indian subcontinent into the Indian Ocean.

Although you wrote that your cabin was quite comfortable, you started to experience seasickness early in the journey. “Seasickness, dizziness, vomiting—Bay of Bengal. Heavy rains. More sickness,” you wrote. It was already a terrible journey for you.

In my restless nights, tossing and turning, I wonder: Had you known you would suffer so many difficulties aboard that ship, would you still have sailed to America, Śrīla Prabhupāda?

When you first stepped on land at Colombo, Sri Lanka, you felt some relief. The captain drove you around to see Colombo by car. The ship then sailed to Cochin on the west coast of India. There your trunks of books arrived and were taken aboard the ship. You spoke to the crew of the *Jaladuta* on Janmāṣṭamī. They were comfortable days.

But by the time the ship sailed by the usually dry, desolate landscape of the Red Sea coastline, you were again experiencing serious health problems. The weather had turned stormy, and the ship’s ride became rough. “Rain, seasickness, dizziness, headache, no appetite, vomiting.” These were more than symptoms of seasickness. You had severe pains in your chest, and in two days you appeared to have suffered two heart attacks.

What kind of person would undergo this kind of hardship for no other reason than faith in the guru and compassion for the fallen souls?

After that you had a dream that Lord Kṛṣṇa was rowing a boat. He told you that you should not fear and should come along. You said that after that you felt assured of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s protection. The violent attacks did not recur.

Thank you, Kṛṣṇa! Thank you!

The ship entered the Suez Canal and sailed through the Mediterranean Sea. From there it sailed across the Atlantic. Fortunately, the Atlantic crossing was smooth. Your ship landed at Boston’s Commonwealth Pier at 5:30 A.M. on September 17, 1965.

What would we have done had you not made that journey, Śrīla Prabhupāda? What would have been our fate? Never mind the struggles of your disciples and the difficulties we sometimes face. Where would we be had you not crossed the ocean? What words can express the depth of gratitude that the living entities of this earth planet owe you for having walked up that sacred gangplank one day in Calcutta?

You infiltrated not only American society but also the whole world, in such a quiet, inconspicuous way, after personally undergoing such severe health risks on the high seas. In like a needle, out like a plough. That was your motto. You were already at such an advanced age. What kind of person are you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to have done that for us? What kind of person would give up his homeland at the age of seventy and, with no money and no motivation other than compassion, make such a difficult journey? What kind of person would start with absolutely nothing except faith in guru and Kṛṣṇa and inspire such an empire of devotees to preach all over the world? What kind of person talks only of Kṛṣṇa and convinces everyone he meets to surrender to the Lord? What kind of person are you, Śrīla Prabhupāda?

I never met such a person before. I have never met such a person since. I am surrendered to you. Please accept me.

Your servant,  
Vipramukhya Swami

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from ISKCON Centers

## Ahmedabad

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Lord Caitanya’s wonderful pastimes as Nimāi Paṇḍita—extraordinary genius, the greatest scholar of his time—were in full bloom. He was surrounded by brilliant students, who were all very proud to study under Him and always accompanied Him everywhere. At the end of this part of His pastimes, before manifesting ecstatic symptoms of *kṛṣṇa-prema,* the Lord went to Gayā to offer oblations to His departed father. On the way He developed a heavy fever. His students gave Him so many treatments, but His fever would not come down. Finally He told His students, “Bring some water that has washed the feet of a *brāhmaṇa,* as there is a saying, *sarva-duḥkha khaṇḍe vipra-pādodaka-pāne*. Only then will My fever come down.” Finally the Lord drank the water from a *brāhmaṇa’s* feet and was instantly cured. The Lord thus demonstrated the glories of His pure devotees’ feet.

Indeed, as we sing every day,

*jāhāra prasāde bhāi, e bhava toriyā jāi,*  
*kṛṣṇa-prāpti hoy jāhā ha’te*

Your lotus feet enable everyone to cross over the ocean of birth and death, and they deliver Kṛṣṇa to all. Once a devotee said, “We are so fortunate, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that we came to the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement,” and you said, “I have created your good fortune.” As Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura sang, *jogyatā-vicāre, kichu nāhi pāi, tomāra karuṇā—sāra*: “When I consider my qualifications, I see have none. I am simply made of your mercy.” Prahlāda Mahārāja said, *evaṁ janaṁ nipatitaṁ prabhavāhi-kūpe*: “I was drowning in the ocean of birth and death like other materialistic people. Your servant Nārada Muni saved me. How can I do anything else but follow his order now?”

There is no way we can repay our debt to Your Divine Grace. If we have any shame, we should simply dedicate this life and the coming hundreds of lives in your service, and consider your instructions in every matter as the absolute words of Kṛṣṇa. In spite of our lowest of the low condition and sinful wretched hearts, if somehow or other we develop this absolute faith in your words and direction, we will inherit the kingdom of Kṛṣṇa just as easily as a son inherits his father’s wealth.

On the other hand, if we start seeing you as an ordinary saintly human being (a very great man but not absolute), then we will clear our pathway to hell. *Guruṣu nara-matir...yasya vā nārakī saḥ*. Kṛṣṇa’s *daivi-māyā* is very bewildering, and many an intelligent and thoughtful devotee’s faith has been shaken up by the continual falldowns of some of your most respected and leading disciples. These devotees want to re-evaluate everything, including your position, as if these falldowns are some kind of reflection on your performance, or as if these falldowns are your personal failure. On the contrary, some of us who heard you extensively and observed your personal saintly behavior from a very close distance knew it very well and protested from the very beginning the imitation tendency of some of our leaders and saw the oncoming disaster very clearly. In spite of many externally evident achievements in the preaching activities, which created some doubt in our inexperienced minds, your clear-cut teachings and example very clearly foretold that “of the existent (*sat*) there is no cessation and of the nonexistent (*asat*) there is no duration.” If anything, the happenings in our own ISKCON family in twenty-odd years since your departure have clearly established the absoluteness of the standard principles of *dharma,* standard behavior of simple saintly devotees, and authenticity of Vedic culture and *varṇāśrama-dharma*, which you carefully propagated throughout your books.

Even though you were humbler than a blade of grass, you taught us to remember your distinguished position and warned very clearly, as in the following conversation, against any tendency in our minds for self-glorification, since that would cause inevitable falldown.

**Prabhupāda:** This is our position. That is very easy for us. We haven’t got to manufacture. To manufacture idea is troublesome. Why should we take trouble? And as soon as you want to manufacture something to my..., that is dangerous. *Guru-mukha-padma-vākya, cittete kariyā aikya, āra nā kariha mane āśā*. This is...You are singing every day, “What our guru has said, that is our life and soul. We do not want...” *Āra nā kariha mane āśā*. And your guru’s article, you have given. Do you think is all right? We are reading every day, *yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādaḥ*. As soon as this poison will come—“Suppress guru and I become Brahman”—everything finished. Spiritual life is finished. Gauḍīya Maṭha finished, that..., violated the orders of Guru Mahārāja.  
**Patita-pāvana:** In that press release that we gave the paper we wrote a great deal about the work of Your Divine Grace in the West and how...  
**Prabhupāda:** So how you get it that “Here is only prominent, Surabhi Swami”?...So it is easy for us. What Kṛṣṇa says and Vyāsadeva says, that’s all. We haven’t got to manufacture. And as soon as you manufacture, fall down. *Evaṁ paramparā-prāptam imaṁ rājarṣayaḥ*...This manufacturing idea is very, very dangerous in spiritual life. Whatever little success is there for me, I did not manufacture any idea.  
**Tamāl Krishna:** Neither you take any credit.  
**Prabhupāda:** No. Why shall I take?

*tāṅdera caraṇa-sevī-bhakta-sane vāsa*  
*janame janame haya ei abhilāṣa*

Our mission is to serve *bhakta-viśeṣa* and live with devotees. Not that you take the place of the guru. That is nonsense, very dangerous. Then everything will be spoiled. As soon as you become ambitious to take the place of guru—*guruṣu nara-matiḥ*. That is the material disease. The article clearly says that it is everything, “your city,” “his city.” That is clearly intimated, that “You have dropped from the sky to give this city to the world.” That is the sum and substance. Is it not? What is this nonsense? So do the needful.

Only due to foolishness will one consider an empowered *ācārya* such as yourself to be an ordinary erring human being full of the four kinds of defects. Kṛṣṇa Himself says,

*ācāryaṁ māṁ vijānīyān nāvanmanyeta karhicit*  
*na martya-buddhyāsūyeta sarva-deva-mayo guruḥ*

**“One should know the** ***ācārya*** **as Myself and never disrespect him in any way. One should not envy him, thinking him an ordinary man, for he is the representative of all the demigods.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, all your words, all your instructions, all your statements are absolute, whether they support our false, egotistical, sense-gratificatory ideas or not. You do not represent any time- or space-bound culture or philosophy, such as the culture of early twentieth-century India. You always represent eternal spiritual principles, *sanātana-dharma*. Again, you are not interested in promoting any so-called religious ideas for the sake of some external show of religion. All your cultural and religious ideas, your ideas of *varṇāśrama*, including the *gṛhastha-āśrama*, as well as your ideas of *sva-dharma,* or duties prescribed according to one’s psychophysical nature—even your simple explanation of the *dehino ’smin yathā dehe* verse, which deals with the transmigration of the soul in changing bodies—all are connected to the highest goal of life, *kṛṣṇa-prema*. You embody the highest and purest position that a *jīva* soul can attain as a minute part and parcel of the supreme infinite Godhead. Yet your stature is so sublime, so great, that all thoughtful men bow down to you in full awe and reverence.

Recently a man named Acharya Dharmendra, who is one of the most popular Hindu leaders in India and probably the most powerful Hindi *bhāgavata-kathā* speaker, was in Ahmedabad. He said to the audience of over one hundred thousand people:

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON, has done the greatest service to the cause of Hindu dharma all over the world. He has increased the prestige, the honor, and the confidence in *sanātana-dharma,* and millions of people all over the world who were *go-bhakṣī* \[cow-eaters\] are becoming *go-bhaktas* \[devotees of cows\].

For twenty minutes he glorified you and your movement.

After his seven-day reading of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* he came to the temple, where he also delivered a speech, in which he said,

Of all the sons that mother India has given birth to in the twentieth century, Śrīla Prabhupāda is the greatest. No one can be compared with him. Swami Vivekananda only spoke theories. Prabhupāda was both theories and practice. He demonstrated practically how the *Bhagavad-gītā* is truth, how the *Bhāgavata* is truth, how Vedic culture is truth. He transformed the lives of people. He performed the greatest magic, achieved the highest mystical achievement. He was a simple saintly person. He was not a flamboyant speaker or a materially charismatic young man. His only power was pure devotion, with which he created a revolution in the world.

His eyes started shedding tears like torrents of rain and his voice choked up. He went on glorifying you for twenty minutes; I couldn’t have chosen better words or spoken more eloquently.

He has never stayed in ISKCON temples. He has only visited some of them and on a few occasions met a few devotees, but he has not associated with us intimately anywhere. He said he has read your books. But his profound realization of your greatness struck me with wonder. I realized the kind of expectations the pious, the elite, the enlightened religious Hindus have of ISKCON and your followers. These people are in fact admitting that ISKCON and your ideology are the only hope for *sanātana-dharma*. They are standing with folded hands, with anxious eyes, “Please save *sanātana-dharma*. Please save the world. ”

What to speak of the above-mentioned Indian religious leader, world-renowned Indologists, intellectuals, scholars, and religious historians such as A. L. Basham, Harvey Cox, Larry Shin,. Thomas Hopkins, and Stillson Judah have recognized and praised your spiritual status and achievements as unique in the modern world, comparable to what Jesus Christ and the Buddha did.The most attractive things about your wonderful personality are your honesty, transparency, absolute simplicity, and strong determination to preach the glories of the Lord. As Sanātana Gosvāmī told Haridāsa Ṭhākura in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Antya-līlā* 4.99–103):

*sanātana kahe,—“tomā-sama kebā āche āna*  
*mahāprabhura gaṇe tumi—mahā-bhāgyavān!*

*avatāra-kārya prabhura—nāma-pracāre*  
*sei nija-kārya prabhu karena tomāra dvāre*

*pratyaha kara tina-lakṣa nāma-saṅkīrtana*  
*sabāra āge kara nāmera mahimā kathana*

*āpane ācare keha, nā kare pracāra*  
*pracāra karena keha, nā karena ācāra*

*‘ācāra’, ‘pracāra’,—nāmera karaha ‘dui’ kārya*  
*tumi—sarva-guru, tumi jagatera ārya*

**Sanātana Gosvāmī said, “O Haridāsa Ṭhākura, who is equal to you? You are one of the associates of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Therefore you are the most fortunate. The mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, for which He has descended as an incarnation, is to spread the importance of chanting the holy name of the Lord. Now instead of personally doing so, He is spreading it through you. My dear sir, you are chanting the holy name 300,000 times daily and informing everyone of the importance of such chanting. Some behave very well but do not preach the cult of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, whereas others preach but do not behave properly. You simultaneously perform both duties in relation to the holy name by your personal behavior and by your preaching. Therefore you are the spiritual master of the entire world, for you are the most advanced devotee in the world.”**

So this is the absolute truth: *tumi—sarva-guru, tumi jagatera ārya.* You have shown the world how, by leading a simple ordinary honest Kṛṣṇa conscious life, first as a family man and then as a renunciant, how much can be accomplished by having faith in the holy name and the words of the spiritual master and by sincerely acting upon those words. You know this can be done by anyone and showed us how even we could do it by assisting you. Actually, you have made the highest spiritual life so real, so accessible and practical. Lord Caitanya appeared and showed by His example, *āpani ācari’ bhakti śikhāimu sabāre:* “By My example I will teach others.” In the same way, you taught and achieved the highest success. We have to keep your spirit alive, and then we can do miracles. But due to countless offenses at your feet, that spirit has diminished. You made spiritual life easy for us, but we didn’t deserve it. You once said, “You cannot offer pearls to the swine.” Instead of becoming swan, we have become swine. As Locana dāsa Ṭhākura sings:

*saṁsāre majiyā, rohili poriyā,*  
*se pade nahilo āśa*  
*āpana karama, bhuñjāye śamana,*  
*kahoye locana-dāsa*

**“I, Locana dāsa, regret that I am entrapped by sense gratification. Since I have no attraction for Lord Caitanya’s and Lord Nityānanda’s lotus feet, the superintendent of death, Yamarāja, is punishing me by not allowing me to be attracted by this movement.”**

In such a situation you are our only shelter. Please forgive our offenses and keep us in the shade of your lotus feet. Please give us the strength to understand your mission and push it forward. We can never repay the debt we owe you. But we know the only way to try to please you is to push forward your divine mission of saving mankind.

Please give us some faith and humility to help ourselves and suffering humanity.

Your humble servants at the ISKCON temple in Ahmedabad, Gujarat, India.

## Amravati

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances on this most auspicious day of your appearance.

This is our first opportunity to write a formal offering for your Vyāsa-pūjā. We are a relatively new center situated in the town of Amravati, very near the birthplace of Śrī Rukmiṇī-devī, in Maharashtra. We have a very strong and supportive congregation, and they have already donated a piece of land for a Rukmiṇī-Dvārakādhīśa temple. We are also holding a regular Sunday Feast program.

Rukmiṇī was attracted to the Personality of Godhead just by hearing about the Lord. On this most auspicious day of your appearance, we beg to follow her example and develop attraction for hearing the glories of the Lord. This will guarantee our journey back home, back to Godhead.

Your fledgling servants at the ISKCON center in Amravati, India.

## Aravade

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your fearless lotus feet.

It’s our good fortune to glorify you. You are a real *ācārya* because you showed by your own example how to become Kṛṣṇa conscious.

Whenever the Lord descends here, He comes with His associates. Similarly, you came here to deliver us, and then your associates also came to follow in your footsteps. You are always with us in your *vāṇī* form, protecting and guiding us in our daily lives. Your books are like magic. Wherever these books are read, the place and the person both get purified.

Here at ISKCON Aravade we are attempting to expand our activities for your pleasure. Most of your projects are in the cities, but we are starting a unique project in a typical village setting. But it is not just for one village—we wish to influence hundreds of villages in the vicinity. We are designing our temple project with various features that will serve this purpose. Eighty percent of Indians live in villages, and we are taking our first small steps to try to reach some of those people.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I feel in my heart that you are always with us. Kindly be merciful to me so that I will stay forever in your ISKCON, being fully Kṛṣṇa conscious and following strictly in your footsteps.

Your servants in Aravade, India.  
(written by Dharmarāja dāsa)

## Austin

My dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble *daṇḍavats* in the dust of your lotus feet.

I am most unqualified and unfit to be your disciple and serve you nicely. Therefore I must beg for your mercy that I may be able to somehow render some service that is pleasing to you. Especially this means being the dedicated servant of your servants. Please bless me that I can successfully discipline my thoughts, words, and deeds in all times, places, and circumstances so that your representatives, the ISKCON devotees, and you will happy with me.

In my initiation letter you ordered me to make the Austin center a grand success. In the second letter you wrote me you expressed your pleasure with my eagerness to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Amazingly enough, in spite of my innumerable disqualifications, in later correspondence you even encouraged me to become a spiritual master. Even though you have given me unlimited blessings to be a successful preacher, still I have accomplished so little for pushing forward your movement. Therefore I am humbly begging your mercy that in the remaining years I have in this body I may be able to do something truly wonderful for spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness, something that will please you very, very much. When I meet you again in Goloka Vṛndāvana I want to be able to give you a preaching report that will bring a big smile to your face. Apart from pleasing you, my life is a useless, meaningless void.

Your servants in Austin, Texas, USA.  
(written by Saṅkarṣaṇa dāsa Adhikārī)

## Benin

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your all-auspicious lotus feet. We, the servants of your humble servants, wish to express our profound gratitude for so much trouble and austerities you undertook during your old age—when people generally retire to enjoy the fruits of their labor—to establish this movement, from which we are benefiting so much.

You have given us so much, in spite of our faults. Your books fulfill all the desires of everyone, and your infinite compassion is manifest in every letter.

You are like a wish-fulfilling desire tree and have descended especially to save the Jagāis and Mādhāis of this age. We pray that, despite our disqualifications, we may hang on to the lotus feet of your servants, and also that we may be able to do a little insignificant service for the pleasure of your servants.

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda! Jaya Jagad-guru!

Your servants in Benin, Nigeria.

## Bhaktivedanta Manor

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Bhaktivedanta Manor is still here, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Your rooms are waiting for you. When are you coming?

This was the last temple in the West that you visited. You stayed here for three weeks in August and September of 1977. Do you remember, Śrīla Prabhupāda? When are you going to visit us again?

Your most merciful Lordships, Rādhā-Gokulānanda, are still here, Śrīla Prabhupāda. The number of visitors to the temple has dramatically increased over the years. Last year we had over 40,000 visitors for Janmāṣṭamī. You said that the Manor would become popular, and you were, of course, right. It has become very popular indeed.

We have more than thirty cows, bulls, and oxen now, and slowly Śyāmasundara dāsa and his crew want to increase the herd to sixty. The last time you visited we had fifteen acres of land. Now we have eighty. We have balanced the budget, and all the departments follow regular accounting procedures. Every year we get more than twenty thousand schoolchildren visiting from around the area. We have a great theater group. When are you coming again, Śrīla Prabhupāda?

We will pick you up at the airport and transport you to the Manor in a Rolls Royce. We will invite George Harrison to come and see you again, and we shall also invite many important dignitaries and politicians. Or, if you just want peace and quiet for translating work, we can arrange that, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Please come back, Śrīla Prabhupāda. See our new driveway coming in through the fields. See the newly planted trees. Encourage the devotees by confirming that you still love us, that you still care for us, and that you still remember us. Please come back.

We will gather around you in the temple room and play on the drums and *karatālas*. We will give you *caraṇāmṛta* and wash your hand after you greet the Deities. We will sit attentively and hear you speak about Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and we will follow you as you walk through the local fields on your morning walks. Please come back to the Manor, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

We will cry with tears of love and jump up and down. We will fall at your feet and wash them with rose-scented water. We will do anything that you want, and give you everything that you want. Only we ask one thing of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda: please visit us again.

Your servants at Bhaktivedanta Manor, UK.  
(written by Vipramukhya Swami)

## Bhubaneswar

Parama-pūjyā Parama-guru Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I remember you lovingly and worship you in my heart as a most revered personality and as the first successful preacher of *sanātana-dharma* throughout the whole world. For your incomparable devotional endeavor, the world will for all time to come remain ever grateful at your lotus feet. Though we understand this was possible only by the unlimited mercy of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, still it seems miraculous to us, since no one in the world but Your Divine Grace ever received such mercy from the Supreme Lord for such tremendous achievements. By your glorious efforts the message of Śrī Śrī Gaurāṅga Mahāprabhu and the holy name of the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa have reached every town and village in the world within a very short time. People from all countries throughout the world can now read *Śrīmad Bhagavad-gītā, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Śrī Īśopaniṣad, The Teachings of Lord Caitanya,* and many other revealed scriptures with your lucid purports, all beautifully bound and colorfully illustrated. This has no doubt helped innumerable people worldwide to understand *sanātana-dharma,* by which human life gains real success and becomes blissful. Men, women, children, young and old alike, can now see many lively paintings depicting the all-attractive pastimes of the Supreme Lord.

Due to your sincerity in chanting the holy name of the Supreme Lord and the favorable will of Lord Jagannātha, your single-handed preaching manifested in gigantic form, the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement throughout the world, with many temples, *gośālās,* farming communities, *gurukulas,* all in the service of Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. All thoughtful men should deeply reflect upon your devotional activities and gentle approach for successful service at the lotus feet of Śrī Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga. Revered Śrīla Prabhupāda, you came to this temple city of Bhubaneswar during the winter of 1977 and laid the foundation stone of Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma temple, your last founded ISKCON temple project in this world.
