As such your visit to Bhubaneswar is as significant as your first visit to the USA during September 1965.

After your arrival in New York, you began the global preaching of *sanātana-dharma* with a mere forty rupees, but by your deep faith in chanting the holy name of Lord Kṛṣṇa in homes, colleges, gardens, parks, and streets, people from all sectors gathered around you and joined with Your Divine Grace in congregational chanting. Some sincere people who understood the value of this human form of life but had little knowledge of the spiritual science taught by Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself understood your intense endeavor for the upliftment of all mankind and other living entities. In this way the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement was started by you in the modern world, while the people in general were boasting of great advancement in material science and basking in the outward glamor of material advancement, without realizing its misuse and devastating effect upon life. It was hard to imagine that human society could receive from you, with due submission, the sacred message of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and that by chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* people could gain real knowledge of the self without undergoing severe austerity and penance to achieve self-realization.

Your arrival in Orissa is a glorious chapter in the history of ISKCON, being the last part of your preaching mission. Your Divine Grace first established a temple in San Francisco and installed the deities of Śrī Śrī Jagannātha, Baladeva, and mother Subhadrā, but significantly you came to the land of Śrī Jagannātha during the later part of your mission. We feel you delivered here your grace and wisdom when your movement was in a fully matured state. Hence the people of Orissa were very fortunate and are now trying their level best to fulfill your desire. When Your Divine Grace came to Bhubaneswar, our most worshipable Gurudeva, His Holiness Gour Govinda Swami, booked several rooms in the Orissa State Guesthouse to comfortably accommodate Your Divine Grace and the devotees from various Western countries accompanying you. But you preferred to remain in a cottage constructed on temple land by our revered Gurudeva, His Holiness Gaura Govinda Swami, while the devotees were put up in tents.

At that time Your Divine Grace began translating the Tenth Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and inaugurated a *gurukula* by giving discourses every morning and evening on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and *Śrīmad Bhagavad-gītā* to thousands of people who had gathered. They were all astonished to hear from your lotus lips the most confidential messages of the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa and the deep purports contained in these Vedic literatures. They were also greatly amazed to see your simple living and high thinking. In your loving memory, as one of your most dear disciples, our Gurudeva, His Holiness Gour Govinda Swami Mahārāja, has accomplished the following:

1\. Publishing of a monthly magazine, the *Bhagavat Darsana,* in the Oriya language to bring your mission to every door in Orissa.

2\. Construction and inauguration of the Śrī Kṛṣṇa Balarāma temple at Bhubaneswar.

3\. Construction of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha temple at Bhubaneswar.

4\. Construction of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopāl-Jiu temple at Gadeigiri in Jagatsinghpur District.

5\. Establishment of Śrī Śrī Guru Gaurāṅga Press at Bhubaneswar.

6\. Establishment of a *gośālā* and farm at Jagannathpur, near Bhubaneswar.

7\. Construction of a temple at Jantaribol in Dhenkanal District.

8\. Establishment of Govind Bhojanalaya (Govinda’s Restaurant) at Bhubaneswar, inside the Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Temple compound.

9\. Translation into the Oriya language of many books by Your Divine Grace.

10\. Initiation of many devotees in Orissa and Western countries to preach your mission.

11\. In your loving memory, His Grace Śacīnandana dāsa, a grand-disciple of Your Divine Grace’s, has started a large temple project at Danda-bhanga, in Chandanpur, near Śrī Jagannātha Purī.

Many household devotees in various fields are leading Kṛṣṇa conscious lives of the ISKCON standard set by you under the spiritual guidance of our revered Gurudeva, working under the guidance of the present GBC, His Holiness Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Mahārāja. *Saṅkīrtana,* Padayātrā, Life Membership programs, book printing and distribution, *pandal* programs, etc., are in full swing in Orissa. A substantial number of *brahmacārīs* are now preparing to take charge of the ISKCON Orissa preaching.

O dearest grand–Guru Mahārāja, Śrī Śrī Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā are very dear to you. The people of Orissa and sacred Mohodadhi (the Bay of Bengal) are also very dear to you. You have given everything to us that we had long cherished, the service of the Supreme Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. However, you came to this land, known as Utkala in the *Vedas,* only once during your *ācārya* pastimes, and therefore we feel in urgent need of your association. We pray earnestly at your lotus feet to have your merciful glance upon us once again. Paying prostrated *daṇḍavats* at your lotus feet, we remain dependent upon your mercy.

Your servants in Bhubaneswar, Orissa, India.  
(written by Patita Pāvana dāsa)

## Boise

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

All glories to Lord Caitanya and to your incessant service for His *saṅkīrtana* fleet!  
All glories, all glories to your lotus feet,  
Which dance nonstop to the Hare Kṛṣṇa beat!

You gave us the four regs  
And the Bhaktivedanta purports  
Of incalculable import.  
Our lives they mold and support.  
You gave us Kṛṣṇa’s unlimited love and devotion.  
In return, you just wanted book distribution.

You gave us ISKCON bullets and other goodies  
For our empty hearts and hungry bellies.  
In return you just wanted Food for Life,  
So even the most unfortunate could survive.

You picked us up, one by one, patiently—  
*Śūdras, mlecchas,* and *narādhamas,* unconditionally.  
With the amazing ability to turn matter into spiritual transcendence  
And adjust according to time, place, and circumstance,  
You turned us into *brāhmaṇas* with your *paramahaṁsa* qualities—  
No, even higher: into devotees.

And then  
You founded ISKCON  
To facilitate six types of exchanges  
Between devotees:  
Accepting charitable gifts and offering gifts in charity,  
Revealing one’s mind in confidence and inquiring confidentially,  
Accepting *prasādam* and offering *prasādam* lovingly.

For this Vyāsa-pūjā offering, we meditate on the fourth *śloka* of *The Nectar of Instruction.*  
Had it not been for you, how close we were to self-destruction!  
How much you taught us is beyond calculation,  
And how poorly we followed is fit for condemnation.

Your mercy is unlimited.  
We do not know what we did to deserve it.  
That is inconceivable to our mind.  
However, we could use some more of it.  
We will be standing in line  
When you specially distribute it on your Vyāsa-pūjā divine.

Ever hungry for your mercy,  
The undeserving in New Biharvan, Boise.  
(written by Anantarūpa dāsa)

## Brisbane

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

On this most auspicious day of your divine appearance, we wish to make you an offering. It may take us a lifetime to complete this offering, and it will take lifetimes to maintain it.

We have undertaken the task of building a temple for your pleasure.

This is the perfection of our life. Building this place of residence for the Supreme Lord and His pure devotee is an offering of gratitude and appreciation. Without your distribution of the knowledge of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, neither the desire nor the attempt would ever have been a reality. There is no meaning to building a temple without you as the director, the project manager, and the grandfather of such an endeavor. We are pleased to report that many Indian persons have come forward to help. Many devotees in the temple and congregation have worked hard together to make this project unfold. It has been at times very demanding and at other times very slow, waiting for approvals from the city council and negotiations with other parties.

We have read of your struggles to make the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Rasabihārī temple in Juhu a success. We pray for your blessings that we can similarly overcome the obstacles we face in making this a success for our worshipable Lords, Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, so that Their name and fame can be spread throughout every town and village. We also require the protection of Your Divine Grace so that we do not become attached to the marble stone of the temple and quarrel among ourselves. We hope and pray that becoming unalloyed pure devotees of Kṛṣṇa will be our focus, and that we will not become complacent upon completion of this new facility but rather increase our preaching. Your mercy is essential for us to succeed.

Our vision for this project is multifaceted, but our major dream is for it to become a facility for educating souls about the science of *bhakti-yoga*. You have said so many times in your letters, lectures, and purports that although there are numerous institutions for learning mundane subject matter, where are the institutions for teaching about our real need as spiritual entities? We want to be able to offer to others the wonderful knowledge you have offered to us. So, Śrīla Prabhupāda, please allow this to manifest. Please use us as your tools in crafting the vessel of Lord Caitanya’s mercy.

Your servants in Brisbane, Australia.

## Buenos Aires

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances to your sacred lotus feet.

We came here today to get together with you in this physical place, hoping to be with you someplace just once in our consciousness. We don’t want you to think that we are before you because we have responded to some kind of Hindu tradition. In these times of skepticism and suspicion, interest in a mere cultural tradition could never make such a heterogeneous group of people leave their conditioned daily activities even for a few hours. It takes more than the force of a memory to rouse us from our “comfortable” inertia. The material world doesn’t stop to crumble, and several pieces of it had already fallen down on some of us, and sometimes we feel that being buried can guarantee us that nothing more will fall on our heads. The point is that today we made the effort to get out of our safe holes to come to see you, although so much light burns our eyes a bit.

The truth is that in spite of anything that could have happened or anything that could not be attained, we keep the highest expression of our love to you. We feel shamefully indebted, knowing we are incapable of repaying your consecrated life by which you give us the marvelous present of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. This present has proven to be a coat on a cold rainy night. If we wear it, if we put it on, it enables us to wait till the rain stops and the sun rises without major tribulations. As an option, we can carry it on hand till we decide to use it. Thank you for making it so simple. Thank you for not embellishing it with unnecesary magic. Thank you for making accessible the inaccessible. Just to cover ourselves with the sincere chanting of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa protects us from the moral, spiritual, and social catastrophe that razes our countries, neighborhoods, and families. Thanks again, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us something so valuable to teach to our children, brothers and sisters, friends and neighbors.

Our passing through the world could have been a complete loss of time if you hadn’t come from so far to show us how Kṛṣṇa manifested Himself in your heart and to teach us how to prepare ours for Kṛṣṇa also to manifest in. That’s why we came to honor you, for if we consider our own existence to be worthy, it’s not for any other reason than that you are giving us that dignity by making us carriers of the message of Kṛṣṇa and Lord Caitanya.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, though today we are making a special celebration, our entire life is a celebration of your appearance in this sad world. May Lord Kṛṣṇa enable us at some time to make something that makes you feel satisfied to have left your legacy in our hands.

Your servants in Buenos Aires, Argentina.  
(written by Muktinātha dāsa)

## Caracas

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my *daṇḍavat praṇāmas* at your lotus feet.

This year we are celebrating the twenty-fifth anniversary of your visit to Caracas, Venezuela, and while meditating on the significance of your very valuable association that we had while you stayed with us here, we decided to offer you some of those remembrances. We understand that these were eternal moments that we lived with you, and we wish, by your mercy, to keep these memories in our heart forever.

The night before your arrival, we stayed up until very late at night fixing the last details of your room by painting the windows and polishing the wooden floor. We finished around 2:00 a.m. Then something wonderful happened. Suddenly I felt very intensely your spiritual presence in the room, and I realized that our relationship with you is based on love and service.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, bless us so we can serve you with love eternally.

The next day, February 18, on the appearance of Śrī Advaita Ācārya, you arrived around 8:00 p.m. The *kīrtana* grew in intensity, and so did our enthusiasm. We felt that the temple was floating on air due to the transcendental atmosphere created by your presence. You were sitting on your *vyāsāsana,* and we chanted and danced vigorously in front of you. Suddenly you glanced at me. I never saw in my life anybody with such pure and transparent eyesight. It was like a window to the spiritual world being opened in front of us. An inhabitant of Kṛṣṇa’s land, Vṛndāvana, was among us. Your eyes were full of *prema,* pure love for Kṛṣṇa. With a compassionate glance you were approving our humble welcome. And I felt as if I was meeting a very old friend and well-wisher.

After the *kīrtana* you recited the *prema-dhvani* and then gazed with love at the Gaura-Nitāi Deities. You said that it was very nice to have these two Prabhus with us tonight. You wanted to sing a *bhajana* for Them, and you asked us to follow you. But as soon as you pronounced the first two words of the song, *parama karuṇa,*your throat choked up and couldn’t sing anymore. Then you reclined your head on the *vyāsāsana,* and we could see tears gliding down your face. An absolute silence prevailed, a moment eternal penetrated the temple room and changed our hearts forever. We all had the great fortune to see what the *śāstras* and the Vaiṣṇavas say about the exalted and glorious position of the pure devotee of the Lord. You were giving us your causeless mercy by showing us the symptoms of pure devotional service. Since then the Deities have been known as Parama Karuṇa Gaura-Nitāi.

Blissful days followed as we accompanied you to the park for morning walks, heard your lectures, and served you on *saṅkīrtana*.

Before meeting you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I used to lament because I wasn’t born at the time of Jesus Christ. My desire was to be guided by a genuine spiritual master. Kṛṣṇa mercifully fulfilled all my cherished dreams by putting me in touch with Your Divine Grace. In your presence everything was pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and I felt secure and protected from the onslaught of *māyā*. You were situated in *vasudeva-sattva,* the state in which one can always see Kṛṣṇa eye to eye by dint of pure affection for Him. Your talks were from that transcendental position. Your nectarean words penetrated the dense darkness of my heart, enlightening the self with the Absolute Truth. It is difficult to express with words that security and well-being that was felt in your presence. When very hard times came after your departure from this world, I took shelter in those eternal moments that we had with you. The remembrances of you and your teachings is what keeps me alive in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

One day during your visit I came back a little earlier from *saṅkīrtana*. Being a little distracted, I stepped down the stairs leading to your garden to go to the *brahmacārī āśrama* instead of going the other way around, as instructed. As soon as I saw you sitting in your rocking chair, I offered my obeisances. You called and asked me where I was coming from. I explained that I had just came back from distributing your books in the city. You smiled at me and asked how people in Venezuela liked your books. I described to you that the people here were generally pious and simple and that after reading your books they became favorable to the movement and continued to purchase them. You moved your head satisfactorily and asked me to come with you to your room. There you gave me a *mahā* sweet from a tray sitting on your desk while thanking me for assisting you in your mission. I was really moved by your gesture and just offered my obeisances. Then with your permission I left the room while eating the sweet you gave me. I was so happy for this unexpected causeless mercy.

Then the inevitable day of your departure from Caracas came. We were all crying, feeling your imminent separation. If a poor person suddenly becomes rich and after some time he loses his riches, then he will think of it all the time. Nobody appreciates what he has until he loses it. But the beauty of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is that our affection and attachment for Kṛṣṇa and His pure devotees, if genuine, increases with separation. In a letter to a disciple you explain very nicely the meaning of this feeling.

To feel separation from the Spiritual Master or Krishna is very good position. That means one who is in pure love with Krishna and His Representative, Spiritual Master, he thinks always of Them. And this thinking process is Krishna consciousness. If we can think always of Krishna even in separation, that is Krishna Consciousness. And in the absolute platform, there is no difference of separation and meeting. The separation is also meeting, rather in separation one relishes the loving relationship more tasty. \[Letter to Kṛṣṇa dāsa 21 October 1968\]

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, allow me to always remain a fool at your lotus feet, feeling loving union with you in separation.

Your servants in Caracas, Venezuela.  
(written by Jagat-cakṣur dāsa)

## Chamorshi

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

The devotees here at ISKCON’s farm in Chamorshi, Maharashtra, don’t have the technology of e-mail that is now spreading throughout India. Nor do we have televisions, videos, computer games, kitchen blenders, vacuum cleaners, or any other such modern conveniences. Here it is just simple Indian village life blessed with the mercy of devotional service to Kṛṣṇa

For this community of hard-working devotees who are mainly from East Bengal, having been relocated during the Bangladesh-India war of the 1970’s, Lord Nityānanda’s appearance day is the biggest festival of the year. We hold a week-long *pandal* program, inviting many ministers and VIP’s from the area. Thousands of people from the surrounding villages come to participate in nonstop *nāma-saṅkīrtana,* see the dramas depicting the *līlā* of Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityānanda, have *darśana* of the beautiful Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara, meet the devotees, and take a full plate of *kṛṣṇa-prasādam*. This festival is becoming very popular in the region, and each year we have more visitors. One favorable government minister has promised to build a new road so that visiting tourists and pilgrims can have easy access to the temple from the main road.

To hold bigger festivals and better glorify Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara, we would like to construct a large temple. We already have our own brick factory and plan to make the required bricks for the temple construction.

We invite full-time and congregational devotees to come and stay with us for awhile. Breathe the clean air and eat the fresh vegetables, and you will feel the health of your body strengthen and the peacefulness of your mind increase. We guarantee it. We recently acquired some more land, so now the production of agricultural crops has also increased.

We want to add some dioramas in a museum to convey spiritual knowledge in an easy way and attract the simple-hearted villagers to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were always in favor of dioramas for preaching. Your Guru Mahārāja even had a traveling diorama exhibit. If spiritual instruction can be imparted in this way, it can be very effective.

We conduct regular programs in as many as forty of the surrounding villages. We have a lot of guests coming, but we still lack guesthouse facilities.

His Holiness Lokanāth Mahārāja has been reminding us of your thoughts and concepts of self-sufficiency—depending on the land and the cows. He tries to hammer it into us, but we are a bit slow in implementing it. We wish to do this sooner rather than later.

Thank you for your kindness in creating ISKCON for us to take shelter in. We don’t always do it right, but we are trying, and hopefully, through our faulty attempts, we will gradually learn and become stronger in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Your servants at in Chamorshi, India.

## Chandigarh

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Jaya oṁ viṣṇu-pāda paramahaṁsa parivrājakācārya aṣṭottara-śata śrī śrīmad 108 His Divine Grace Śrī Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!

ISKCON, BBT, BI founder-*ācārya* Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!

Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara *kī jaya*!

Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava Aṣṭa-sakhī-vṛnda *kī jaya*!

Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Rasabihārī, Śrī Śrī Sītā-Rāma-Lakṣmaṇa-Hanumān *kī jaya*!

Śrī Śrī Jagannātha, Baladeva, Subhadrā-devī *kī jaya*!

Śrī Śrī Pañca-tattva, Śrī Śrī Lakṣmī-Narasiṁhadeva *kī jaya*!

Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu *kī jaya*!

Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra kī jaya*!

Śrī Śrī Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava Vedāntācārya-vṛnda *kī jaya*!

Vṛnda-devī, Gaṅgā-devī, Yamunā-devī, Bhakti-devī *kī jaya*!

Līlā-dhāma *kī jaya*!

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet, and please continue to give your causeless mercy through this Hare Kṛṣṇa movement. Your Divine Grace has done so much in such a short time to propagate Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s message all over the world. The significance of this movement was never in doubt. Your glory is unlimited in founding and guiding this movement.

Please bless your aspiring grandsons and granddaughters in Chandigarh to remain strong in their devotional service.

Your servants in Chandigarh, India.

## Colombia

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my fallen obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to you and the unlimited mercy you bestow on behalf of the Supreme Personality of Godhead!

“Hope against hope.” Sometimes I try to express this thought in Spanish, but it’s difficult. (Sometimes they say “hope is the last thing you lose,” but I don’t think that quite captures the phrase.) Yet I wish to convey this feeling to you for your divine appearance day. I may remain a wretched nobody, floundering down here in Colombia, still struggling just to maintain ISKCON’s humble foothold in this troubled land. Yet I remain hopeful, even in the face of hopelessness. There are hopeful signs: Māyāpur-śaśi Prabhu’s considerate one-line message to us, “Thank you for sending in your March *saṅkīrtana* scores.” (New millennium, new determination: let us do this each and every month.) And right now, downstairs, I hear an exuberant yet sweet *Gurv-aṣṭaka kīrtana* in honor of your good disciple, Jayapatāka Swami, since today (as of this writing, April 14th) is his birthday and his disciples are rightfully honoring him with many Ekādaśī offerings and their heartfelt gratitude. Of course, they are automatically glorifying Your Divine Grace as well. How could they fail to do so?

Hopeful signs...The temple atmosphere has gradually improved over the years. In Alachua, your New Ramaṇa-reti community, Hridayānanda dāsa Goswami stated in a class that the movement’s maturing is natural. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, had to work not only with hippies but with very young ones at that. Mahārāja noted that now we have many devotees over fifty years old actively serving and participating in your society. Back in the old days it was a great novelty for even one such senior fellow to be seen and heard chanting the holy names. Here in Bogota we are also getting older, yes, and wiser by your mercy. We read the matchless gifts you still hand us in the form of your divinely written tomes. Passion and ignorance are actually subsiding, and for this you prayed in your ecstatic poem penned upon your Western arrival, at Commonwealth Pier, Boston. (Of course, now the *kīrtana* for Mahārāja is building up, but in transcendental passion, I daresay...)

The last words you spoke to this fool were “And we are not asking for any money.” But I worry about that issue, and have been doing just that for the major part of my years trying to serve your ISKCON in one or another of its various “camps.” So another sign of hope is that despite the very poor economic (and political, etc.) situation in this place, I nonetheless trust that we will become blessed by the Goddess of Fortune so as to better represent this sacred line of Vaiṣṇavism. We must “show” as well as “tell” the deep and sublime culture of *bhakti*.

Of course, purity is the force. And that is the greatest challenge, and really the ultimate hope—that we may accelerate our march back to Godhead by sacrificing all our *anarthas*. Unfortunately, we still have a lot to lose, but such “loss” is all-auspicious, to become free from the maudlin, lingering obsessions with the objects of the senses, to yank our anchors up from the muck once and for all. This is the noble fight, this is worth striving for. Let the knot in the heard be sundered, all doubts smashed to smithereens, and the chain of fruitive action be everlastingly ended! And that this wonderful liberation is actually occurring is all by your divine grace. Otherwise, it is only our own nonsense which stands between ourselves and the waiting, open arms of Guru and Kṛṣṇa.

So I send this simple offering on behalf of the devotees here in Bogota and (if they permit) of other Vaiṣṇavas from other sections of Colombia as well. It is also being sent from my own heart. Despite my optimism, I still remain a very fallen, most insignificant aspiring servant. And is there hope for me? Yes. By your mercy I am gradually becoming less offensive!

On a very personal note, I pray to Your Divine Grace that you may somehow extend one drop more of your mercy toward my elderly, ailing material father. He innocently went to speak with you in 1966, at your 26 Second Avenue temple. He didn’t believe in Lord Kṛṣṇa, but he thought you would be able to advise him concerning his wayward, LSD-affected son. (You told him something to the effect that “We can fix him up...”). He is very proud to have met you back then, and that is his good fortune. I beg you to please, kindly bless him again, that he may remember his most auspicious encounter with you at the crucial moment. All glories to Your Divine Grace! Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!

With reverence and with love,  
Your servants in Colombia.  
(written by Prabhupāda dasa)

## Columbus

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet on this most auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā.

Most of us here at the Krishna House in Columbus, Ohio, never had the opportunity to physically meet you. Most of us joined in the 80’s and 90’s. We are disciples of your disciples. Still we feel the connection with Your Divine Grace as very real. This is why:

Just as you painstakingly taught your disciples the art of *bhakti-yoga,* so in their turn they are teaching us how to please Kṛṣṇa and His devotees.

Just as you patiently instructed them how to vigorously spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness to every town and village, they are constantly instructing us by their example.

Just as you spoke tirelessly about the importance of understanding the goal of life, so they are stressing this importance to us over and over again.

Just as you freely shared with your disciples the bliss and excitement of preaching, so they are equally sharing them with us.

Just as you inspired them to go out of their way to try to please you, so they are inspiring us in so many ways to try to please them, and through them, to try to please you.

How do we know all this to be true?

Because your instructions are in your books, in your letters, in your lectures, and in your disciples’ hearts.

For the benefit of all devotees (present and future) you have built a beautiful treasure-house of transcendental knowledge, carefully organized and available to all.

Dear spiritual Grandfather, we are getting ready for yet another summer of preaching the science of self-realization. More devotees are coming to Columbus to enroll in Ohio State University. The preaching field is expanding. We are all eager to get the “higher taste.” The ingredients of the recipe for spiritual bliss (to give out in order to experience) have not changed: the distribution of your books, the distribution of *kṛṣṇa-prasādam,* and the distribution of the holy name.

We humbly beg for your blessings to be able to wholeheartedly become engaged in these activities. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for allowing us to be part of your ever-expanding ISKCON family.

Your aspiring servants in Columbus, Ohio, and Louisville, Kentucky, USA.  
(written by Rasa-mañjarī-devī dāsī)

## Coventry

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. Hare Kṛṣṇa!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, on my annual trip to Bhāratavarṣa for the GBC meetings, I get the opportunity to preach on the trains and the railway station platforms. Every year my conviction is strengthened that your wonderful books and the *śikṣā* in them are very clear and potent.

When I first read in your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* about the phenomenon of *sarga* and *visarga,* I never thought I would ever use it for preaching. I am amazed at how well this explanation of primary and secondary creation goes down in Bhāratavarṣa, or with the Bhāratīya people living abroad. The listeners are suddenly overwhelmed with the depth of it all and stop arguing. They then attentively listen to the rest of the Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava message. After hearing details of the *puruṣa-avatāras, guṇa-avatāras,* and *līlā-avatāras,* they usually end up accepting that Śrī Kṛṣṇa is the *ādi-puruṣa* and not an expansion of Śrī Viṣṇu, as is the common belief in Bharata.

Another regular example from your books that I often use is of our experience during deep sleep each night, when we all become unaware of our bodies. This example convinces the listeners that they are something other than the body or mind. After hearing about self-realization and God realization and the eternal relationship among God, matter, and the soul (*sambandha*), they usually take to the chanting of the *mahā-mantra,* which of course is our whole aim. Following the combination of chanting and preaching, we finally exchange addresses. At this point I strongly recommend that they read your wonderful books, which I tell them will guarantee enlightenment.

I normally carry a *mṛdaṅga* and cymbals with me and encourage everyone to join in the chanting. In the beginning people are shy, but when they are convinced by the preaching it is amazing how they end up closing their eyes and blissfully joining in. Śrīla Prabhupāda, I can feel your presence every time I preach and glorify your transcendental books. Thank you for bringing Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mission and giving us an opportunity to engage in this transcendental activity. Thank you for giving to this world all that you have. It has made all the difference.

We here in the Coventry *yātrā* are excited about Kṛṣṇa consciousness and look forward to newer and newer experiences in our Kṛṣṇa conscious life. We beg for your mercy. Your lovely center in Coventry, UK, is attracting nice devotees. The mood is one of unity and co-operation. You are daily being worshiped with awe and reverence. You have given us two lovely Deities, Śrī Śrī Śacīnandana and Nitāichand, which are the life of the devotees serving here. Thank you from everyone here. Please keep giving us more service, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Haribol!

Your servants in Coventry, UK.  
(written by Hari dāsa)

## Czech Yatra

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

As the darkness of Kali-yuga steadily spreads around, the glowwormlike speculators, with their fancy humanistic, socialistic, and democratic views, are becoming more and more prominent. With their high-sounding words they are trying to cover the original meaning of your teachings, bringing forth their relative and anti-authoritative views. Considering simple and sublime to be primitive, they try to impress the ignorant public with their complicated speeches, which finally aim at the classical Māyāvāda ideals of impersonal oneness. And so they are fascinated by social and economical concepts, but neglect the personal soul. Eager to hear what is spoken but not who is speaking, they consider a mere accumulation of words to be sufficient to prove the intellectual merit of the speaker. Puffed up with their academic education, they declare the simple and sublime nature of devotional service to be not adequate to satisfy their intricate mental needs. Bringing everything to the mundane level of their limited minds, they declare that there is nothing beyond that and that the requirement for devotional service is mental, physical, and of course economic satisfaction. So-called philanthropists are preaching, in name of spirituality, that bodily needs are the highest goal. In this way Kali is whipping his victims to newer and newer heights of madness resulting from sensual stimulation or frustrated *tyāga—*ideals preached by the bogus leaders and so-called spiritualists. These so-called transcendentalists are not even able to follow the basic moral principles of human life. As they boast with their manifestoes through the global media, the streets are getting empty of *harināma* parties, your book warehouses are becoming museums of dusty books, and the temples are empty, with no new *bhaktas* and *bhaktins* in sight.

As the residents of Kali-yuga engage in *grāmya-kathā* globally, they are more and more becoming poor-hearted and lonely locally while sitting isolated in their concrete compartments and dedicating their energy to mental schemes.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, this darkness makes your sublime words shine all the more—words describing in an ingenious way the life of simple living and high thinking. You wonderfully distilled the essence of all knowledge and removed the darkness, not only by your words but also by your personal example. What potent ways you showed us to discover reality by pushing us out into the streets with *kīrtana,* by impressing upon us the absolute need of distributing your books, and by saving the conditioned souls with the miracle diet called *prasādam*. Just by dedicating our lives to these three devotional activities, we find ourselves dancing in the street amidst the dark residents of this age, we find ourselves tasting the immediate nectar of giving people your books and then watching them get excited in ecstasy as we laugh in pure bliss while driving to the next destination in our *saṅkīrtana* vans, and we find ourselves witnessing the gradual transformation of their hearts as they taste what they believe to be “vegetarian food” but what is in reality Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s *prasādam*.

What a life you gave us, Śrīla Prabhupāda! Laughing cynically at our minds, we may learn how to tolerate them. And while our false egos scream at us to finally become God, we, even more inspired, bow down again and again to your lotus feet, by your causeless mercy feeling again and again relieved from carrying the heavy load of pride and arrogance.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, in this ocean of Kali-yuga madness, your kindness is daily being revealed more and more. It was you who taught us that life comes from life and that this life means preaching. The debt we owe you can never be repaid, but at least we can try by tolerating our conditioning and dedicating whatever energy we have left to your preaching mission. This mission is simple for the simple and complicated for the complicated. Chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa in public and introducing people to your books and to *prasādam* is simple and sublime.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for these simple and sublime gifts. Thank you for the simple, blissful, and sublime life you gave us. Thank you for offering us the possibility of going back home, back to Godhead, and never returning to this material world.

All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Your servants in the Czech Republic.  
(written by Maṇidhara dāsa)

## Dallas

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

We praise His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, the bona fide spiritual master at whose feet all other bona fide spiritual masters sit, a perfect representative of God, giver of knowledge of God, and guide to the kingdom of God. Without the blessings of His Divine Grace, achieving the goal of life, love of God, is impossible.

Information about God is found in the scriptures of the world. Indeed, that information is what makes scripture different from all other books, fiction and nonfiction. Fiction is made-up stories, and nonfiction, although supposedly factual, deals only with the mundane, temporary sphere of life. An unbiased person who looks at the various scriptures of various cultures will agree that the Vedic scriptures of India contain the most complete information about God. But without the guidance of a bona fide spiritual master, even the knowledge they contain is difficult to understand and apply.

Many people study the *Vedas* and conclude that there are many Gods, or that God is ultimately impersonal. In the *Bhagavad-gītā,* however, the Supreme Lord, the source of all spiritual and material worlds, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, declares that one must approach a spiritual master if one wants to know the truth. One must seek out a saint, a *mahātmā*—a great soul. How do we know who such a person is? In the ninth chapter of the *Gītā* Lord Kṛṣṇa says that “those who are not deluded, the great souls, are under the protection of the divine nature,” that they are “fully engaged in devotional service because they know Me \[Kṛṣṇa\] as the Supreme Personality of Godhead, original and inexhaustible,” and that they are “always chanting My glories, endeavoring with great determination, bowing down before Me,” always “worshiping Me with devotion.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 9.13–14)

Although Śrīla Prabhupāda, in his transcendental humility, never declared, “I am such a *mahātmā,*” one can, by examining Prabhupāda’s sterling qualities, clearly see that he perfectly fit the above description.

Prabhupāda gave us the essence of the Vedic scriptures.

Prabhupāda was always under the divine nature.

Prabhupāda engaged fully in devotional service...and taught his followers to do the same.

Prabhupāda knew that Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Personality of Godhead and broadcast that fact.

Prabhupāda canvassed on Kṛṣṇa’s behalf: “My dear, suffering souls lost in the darkness of this illusory world of ignorance, please take up the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness and go back home, back to Godhead, back to Kṛṣṇa.”

Prabhupāda taught the *yuga-dharma,* the *saṅkīrtana-yajña,* the means of God realization for this age, the chanting of the Lord’s holy names—Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare—as taught by Lord Caitanya.

Prabhupāda spread the teachings of Kṛṣṇa and Kṛṣṇa’s name all over the world.

Prabhupāda trained disciples to carry on his wonderful work, opening temples and installing Deities, so that people everywhere can receive transcendental benefit.

Śrīla Prabhupāda was indeed the most bona fide of all bona fide spiritual masters, and he continues to distribute nectar to all who are fortunate enough to read his enlightening books.

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s servants in Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kālachandjī Dhāma, Dallas, Texas, USA.  
(written by Prajāpati dāsa)

## Denpasar

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Although you visited Indonesia only once, staying for just seven days in Jakarta about twenty-seven years ago, that visit was very tremendous. It will eternally be a very important event in the history of the Indonesian branch of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. During that visit you planted the seed of the tree of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in Indonesia, and that tree will surely grow bigger and bigger because that is what you wanted.

With almost 200 million citizens, Indonesia is the fifth most populous country in the world. How could Indonesia—or, for that matter, the world—face its many problems without Kṛṣṇa consciousness? We have received your mercy in the form of your extraordinary books, and now the movement is increasing tremendously. This is all happening due to your mercy.

The best service we can do for you is to just distribute your books as much as possible, year after year. O Śrīla Prabhupāda, please kindly shower your mercy upon us by sending some of your pure disciples to Indonesia to preach. Only in this way can we be inspired to continue your very sublime and holy mission.

Finally, all of us Indonesian devotees request your perpetual shelter and guidance, because we are fully conscious that you never passed away. You eternally exist together with your disciples.

*Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare*

Your servants in Denpasar, Indonesia.  
(written by Sūrya-nārāyaṇa dāsa)

## Devasadan Mandir

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Our most beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

You came to Detroit and gave this wonderful Devasadan Mandir to all the residents of Michigan. Now they can come and have the most divine experience just by visiting this temple. In your short visit, you not only purified Michigan but also established a pilgrimage spot in Detroit. When we see the photos of your different pastimes here, we realize that wherever you went you were planting the seeds of *kṛṣṇa-bhakti*. The photo of you paying obeisances to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kuñjabihārī reminds us of how much you loved Them and wanted Them to be worshiped nicely. You also instructed us to take care of Them like a king, a queen, and princes. At the same time, you prayed to Them to accept our services even though we were not perfect. Śrīla Prabhupāda, only with your power and blessings will we be able to serve Them perfectly.

We see the picture of you in the garden, instructing us to preach. It is only due to your power that so many visitors are attracted to come and see your Bhaktivedanta Cultural Center. They are pleased to see the beauty of the Cultural Center, so well maintained under the able supervision of Śrīmān Bhūṣāya Prabhu and with the most generous continuing financial support of Śrīmān Ambarīśa Prabhu. But beyond just visiting the Center, guests are introduced to the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness through the tours, *prasādam,* beautiful paintings, and your transcendental books.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, by your inspiration and guidance we have displayed many of your books in the reception hall, the library, the guest bedroom, and your room. Now we are trying to display all your books, including all sixty translations of your *Śrīmad Bhagavad-gītā As It Is*, in Śrī Nāthjī Hall. The talented father of our temple president, Śrīmān Giri-Govardhana Prabhu, has agreed to make the display with the help of other devotees. So please bless us so we may complete it soon.

One most wonderful bit of news we have to report is that your *Śrīmad Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* has become the most popular book in Michigan among Indians. So now all the different *samājas* (societies) are agreeing to distribute your *Bhagavad-gītā* to their members as a gift. We are so fortunate that your great leaders at the BBT are continuing to print new books of yours, such as *Beyond Illusion and Doubt.* These are printed first class, with a very attractive cover and binding. These books help our team of book distributors, under the leadership of Śrīmān Puruṣa-sūkta dāsa, so much that many people on the street actually approach them looking for your books.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we would like to thank you so much for giving us the Devasadan Mandir, the most beautiful Deities—Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kuñjabihārī; Śrī Śrī Śrī Jagannātha Swami, Baladeva, and Subhadrā Mātā; Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi; and Lord Śrī Nāthjī—the Bhaktivedanta Cultural Center, the Sunday Feast program, the Ratha-yātrā festival, *harināma,* and book distribution and *prasādam* distribution programs. And finally, we would like to thank you especially for giving us as our guides, caretakers, and GBCs your most loyal and sincere disciples His Grace Ravīndra Svarūpa Prabhu, His Holiness Bhaktitīrtha Swami, and Her Grace Mālatī Prabhu.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given us the perfect plan to go back to Godhead in this lifetime by providing us with this rare opportunity to serve you. But we are totally unqualified and *sumanda-mataya.* So please hold our hand tight all the time like a father holding his son’s hand while crossing a flooded river, since we tend to go away from you.

Our most dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we would like to always remember you as follows. You are...

- a divine personality who never forgot Kṛṣṇa in his life;
- a divine personality who never did anything for himself;
- a divine personality who had no flaw and never made any mistake or error in judgment while always telling the absolute truth;
- the *jagad-guru* who is an ever well-wisher to everyone.

Again, Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you so much for putting so much nectar in your books.

Your unworthy servants at the Devasadan Mandir, in Detroit, Michigan, USA.  
(written by Jagad Guru dāsa)

## East Hartford

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

We at the Hare Kṛṣṇa Mandir in East Hartford, Connecticut, want to thank you for making all the sacrifices you made in order to establish Kṛṣṇa consciousness throughout the world.

We want to thank you for empowering many of your disciples to continue your mission even after your physical departure. It is unfortunate that some of them have temporarily fallen to the wayside. By your mercy others are coming forward and taking their places in the spiritual army you have created. Wherever you are, Lord Kṛṣṇa is also present, and wherever Lord Kṛṣṇa is, there is certainly victory.

By your mercy, the state of Connecticut has had a temple for the last twenty years. The residents are able to get relief from the pangs of material life, and we thank you for sacrificing so much to establish another oasis in this desert.

There is no way we can repay you for what you have done. We are indebted to you, now and forever, for the sacrifices you have made for us.

We have also made some sacrifices to try to please you, but it is not possible to repay the debt we owe. We pray for the strength to continue your mission of helping the fallen souls. And if we can continue such sacrifice, we will help ourselves from falling to the wayside.

Always praying for your mercy, we remain  
Your servants in East Hartford, Connecticut, USA.  
(written by Pyāri Mohan dāsa)

## Ekaterinburg

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our prostrated obeisances unto the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, for some years I have tried to serve your mission, following your instructions and injunctions, as given to me by my spiritual master: chanting sixteen rounds of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* daily, studying your books every day, preaching to everyone I meet about the teachings of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and *Bhagavad-gītā,* and helping those devotees who spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness most effectively.

Thank you for your books. They contain the highest knowledge, which can give everyone the greatest benediction and true happiness. This knowledge shows us how to get out of the ocean of material existence and gain relief from the distresses it brings.

Thank you for the holy name. By your mercy I have faith that the pure chanting of the holy names will save me from forgetting Kṛṣṇa and from repeated birth and death.

You came to me through your books, which are as brilliant as a million suns. I was in the emptiness of illusory material existence, but you gave me hope and inspiration. I didn’t see any goal to my existence, but your personality struck me to the depths of my heart, and I found the goal of my life.

You are the kind of devotee about whom Kṛṣṇa says:

*kṛpālur akṛta-drohas titikṣuḥ sarva-dehinām*  
*satya-sāro ’navadyātmā samaḥ sarvopakārakaḥ*

**“O Uddhava, a saintly person is merciful and never injures others. Even if others are aggressive he is tolerant and forgiving toward all living entities. His strength and meaning in life come from the truth itself, he is free from all envy and jealousy, and his mind is equal in material happiness and distress. Thus, he dedicates his time to working for the welfare of all others.”**

By your causeless mercy and the mercy of your disciples, I had an opportunity to join the movement of Lord Caitanya, although I had no qualifications to do so.

On this auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā I humbly bow down to your lotus feet and pray to you, my rescuer and the rescuer of the whole world, to let me be your servant and a servant of your servants life after life, and to serve you with love and determination.

Your servants in Ekaterinburg, Russia.  
(written by Icchanātha dāsa)

## Finland

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Our beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi!

I bow down at the lotus feet of all devotees, your noble sons and daughters (who are now guiding us), your grandsons and daughters and all other readers.

Last spring I (along with many other devotees) had an opportunity to visit India. In Vṛndāvana my first inner feeling was “I’m too sinful to be here. I’m a stranger, a great offender. I must immediately leave because I’m contaminating this holy *dhāma*.” It was a devastating experience to my puffed-up brain.

I forced myself to go through the main gate of the Krishna-Balarāma temple, but a quick look to the left stopped me. The Samādhi doors were open and Your Divine Grace was sitting there. I couldn’t pass by. Inside I bowed down and looked up to you. There you were: golden, shiny, loving, compassionate. You looked straight into my very impure heart, and I immediately understood I was welcome. I wasn’t a stranger anymore. You had invited us there, so Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī was favorable to us and permitted us to stay. Overwhelming joy filled my heart: you are there at every step I take. I am a member of your transcendental family. We all are. Actually, this is our home. Our home is at your divine lotus feet.

Who am I to describe the extent of your mercy? It is unlimited and inconceivable.

You taught that *guru* means “heavy.” However, your spiritual weight, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is impossible to measure. The strength of your noble heart is so enormous that you are capturing millions of souls every year by your books.

You have captured us all—a bunch of monkeys, stonehearted Westerners—in the net of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, to be given to Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. And you are telling us what Lord Kṛṣṇa wants: “Yes, just accept Me. Simply chant Hare Kṛṣṇa. I am yours. That’s all.”

*tavaivāsmi tavaivāsmi na jīvāmi tvayā vinā*  
*iti vijñāya devi tvaṁ naya māṁ caraṇāntikam*

**“I am Yours! I am Yours! I cannot live without You! O Rādhe, please understand this and bring me to the service of Your lotus feet.” (*****Śrī Vilāpa-kusumāñjali*** **96)**

You are there in the *rāsa-līlā* with Raghunātha dāsa and other Gosvāmīs. As a matter of fact, you already were there while staying here in Martyaloka with us. Who can understand that? We are only able to humbly repeat every morning:

*nikuñja-yūno rati-keli-siddhyai*  
*yā yālibhir yuktir apekṣaṇīyā*  
*tatrāti-dākṣyād ati-vallabhasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

The remembrance of you is our only hope. You are holding the torch of knowledge so we can some day reach the eternal and never-ending love of Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

We miss you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, although none of us in Finland have seen you with our present eyes, and although we know you will never leave us. You can always be seen in the heart. You are the most confidential servitor of Śrī Kṛṣṇa; therefore we are helplessly turning to you.

We thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your overwhelming love and affection toward us—foolish beginners on the path of *bhakti*. Thank you for letting us stay in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Kindly hear our constant prayer:

*hā hā prabhu koro doyā, deho more pada-chāyā*

We are humbly asking: “Let us surrender. Let us surrender more and more. Let us surrender completely.”

Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura writes, “Have a strong faith that Śrī Kṛṣṇa alone protects you and none else. Admit Him as your only guardian.”

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, showed us the meaning of these words. You are fearless at Lord Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet, so we want to follow you. Ultimately it is you who are leading us. Only by your will can we continue.

Your Divine Grace, please accept us.

Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!

Your grandchildren in Finland.

## Fortaleza

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

The sun,  
when it rises  
on the horizon—  
sovereign,  
powerful,  
bright,  
and wonderful—  
looks like the arrival  
of the spiritual master.

For us,  
Śrīla Prabhupāda  
is the sun  
who brings  
an ocean of light  
that gives us a vision  
of the infinite beauty  
of Kṛṣṇa,  
the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

Now  
in Brazil,  
Fortaleza is yours.  
Here is your home, too.  
And we want to honor you.

All glories  
to Kṛṣṇa’s greatest devotee!  
All glories  
to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your servants in Fortaleza, Brazil.

## Gītā-nāgarī

Beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I dedicate this humble offering to your glorious, loving gaze, to the jewels that are your words, and to eternal shelter at your soft lotus feet. Allow me to abide in these holiest places of pilgrimage, for they are the life of this once-destitute soul.

You have humbled our hearts by your humility  
And caused us to see by your sight.  
O Spirit of Grace, may you bless us  
In the clouds of the darkest night.

The last time I was physically in your presence was the day you came to Gītā Nāgarī in 1975. Three magnificent times our eyes met. Each glance was so complete in its message that it seemed the essence of all things was contained there. Three times my heart was altered—pierced and strengthened in such a momentous way that the memory of your glance is still the nectar by which I progress onward in the service of Kṛṣṇa, always and only by your continued grace.

Back then, we had no official temple building. We lived in an old farmhouse and used the living room as the temple. The last afternoon that I saw you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, all the other devotees were accompanying you on an extended *parikrama*—a tour of the Gītā Nāgarī grounds. I, however, remained in the temple frantically trying to remove every speck of dust and dirt before you arrived for the scheduled *darśana* and lecture. Finally, I quit my labors, scooped up one-year-old Padmāvatī dāsī, and ran out to join the party. Your Divine Grace was just then coming from the barn toward the temple.

On the short run from the kitchen door toward the barn, I hurriedly plucked a flower, handing it to Padmāvatī, who I carried on my arm. A child would always evoke a tender interaction from you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, so she was my emissary. Suddenly, there we were, approaching a face-to-face encounter. You graciously slowed your pace as we neared. I remember how you smiled and appreciatively accepted that meager flower on behalf of the Supreme, as always. Your fatherly, merciful glance of gratitude melted my soul as I backed away and fell to the dirt in obeisance.

The group continued on toward the temple with the leaders close to your side. The women and children were trailing—jockeying for a glimpse and any dear glance from Your Divine Grace. There I stood in the yard, off to one side, as the line wended its way toward the kitchen entrance and, for the second time that day, encountered your compelling glance.

Your look was serious and foreboding; your eyes spoke to my soul as with a caution or a warning of some misguided time to come. What did you see in me then to bring forth such reproach? I’ll never know, but your knowing grace at that moment has always been a reminder to me of the focus and simplicity of our task: to associate with devotees and depend completely on Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. Your staunch faith, Śrīla Prabhupāda, amidst the distractions and confusion of Kali-yuga fortifies my determination. As the years go by, I am more and more thankful for the severity of that look. While unsettling at the time, it has given me untold strength and solace through these long years.

When finally Your Divine Grace entered the temple through the kitchen that I had so meticulously cleaned, I noticed a cupboard door standing wide open. This open door was a dismaying sight to me on that day, and it remains in my mind as a symbol of this world of imperfection and of our need to give up control.

I was in the rear of the procession following you into the temple. As you exited the kitchen, you stepped into a tiny office off to the right. We filed into the temple room from a short hall that separated it from the office. You waited in the office as we filed in. As I came in last, I was at the end of the temple room across from where you sat. *Kīrtana* was underway. I swayed, one arm raised to Kṛṣṇa while holding Padmāvatī on the other. She followed suit, bouncing with a tiny arm raised up to Kṛṣṇa. Once again, your eyes penetrated my soul. Your glorious smile, beaming approval and encouragement, held me captive. My heart sang the refrain you had spoken to my husband, Yadunandana dāsa, when we were new devotees: “Everything is all right. Everything is all right. Everything is all right.” Ever since, when I need encouragement, I glance into my heart for reassurance, and there you are, smiling lovingly upon us.

The many uplifting books you have translated and written continue to inspire us; the memory of your transcendent nature calls out to us to fulfill our higher purpose. I am reminded of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, every time I see your qualities manifested in the devotees here at Gītā Nāgarī. We carry your words, your smile, your gaze, and your boundless love for Kṛṣṇa in our minds and hearts. The intensity of our longing for you is thereby somewhat pacified. Now the devotees here have facilitated my ability to carry out your wishes of so many years ago when you directed me to write for Kṛṣṇa. Please accept this imperfect attempt to glorify you, the most humble and perfect servant of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Dāmodara.

Beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
You cut the knot of our illusion,  
Free us from the bonds of sense perception.

From your garden of devotion  
You see into our heart of hearts.  
You call us out...  
Come my children. Come and sing  
Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa,  
Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare.  
Sing His glories; chant His name.  
In Him alone rests everything.  
Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma,  
Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare.  
Come, my children.  
Come and sing of Lord Kṛṣṇa,  
Head of our original family.  
Sing of the source of all other sources,  
The wisest of the very wise.  
His boundless beauty, His names, His forms—  
Who can in all the world surmise?  
Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
You walk mighty as the lion,  
Sustaining essence in our lives.  
We love you tender as a child,  
Sweet perfection of our eyes.

Your gaze binds and holds us.  
Your love permeates through and through.  
Behold the magnificence of His mercy:  
Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s gift to us is you.

You uncover the key to unlock the door:  
Sing Hare Kṛṣṇa Hare Kṛṣṇa evermore.

Gratefully aspiring to serve you always,  
Your servants at Gītā-nāgarī, Pennsylvania, USA.  
(written by Tarunī-devī dāsī)

## Gītā-nāgarī Baru

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet on this most auspicious day of Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā.

You have stated that half of your mission was to publish and distribute transcendental literatures in all languages of the world, and the other half was to establish *varṇāśrama-dharma*, specifically through establishing Kṛṣṇa conscious farming communities based on simple living and high thinking, to practically demonstrate the philosophy contained in your books. So we are out here in the wilderness of North Lampung with Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, one bull called Dharma, and several devotees, trying to set up one such farming community for your pleasure. Our goal is to set up a Kṛṣṇa conscious village, which we have named Gītā Nāgarī Baru (“New Gītā Nāgarī”), after the essays you wrote many decades ago on the idea of a village where the *Bhagavad-gītā* was sung.

We have understood that you wanted the people of the world to take up a more simple and sane way of life, depending on Lord Kṛṣṇa, the land, and the cows. Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself lived in that way. You started some efforts in this direction during your manifest presence in this world. So we followers of your followers are trying to please you by carrying on in this way, especially on this holy day of your appearance in this world. Please bless us that our efforts may be pleasing to you, our honored Parama Gurudeva, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your aspiring servants at the Gītā-nāgarī Baru Farm Community, Menggala, North Lampung, Indonesia.

## Guadalajara

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humbles obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to your *saṅkīrtana* movement, the greatest benediction for all living beings!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are trying to serve you by preaching through distribution of your transcendental literature. We depend exclusively on your causeless mercy, and we pray to you for purity and potency so that we can perform this service and increase book distribution.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are very fortunate because you visited Mexico City twice and we received your mercy by seeing you face to face. That is very rare in this age of Kali, because by being in the association of a pure devote one can obtain all perfections. But we are very dull-headed and don’t follow your orders because we are very attached to sense gratification. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, keep us under your lotus feet and don’t stop helping us exit this material world.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, all glories to the day of your appearance in this world!

Begging to be eternal humble servants at your lotus feet, we remain

Your servants in Guadalajara, Mexico.  
(written by Premānanda dāsa)

## Hong Kong

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you! All glories to your mercy! And all glories to ISKCON and its work, rescuing all in this most difficult of ages!

Everything goes through different phases, and so does the movement of time. The universal time cycle moves from the Golden Age to the Silver Age to the Bronze Age, and finally to the Iron Age of Kali. With the progress of the Age of Kali, all the good qualities decline and irreligion increases. The Age of Kali is both a benediction and a curse. It is a benediction because by taking shelter of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* anyone can easily become free from the sufferings of this world, and it is a curse because it is not so easy to take shelter of the holy name in this age, what with so many obstacles and distractions. The *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* has this to say about the quality of people in this age: “In the Kali-yuga the following things will diminish day by day: religion, truthfulness, cleanliness, mercy, physical strength, memory, and duration of life.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 12.2.1)

Although it is said that religion, along with all other good qualities, will decline, we also observe a contradictory trend: a movement toward natural life, environmental awareness, vegetarianism, and health consciousness. There is also more awareness of reincarnation and the afterlife, astral travel, mystic powers, and so on. A lot of these “New Age” aspects can be attributed to the large-scale preaching of Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement. Your part in this great movement shines brightest, for you worked most magnificently to fulfill the desires of the previous *ācāryas* and the Lord. The result of spiritual work is not always physically visible, for the vast majority of conditioned entities do not appreciate the value of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Therefore, considering the number of people in the world, we devotees of Kṛṣṇa appear very few. We are confident, however, that the effect of the Lord’s mercy is gradually penetrating society at large. In general we can say that most people still find it beyond their ability to comprehend the subtle spiritual value of pure devotion that we are offering.

Our work in Hong Kong is most difficult. It is an extraordinary commercial civilization with no natural resources—business around the clock! Those who have made something for themselves aspire for more and more, or try to keep up with the Joneses. Many people have no choice but to toil incessantly simply to survive. They work always just to meet their gross bodily needs, so material gain and sense gratification comprise the happiness that keeps them going. Recreation is a luxury and spiritual life does not exist. Very few are situated nicely, and since they lack any self-satisfaction they are generally poor candidates for spiritual life. Alas, O Lord, what a soul-killing civilization! You mentioned the ill effects of this kind of civilization on your arrival in New York in 1965. Therefore, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we really need your unlimited mercy for extending your blessings and the blessings of Lord Kṛṣṇa to the unfortunate rich and poor souls of the Orient. Please cast your merciful glance upon the Orient, for the compassion of the Vaiṣṇavas attracts the compassion of the Lord. By the grace of the Lord and His servants, everything is possible, according to the following verse of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (2.4.18):

*kirāta-hūṇāndhra-pulinda-pulkaśā*  
*ābhīra-śumbhā yavanāḥ khasādayaḥ*  
*ye ’nye ca pāpā yad-apāśrayāśrayāḥ*  
*śudhyanti tasmai prabhaviṣṇave namaḥ*

**“Kirātas, Hūṇas, Āndhras, Pulindas, Pulkaśas, Ābhīras, Śumbhas, Yavanas, members of the Khasa races, and even others addicted to sinful acts can be purified by taking shelter of the devotees of the Lord, due to His being the supreme power. I beg to offer my respectful obeisances unto Him.”**

Praying for your special mercy in this new millennium, we remain  
Your servants in Hong Kong.

## Ibadan

Dear grandfather Śrīla Prabhupāda,

I offer you all my respectful obeisances, which are not worthy to offer. But please accept them from me.

Grandfather, I am thinking of all you have done for my life. First you built a house in which the whole world can live, and today I am one of the extremely fortunate people living in this house. If this were all you had done for my life, then I would see myself as the most fortunate person in the world.

But building the ISKCON house is not all you have done for me. You have also fathered a son who has become my father, and he has initiated me into the chanting of the holy name and made me your grandson.

So I thank you so much for all you have done for my life. The instructions you left with us make it seem as if you had not left us. I pray that I never lose my attraction for these instructions. I also pray that you protect my spiritual life so that I will not fall away from this mission.

Please continue to bless me so that I can control myself and maintain my spiritual life.

Your servants in Ibadan, Nigeria.  
(written by Yadunātha dāsa)

## Japan

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda

Please accept our most humble obeisances at the dust of your golden lotus feet. All glories to your divine personality, which attracts the most fallen souls to your lotus feet and saves them from the blazing fire of this material existence.

One should try to keep himself satisfied in any condition of life—whether distress or happiness—which is offered by the supreme will. A person who endures in this way is able to cross over the darkness of nescience very easily. \[Nārada Muni to Dhruva at *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.8.27\]

Since 1999, ISKCON in Japan has had a hard time. Even in the difficult situation, however, many devotees have kept on steadily preaching. And your intimate associates gave us inspired association. Without this association, we would not have been able to continue to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We have two good-size temples in Tokyo and a small one in Osaka, run by a single *gṛhastha* couple. All they can do is put on a feast every Saturday. But even with so few devotees, they are keeping this program going every week. And because of their sincere endeavor, you send them such nice guests. One boy who went to his friend’s house to help his friend move found your *Bhagavad-gītā* there. He read it and was so appreciative that he bought it from his friend. Then he become a devotee—he shaved his head and began wearing *dhotīs* and chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa. If we just continue to preach, Kṛṣṇa will bring nice people, because your books are everywhere in Japan.

In this Age of Kali, out of envy and anger all the people fight with one another and cheat one another. They can’t make true friendships. They want true relationships with one another, but it’s very difficult make them. Even ISKON is feeling the effect Kali-yuga. My Guru Mahārāja said, “We have to learn how to improve our relationships. This is very much lacking in ISKCON. We need to cultivate true friendship among devotees, in which we can express love and trust and show affection to Vaiṣṇavas. We have to actually care for the Vaiṣṇavas. We all want to have proper relationships among Godbrothers and Godsisters. But we complain that we have no time. We should not fill up our time with so many duties and simply work, work, work until we collapse—and in the process neglect other Vaiṣṇavas.”

When you were on the planet, Śrīla Prabhupāda, all the devotees in your Society felt loving affection from you. They automatically wanted to cooperate. And they were fully absorbed in serving you. They could make impossible things become possible. This was all due to your purity. Every soul wants such loving relationships in this world. We are a very small number of devotees in Japan, but we still want to create such loving relationships within our preaching team. If this loving atmosphere is created among devotees, anyone who comes in contact with them will feel the loving mood. Everybody is looking for such loving relationships. Gradually we can see that this world is becoming more and more hellish. People can find shelter only in this movement. This is actually the meaning of this movement. The movement should be like a fort where people can come to take shelter. To make our temples like this we need to train nice, fixed-up devotees with good character and purity.

Śrīla Prabhupāda! Now we don’t have such purity and good qualities and deep loving relationships. You said, “I wish to leave some trained preachers so that they can do the work of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the Western world. That is my ambition.” So we beg you, Please kindly gave us the association of such trained preachers more and more. By their association we can develop good character and purity. As said in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (3.25.25), “The spiritually powerful message of Godhead can be properly discussed only in a society of devotees, and it is greatly pleasing to hear in that association. If one hears from devotees, the way of transcendental experience quickly opens, and gradually one attains firm faith that in due course develops into attraction and devotion.”

The aspiring servants of your servants in Japan.

## Johannesburg

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our prostrated *daṇḍavats* at your divine lotus feet. All glories to you!

On this auspicious occasion of your first Vyāsa-pūjā celebration in the twenty-first century, it seems to be the most appropriate time for some individual and collective stock-taking.

Lords Gaurasundara and Nityānanda Rāma have broken open the treasure house of *kṛṣṇa-prema* in Goloka Vṛndāvana, and you have plundered and distributed that *dhana* without any considerations throughout the three worlds. The potency of your mercy is being felt every single day in different corners of the globe through the unparalleled distribution of your Vaiṣṇava literatures, *prasādam,* and the supremely sweet holy names of Kṛṣṇa.

Recently at an international music festival held here in Johannesburg, a group of people came to our *prasādam* stall and declared boldly, “This food is the best in the whole universe!” Everyone present agreed between bites of *samosas* and *halavā*.

A couple, students in our Bhakti Sadacara Certificate course at our Bhaktivedanta College, just informed me that on their way home from one of our evening classes they silently drove home in a stunned daze at the wealth of knowledge they were imbibing from a systematic study of your classic *Science of Self-Realization*.

And when we recorded excerpts from your *Light of the Bhāgavat,* which were broadcast once a week for four weeks on national radio, the presenter, a devout Christian, could not help marveling at what he was hearing.

In a post-apartheid South Africa, still sensitive on race issues, people keep on asking us, “How do you get all the different races to live and work together so nicely like this in your movement?” We tell them, “That is the proof of the spiritual potency of what our founder-*ācārya* has brought to the world. He has built a house the whole world can truly live in.”

The only slight hitch, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is in us, we who are meant to be your followers and representatives. We have fallen short of this exalted position. In many cases we have misrepresented you to such an extent that to an outsider we may appear slightly schizophrenic. You know of all these cases only too well, and so there is no need to repeat them on this auspicious day.

Those who are wise have recounted that when the demigods and demons were churning the ocean of milk, many, many substances came out, including poison, before the nectar was finally delivered. In the same way, the poison in the *saṅkīrtana* movement is being extracted by your arrangement. Those who patiently stick to the dust of your lotus feet will be the fortunate souls to receive the *amṛta* of Lord Gaurāṅga, which is scheduled to saturate the three worlds for the next ten thousand years.

We are simply crying out to you, “O spiritual master of all three worlds! Please give us the benediction that we can be rid of the twin enemies of the aspiring *sādhakas*—sense gratification and false ego. Then we can purely serve your servants and be eligible to one day chant the *śuddha-nāma* without interruption.”

Your grandchildren at ISKCON Johannesburg, South Africa.  
(written by Ātmārāma dāsa)

## Kiev

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble respectful obeisances at your divine lotus feet—*bando mui sāvadhāna māte*. Let your fame be spread all over the three worlds—*ebe jaśa ghuśuk tribhuvana*!

When we think of you we feel ourselves like helpless children, sentenced to everlasting imprisonment in the material world. And it is only by your causeless mercy that we now have hope that we will be delivered from this world and go back to the eternal, bliss life of pure devotional service to the Divine Couple.

All of us—dwellers of Western world—were like small children being tightly swaddled in many-colored napkins woven by skillful weavers—the three modes of material nature. We had been soundly sleeping, lulled by the sweet voice of the bewitching illusory energy of the Lord. But you, Your Divine Grace, have brought us the call of the *paramparā:*

*jīv jāgo, jīv jāgo, gauracānda bole*  
*kota nidrā jāo māyā-piśācīra kole*

**“Lord Gaurāṅga is calling, ‘Wake up, sleeping souls! Wake up, sleeping souls! You have slept so long in the lap of the witch Māyā.’”**

Owing to your exceptional purity and devotion to Kṛṣṇa, this appeal was so powerful that it could pass through the Iron Curtain of the former USSR. And we roused ourselves, awaking and making efforts to open our eyes. Then your books came:

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Thus you helped us to see...

Your transcendental books are filled with invaluable instructions; through them you help us take literally every step in our spiritual lives. You have taught us what we cannot do, what we can do, and what we should do.
