You have carefully divided every day of our lives, from the moment of waking up to the moment of going to bed: bath, *maṅgala-ārati, japa* time, *guru-pūjā, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class, *prasādam,* practical service, *prasādam, gaura-ārati, Bhagavad-gītā* class, reading the books, sleeping. You taught us how to accept a spiritual master and serve him, how to worship the Deities, how to give talks, how to cook, how to eat, how to treat our body. Just as a caring, loving father guards every step of his child, you, our dearest spiritual master, have guided all of your followers solicitously, pointing at the aim and warning about the dangers. There is nothing insignificant in your instructions; every detail is exceptionally important. And if we are able to exactly follow everything without deviation, then we are guaranteed to obtain spiritual happiness and achieve the highest purpose of life.

You left behind invaluable treasures—your disciples, our spiritual masters. Being your faithful followers, they take us along the path shown by you, cutting the knots of our attachments to the material world and cultivating the *bhakti-latā* creeper in our hearts with care.

You are an ideal *ācārya,* one who teaches not only by his instructions but by his own example as well. You said that various obstacles you met on your way became your possessions. They are our possessions as well, because when we meet with difficulties we recall how you were going through different tests with firm determination and unflinching faith in the Supreme Lord. We derive great inspiration and strength from your example. What can *māyā* have in store for us that you did not experience in your life? Lack of understanding and estrangement from our relatives, impecuniousness, struggling alone, cold, living amidst materialists, no devotees beside you, illness, death—all of this happened in your life, and throughout all these periods you remained an ideal example of a pure servant of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

By giving the world ISKCON you built a house in which everyone can happily reside. You taught us how to keep this house clean and maintain the spiritual atmosphere of friendliness, compassion, and cooperation. “Your love for me will manifest in your ability to cooperate.”

You gave shelter to a bewildered civilization by showing the way of salvation, and now, owing to your boundless compassion, we have real hope for deliverance.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, there are so many things that may be said about you—about the depth and magnitude of your heritage. Please forgive these helpless children for not being able to properly describe the greatness of their spiritual father.

Just as a beautiful diamond is kept in a beautifully trimmed box, so the greatness of Lord Kṛṣṇa is kept in the precious jeweled box of the mouths of Lord Ananta. (*Caitanya-Bhāgavata, Ādi-khaṇḍa* 1.13)

We believe that Lord Kṛṣṇa will manifest the precious jeweled box that will be worthy of the brilliance of your greatness, the greatness of His pure servant.

We humbly beg you to accept our sincere deep gratitude for the causeless mercy you have bestowed upon us. *Prati-janme kori āśā caraṇera dhūli:* we desire the dust of your holy feet in every birth we may take.

Humbly and with great reverence we bow down at your lotus feet.

Your eternal servants in Kiev, Ukraine.

## Kwa-Zulu Natal

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

You very kindly came to South Africa in 1975. Thank you very much for coming here. It really means a lot to us.

This is a very fertile field for preaching. The people here are generally pious. They often greet the devotees by saying, “Hare Kṛṣṇa,” or “Hari Bol.” Thousands of people come to our temples and our festivals. Our congregation here is very large and enthusiastic, and contains many sincere and wonderful Vaiṣṇavas. There are approximately 240 initiated devotees in Kwa-Zulu Natal alone. Many others are chanting sixteen rounds a day and following the four regulative principles. We have initiated a Shiksha Program for recognizing and encouraging the devotees at various levels of commitment in following the principles of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Every week throughout the province, about four hundred persons attend Bhaktivedanta College courses, where they systematically and progressively study your books. There are about five thousand subscribers to your *Back to Godhead* magazine in South Africa. We also maintain four weekly Bhakti Yoga Society meetings in universities and other tertiary institutions around Durban. In addition, there are twenty-seven weekly Kṛṣṇa conscious services carried on around Kwa-Zulu Natal. These are largely maintained by our congregation. The preaching is going on in all of the various cultural communities here.

Our Food for Life program here is one of the largest in the world. In 1999 Food for Life distributed 260,000 plates of *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* at festivals, in schools, and at squatter camps and townships. The politicians and the people in general especially appreciate this program. The former president and Nobel Peace Prize laureate Dr. Nelson Mandela praised the Hare Kṛṣṇa Food for Life program on prime-time national television. When he visited our temple in Chatsworth, Durban, he offered you his humble obeisances by bowing down on the floor in front of your *mūrti*. The current president, Mr. Thabo Mbeki, attended our Ratha-yātrā festival several years ago and blissfully distributed *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* to the festival-goers.

Our temple in Chatsworth, Durban, is an architectural masterpiece, a local landmark and tourist attraction. It is one of ISKCON’s few purpose-built temples outside India. The street in front of the temple is officially named the Bhaktivedanta Swami Circle. The Chatsworth temple is in one of the most strategic preaching locations in the country, Durban’s southern suburbs. Another ambitious temple project is coming up in the northern suburbs, in Phoenix. Already about 300 people are regularly attending the Sunday program at the Phoenix site, even though it is still under construction. Another important project is coming up in Pietermaritzburg, about an hour’s drive from Durban.

Our four-day Durban Ratha-yātrā festival is one of the largest in the world. It is held over the Easter weekend at the beachfront and attracts many thousands of people. It is a very important annual preaching event, and is attended by many of ISKCON’s senior preachers and *sannyāsīs*.

All in all, ISKCON is very much flourishing and growing in South Africa. We are very grateful to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Where would we be without you? Due to your mercy, we are now on the path of reawakening our loving relationship with Kṛṣṇa. Without you we would have continued to rot in the material world, finding a little bit of happiness but oh so much suffering. Thank you for showing us the way out of this dark world, and for teaching us the process for going back to Godhead. The method you have given us is so pleasing: chanting the sweet holy names; worshiping the Deities; honoring Kṛṣṇa’s *prasādam;* associating with Kṛṣṇa’s devotees; and hearing *kṛṣṇa-kathā*. Gradually our hearts and minds are becoming purified and we are appreciating these things more and more. The more we become purified, the more we appreciate what you have done for us. Now our lives are based upon your instructions and have become meaningful and successful.

There are many varieties of cultures on the continents of this planet. You have given us the Vedic culture, which is meant for training us in spiritual realization and Kṛṣṇa consciousness. It is the highest culture because it trains us in attaining the ultimate goal of life, *kṛṣṇa-prema*. Thank you very much.

We are eternally indebted to you and beg to remain,  
Your servants in Kwa-Zulu Natal, South Africa.  
(written by Rāmānujācārya dāsa)

## London (central)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances and please forgive all our offenses to you. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

“There are many saintly persons,” Prahlāda Mahārāja tells Lord Nṛsiṁha-deva (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 7.9.44), “who are interested only in their own deliverance. They don’t care for the big cities and towns and are not interested in delivering others.”

Fortunately for the inhabitants of this planet, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you’re a saintly person far different from that. You’re the kind that Prahlāda Mahārāja goes on to describe that he himself is: “I do not wish to be liberated alone, leaving aside all these poor fools and rascals, since I know that without Kṛṣṇa consciousness they cannot be happy. I want to bring them back to shelter at Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet.”

You’re *that* kind of saintly person, Śrīla Prabhupāda¾the kind who wants to be right in the midst of the people. Out of compassion, you want to deliver to them, as many as possible, the sublime message of Lord Caitanya. That means right down in the city centers¾the centers of big, dirty, lusty cities, cities such as London. And that’s why *we’re* here in London¾to help you. And that’s why we want to stay.

After all, London is one of the two most important cities in the world. The other is New York. And what you have to say about New York as a big city can also be applied to London. In 1967 you wrote to Gargamuni Prabhu: “I do not agree with your wife’s statement that New York is unfit for human habitation....A Krishna Conscious person is always satisfied, whether in Vaikuntha or in hell.”

And hell this is. Once you were asked in a television interview, “What is your idea of hell?”

Your reply: “London is hell.”

It’s noisy here; the temple’s right on the street in the middle of all the materialistic action, cars and trucks and motorcycles roaring past all day and all night, and police and ambulance sirens wailing. There’s a pub on our left-hand side and a disco next to that, open almost until the time we’re having *maṅgala ārotika*.

On our right side is another pub, a popular stop, especially for homosexuals. In the summer, the drunk and lusty clientele spill out into the street¾as many as two hundred at a time¾chattering, laughing, and yelling in outrageous, ghoulish celebration of all that is twisted and evil. Just past the pub, in Soho Square, people are almost having sex right out in the open in the middle of the afternoon. On the weekends, in addition to the noise of the traffic, people are screaming and yelling in the street like banshees all night long. Our *āśramas* are not soundproof, so trying to sleep at night is like trying to sleep in the middle of a small war.

But, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you wrote,

My Guru Maharaja never liked to open a branch preaching center in a place where there is less population. We are not meant for living peacefully in a secluded place. We are meant for recruiting Krishna’s eternal servants and therefore a better populated place. \[Letter to Subāla dāsa, 16 December 1967\]

And, “My Guru Maharaja wanted us to open our centers in the most congested parts of major cities. We do not want...a quiet and solitary place.” (Letter to Jayapatāka Swami, November 10th, 1970)

So, following in your footsteps and the footsteps of Prahlāda Mahārāja, we are happy to stay right here in the midst of this horrific city, with the fullest opportunity to take shelter of Kṛṣṇa and to serve you. We are happy to walk out the door and immediately find a ready-made market of tens of thousands of people for our daily *harināmas* and book distribution, as well as regular *prasādam* distribution. And of course every day people are coming in to see the temple and to eat in Govinda’s Restaurant.

We are happy to stay here in hell and follow you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, according to your instructions¾to help you in your mission to please your Guru Mahārāja in reclaiming the materially conditioned souls back home, back to Godhead.

All glories to you!

Your servants in central London, UK.

## London (south)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, who fearlessly traveled throughout the world to spread the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement!

It is absolutely amazing that, to save materialistic people like us, an elderly gentleman who had never left his country before decided to travel at the age of 69 and go to a place considered by pure spiritualists to be one of the most abominable in the world, a lonely place where he had no friends or relatives. At an age when most people would consider retiring, he started a movement that would save the world. He wrote over seventy books, traveled around the world eleven times, opened 108 temples, initiated 5,000 devotees, and influenced countless other souls wandering in this world. All within a period of 11 years. How absolutely amazing.

Your transcendental appearance in this world is a benediction given to us by Lord Kṛṣṇa. The Lord wanted to glorify His pure devotee throughout the world, but by spreading the holy name all over the world you managed to glorify the Lord. As a true disciple of a bona fide spiritual master, you attributed all your success to Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Goswami Prabhupāda. Your faith in him and the Lord is unfathomable. When you first arrived in the US you mentioned to one stranger that you had many temples and disciples, and that only time was separating them from manifestation.

We are in a position similar to that of Arjuna, who said to Lord Kṛṣṇa:

*kārpaṇya-doṣopahata-svabhāvaḥ*  
*pṛcchāmi tvāṁ dharma-sammūḍha-cetāḥ*  
*yac chreyaḥ syān niścitaṁ brūhi tan me*  
*śiṣyas te ’haṁ śādhi māṁ tvāṁ prapannam*

**“Now I am confused about my duty and have lost all composure because of miserly weakness. In this condition I am asking You to tell me for certain what is best for me. Now I am Your disciple, and a soul surrendered unto You. Please instruct me.” (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **2.7)**

We have lost our relationship with the Lord and thus are confused about our duty. In this condition we desire to take shelter of your mature and devoted disciples, as well as your glorious books, to guide us back to our original constitutional position.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your tireless effort in bringing Kṛṣṇa consciousness to us. This is a priceless gift for which we will not be able to repay you. Please accept whatever tiny service we are trying to offer you.

On this occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā, we seek your blessings so that we can continue to serve you and your divine movement to the best of our abilities.

Your grand-disciples in south London, UK.

## Madrid

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

Here in Madrid, we continue with your preaching mission in your small and dynamic temple. By your mercy, several preaching activities are going on, and we feel very happy to offer all of them for your pleasure on this most auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā. The main activities going on regularly in the temple are the distribution of your books and *prasādam, harināma-saṅkīrtana,* Deity worship, educational courses in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, congregational preaching, and other public programs such as conferences and festivals. Please accept the little results we have achieved as our humble offering at your lotus feet.

We are dreaming about doing something much bigger here in Madrid. Please bless us so that our dreams come true in a very short time for the pleasure of all the *ācāryas* in the disciplic succession.

Your humble servants in Madrid, Spain.

## Manchester

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Then spangling dark night’s velvet cloak,  
in time and place divinely meant,  
stood stars in silent lines that spoke  
how here is love incarnate sent.

Marking the all-auspicious hour  
that for an age shall yet be marked,  
predicting that the purest power  
has here with us to earth embarked.

Speak, mystic seer of the skies,  
that we the wretched joyful be,  
how one free from all hate and lies  
has come to lead humanity.

And all you gods, let cry with might,  
that all the worlds may wakened be,  
to gaze with wonder on the sight  
of man’s departing misery.

Celestial drums reverberate,  
as love displays its causelessness,  
to brilliantly illuminate  
a world of darkness and distress.

While we who would devoted be  
offer our praise in harmony  
and, showing its sincerity,  
expand our number endlessly.

Today forgiveness does descend  
to walk among us: Gurudeva.  
The thraldom of transgression ends  
in freedom that you freely gave.

A day when every anguished cry,  
in hopeful and heartfelt prayer,  
receives reciprocal reply  
by mercy born of boundless care.

Until this day shall be proclaimed  
in every quarter of the earth,  
and every man shall know your name,  
we yet shall take repeated birth.

To forge your mission far and wide—  
let that be all that holds the heart.  
Our passion and our only pride:  
a loving message to impart.

The legend of a long lost land  
made real today, as you have shown  
how they who take your outstretched hand  
shall find it, our forgotten home.

Lead on then, lord, and let us be  
a little part of that long line  
that heads toward eternity,  
beyond this shackled space and time.

O Prabhupāda, we can but pray  
that you might throw your sidelong glance  
on us, and thus, by grace we may  
enter the everlasting dance.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you!

Without any doubt your appearance has begun a revolution. It is a revolt against atheism and ignorance. A swelling undercurrent rising up against the tidal wave of madness that threatens to engulf the world. Thanks to the so-called advancement of modern society, a steady and increasing stream of people are going straight to hell. Gradually all morality is being abandoned, along with any belief in God, or at least in a God who would like us to behave ourselves. Either there is no God, God is dead, or He has forsaken this world forever. Scientists, philosophers, and politicians arrogantly posit their atheistic theories, putting forward absurd ideas about how the increasing problems of the world—problems born of atheism in the first place—can be solved without any reference to God. Godless so-called religions like Buddhism and various other impersonalistic beliefs are burgeoning. And, acting as the harbingers and heralds of all this insanity, the media and entertainment industries constantly and energetically promote their cynical message of “pleasure at any damn cost.”

But you know all this well enough, Śrīla Prabhupāda. It was the reason you came here. To begin a transcendental insurgency movement. You described it yourself in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam:* “...a revolution in the impious lives of this world’s misdirected civilization.” I guess that sums up your mission—and the mission of us who would be your followers—to create that revolution. Surely it is the only real hope for this Kali-stricken world. But it is a very real hope. The “transcendental words” which are meant to bring about the revolution are extremely powerful. Like brilliant sun rays dissipating a morning mist, they cut through the thick veil of ignorance that shrouds the world. We see ourselves as the perfect examples. Before hearing from you we believed that we were advanced and highly civilized. But now we know the truth. Condemned, unfortunate. Mad. Blindly following the blind. Two-legged animals. All godless rascals. And now we have to convey that truth to an unsuspecting world.

We are convinced that if we simply transparently present your messages then the revolution will really start taking off. To any honest person it is all so obvious. Every time we feel ouselves being dragged down by the ignorance all around us, we listen again to your sublimely simple words. It is a transcendental “fix” for the mind. The lights go on. A divine power is flowing through you, without any doubt at all. But now we have to make ourselves clear conduits for that power. That’s all. If we can somehow just purely represent your instructions to the world, then we will surely see wonderful things happening.

However, although it is simple, it is not easy. Becoming pure ourselves is the first big revolution required. A revolution in our own hearts. Do we really want to give up sense gratification? Can we? Sometimes it seems almost impossible. But by your grace we know it is not. We just have to plug into your words again and again. Go on hearing—*naṣṭa-prāyeṣv abhadreṣu—*and constantly chant the holy name. Gradually, as our own ignorance is thereby destroyed and we become clear *via media* to you, we will be able to play some part in your mission.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you once said that society will one day look upon the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement as a world savior. To us that seems to be already the case. *Karmāṇi nirdahati kintu ca bhakti-bhājām*. We are convinced that, although the world situation is grim, it would have been far grimmer if you had not begun your mission. But there is no room for any complacency. That tidal wave is still rising and our undercurrent seems puny by comparison. Hell is still claiming countless souls as they boldly and bravely advance towards death.

Let us therefore become an instrument in your revolutionary mission, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Help us to always remember that we are dependent upon your mercy. Shower us with that mercy so that we may become humbler than the grass. Invest us with the power to show proper honor to all living beings, remembering that they are all dear to Kṛṣṇa. We are low, fallen, and without any spiritual strength or intelligence. You are our only hope, and we pray to you today. Please engage us in your service. Otherwise life has no meaning for us.

Your servants at ISKCON Manchester, United Kingdom.  
(written by Kṛṣṇa Dharma dāsa)

## Markham

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

We pray to remain your servants eternally.

Your servants at ISKCON Markham, Canada.

## Melbourne

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most respectful obeisances on the most auspicious day of your appearance.

The year 2000 is a very special year for the devotees of the Melbourne Mahāprabhu Mandir. This year represents our silver jubilee year. Twenty-five years ago Your Divine Grace established this temple in Melbourne, and you also established large brass Deities of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi. You established the Melbourne Mahāprabhu Mandir in 1975, and to this day it remains the only *tīrtha* in the southern hemisphere to be blessed by your mercy. As you said in your 1974 Melbourne lecture:

**Prabhupāda:** If you want to remain in the spiritual world, this temple is the spiritual world. We are not living in Melbourne. This temple is not Melbourne. It is Vaikuṇṭha.

**Devotees:** *Jaya!*

**Prabhupāda:** It is Vṛndāvana. So if you stick to this temple service, Kṛṣṇa’s service, then you are not in this material world. This is your position.

To this day, devotees of Melbourne Mahāprabhu Mandir have maintained the high standard of Deity worship established by you. Anyone who steps into the temple cannot help but feel the transcendental atmosphere of the spiritual world, Vṛndāvana.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, despite your advanced age, you traveled around the world fourteen times, making sure that the Kṛṣṇa conscious centers you established continue to function into the future. You trained the devotees so expertly.

In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya-līlā* 7.128, you wrote:

This is the sublime mission of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. Many people come and inquire whether they have to give up family life to join the Society, but that is not our mission. One can remain comfortably in his residence. We simply request everyone to chant the *mahā-mantra:* Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare. If one is a little literate and can read *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* and *Śrīmad*- *Bhāgavatam*, that is so much the better.

Here in Melbourne the congregation has grown from a few hundred families in the 1980’s to nearly four thousand families today. This is by your mercy and the mercy of your devotees. These members of the congregation are now helping the temple to expand the preaching by distributing *prasādam* and books and the holy name of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa and celebrating many nice, big festivals. All this is happening only by your blessings. Despite all the difficulties, devotees are working together co-operatively because of their love for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

In Melbourne (May 1975) you invited every hungry man to come and take *prasādam:* “Oh, yes, oh, yes. Everyone, we \[are\] open to everyone. You come and take *prasādam*.”

To date devotees have distributed more than three million plates of *prasādam* in Melbourne. Guests have remarked that whenever they come, they always get *prasādam*. This is your opulence, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

We do not know how to thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Our only hope is to try to follow your orders. That way, by pleasing you, we can hope to please Kṛṣṇa and thus make our life successful.

Your indebted servants of the Melbourne Mahāprabhu Mandir, Melbourne, Australia.  
(written by Vijaya Gopīkeśa dāsa)

## Mexico City

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humbles obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

This year it’s my turn to write an homage glorifying you on your all-auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā. I am not the best person to do it, being fully unqualified and incompetent, but I can’t find anyone to replace me. There are no initiated disciples of yours around to perform this noble duty. Where are they? Did you not give us everything we need to be happy in this material world? Did we really realize the great treasure you gave us? Such a great treasure that we cannot repay you for many lifetimes? You gave us everything. Our illusion is so strong that we do not even recognize the transcendental saint who came from so far, alone, to save us from all dangers. Is it so difficult to follow you, Śrīla Prabhupāda? We do not want to be victimized also and lose the opportunity to walk with you in this blessed way of life.

You undoubtedly have all the potency to save us from spiritual death. You possess and give us full enthusiasm for preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Nothing was neglected by you. But the problem is the lack of one ingredient, one element: desire.

We should not want to wait for the mercy to come from heaven, like some kind of mystical power or enchantment. No, we should not adopt a passive approach. We should rather seek with intensity that which you tried to give us, love for Kṛṣṇa. Only by increasing our intensity can we attract your attention. In *Light of the Bhāgavata* you write in your commentary on picture 43: “A devotee, therefore, should execute his devotional services with full energy, endurance, and confidence. He should perform his scheduled duties, he should be pure in heart, and he should serve in association with devotees. All six of these items will lead the devotee to the path of success.” Have we lost faith in your words?

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we believe strongly that if we are sincere you will surely give us the strength to continue on the path of devotional service. Let us continue to associate with your faithful followers, even if they are simple devotees. Let us strive to fulfill your mission among devotees who have tolerance, compassion, and comprehension of your teachings. Please let us avoid the tragedy of devotees fighting among themselves in the name of serving your Lord, Kṛṣṇa. Let us understand truly, really, and deeply that we are a tiny spark of spirit whose home is the spiritual world. We are not interested in big external projects, building big false egos, monuments to the fake glories of a temporary world. We are interested in elevating our souls to the platform of service to you and Śrī Govinda.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for all the gifts you brought from the spiritual world. Thank you for your sweet and soft appearance among us.

Your aspiring servants at Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Madana Gopāla Mandir, Mexico City, Mexico.  
(written by Harikathā dāsa)

## Montreal

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most respectful and humble obeisances at the dust of your divine lotus feet.

Mistakes we have done, mistakes we will do, because no one is perfect like you, Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

Some people are unhappy, others remain optimistic, knowing that Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu is still here on earth, spreading the *harināma-saṅkīrtana* movement through your ISKCON devotees.

The time will come...very soon...when everyone will be in ecstasy chanting the holy name of Śrī Kṛṣṇa. There will be no more suffering, all anxieties will go away. Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa will dance on everyone’s tongue. Very wonderful feelings of divine love will overcome everyone’s heart.

This whole desertlike universe will be over-flooded by the sweet nectar of *kṛṣṇa-prema*. Fallen souls will be waking up from their sleeping state just as popcorn jumps when it comes in contact with fire.

Envy, lust, greed, anger, madness, and illusion will all disappear by the influence of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s Sudarśana *cakra,* just as the snakes in the forest are burned very quickly by the forest fire.

All the leaders of the world will be Vaiṣṇavas wearing *tilaka* on their foreheads, chanting the holy names of Kṛṣṇa. There will be one saintly king, fully Kṛṣṇa conscious, in charge of the whole planet. He will apply all the principles of your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is,* and that king will engage everyone in the transcendental loving service of Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

We have faith in your words, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your servants in Montreal, Canada.  
(written by Kānāi Kṛṣṇa dāsa)

## Mumbai (Juhu)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa on this earth, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.**

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet, which act as a cooling shelter from the blazing fire of this material world.

In the *Gurv-aṣṭaka,* by Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura, it is said:

*yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo*  
*yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ‘pi*  
*dhyāyan stuvaṁs tasya yaśas tri-sandhyaṁ*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam \*\**

**“By satisfaction of the spiritual master, the Supreme Personality of Godhead becomes satisfied. And by not satisfying the spiritual master, there is no chance of being promoted to the plane of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. I should, therefore, meditate and pray for his mercy three times a day and offer my respectful obeisances unto him, my spiritual master.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, obtaining your mercy is the goal of our lives. For if your mercy is obtained, all perfection will be reached, as stated in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 5.18.12:

*yasyāsti bhaktir bhagavaty akiñcanā*  
*sarvair guṇais tatra samāsate surāḥ*  
*harāv abhaktasya kuto mahad-guṇā*  
*manorathenāsati dhāvato bahiḥ*

**“All the demigods and their exalted qualities, such as religion, knowledge and renunciation, become manifest in the body of one who has developed unalloyed devotion for the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Vāsudeva. On the other hand, a person devoid of devotional service and engaged in material activities has no good qualities. Even if he is adept at the practice of mystic yoga or the honest endeavor of maintaining his family and relatives, he must be driven by his own mental speculations and must engage in the service of the Lord’s external energy. How can there be any good qualities in such a man?”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we have many excuses for engaging in sense gratification. However, it is stated in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 11.5.41:

*devarṣi-bhūtāpta-nṛṇāṁ pitṝṇāṁ*  
*na kiṅkaro nāyam ṛṇī ca rājan*  
*sarvātmanā yaḥ śaraṇaṁ śaraṇyaṁ*  
*gato mukundaṁ parihṛtya kartam*

**“Anyone who has completely surrendered unto Kṛṣṇa, Mukunda, giving up all other duties, is no longer a debtor, nor is he obliged to anyone—not the demigods, nor the sages, nor the people in general, nor kinsmen, nor humanity, nor forefathers.”**

You have engaged us in devotional service by your causeless mercy; thus all our obligations are fulfilled. Help us dedicate our lives to satisfying your desires. Take away our hankerings for money, accommodations, and followers. Let us see all these as obstacles blocking our progress.

Without your causeless mercy, we cannot understand even theoretically the meaning of the following verses:

*īśvaraḥ paramaḥ kṛṣṇaḥ sac-cid-ānanda-vigrahaḥ*  
*anādir ādir govindaḥ sarva-kāraṇa-kāraṇam*

**“There are many personalities possessing the qualities of Bhagavān, but Kṛṣṇa is the supreme because none can excel Him. He is the Supreme Person, and His body is eternal, full of knowledge and bliss. He is the primeval Lord Govinda and the cause of all causes.” (*****Brahma-saṁhitā*** **5.1)**

*ete cāṁśa-kalāḥ puṁsaḥ kṛṣṇas tu bhagavān svayam*  
*indrāri-vyākulaṁ lokaṁ mṛḍayanti yuge yuge*

**“All the lists of the incarnations of Godhead submitted herewith are either plenary expansions or parts of the plenary expansions of the Supreme Godhead, but Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Personality of Godhead Himself.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.3.28)**

By our efforts it may not be possible to satisfy Your Divine Grace. Still it is better to have engaged in devotional service and not completed it than never to have tried at all.

*tyaktvā sva-dharmaṁ caraṇāmbujaṁ harer*  
*bhajann apakvo ’tha patet tato yadi*  
*yatra kva vābhadram abhūd amuṣya kiṁ*  
*ko vārtha āpto ’bhajatāṁ sva-dharmataḥ*

**“One who has forsaken his material occupations to engage in the devotional service of the Lord may sometimes fall down while in an immature stage, yet there is no danger of his being unsuccessful. On the other hand, a nondevotee, though fully engaged in occupational duties, does not gain anything.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.5.17)**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, what you desire for us is all we can ever hope to be. You explain in your purport to *Bhagavad-gītā* 2.46: “Therefore, revival of Kṛṣṇa consciousness by the individual living entity is the highest perfectional stage of Vedic knowledge.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we pray that you bring us out of ignorance so we can understand that this material world is a miserable place where there are dangers at every step.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, kindly engage us more and more in assisting your preaching. Actually, you are doing all the preaching and we can only lend a hand. In aiding you, we will understand that unless we are Kṛṣṇa conscious it will not be possible to control our senses. We learn from your purport to *Bhagavad-gītā* 2.61:The great sage Durvāsā Muni picked a quarrel with Mahārāja Ambarīṣa, and Durvāsā Muni unnecessarily became angry out of pride and therefore could not check his senses. On the other hand, the king, although not as powerful a *yogī* as the sage, but a devotee of the Lord, silently tolerated all the sage’s injustices and thereby emerged victorious. The king was able to control his senses because of the following qualifications, as mentioned in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (9.4.18–20):

*sa vai manaḥ kṛṣṇa-pādāravindayor*  
*vacāṁsi vaikuṇṭha-guṇānuvarṇane*  
*karau harer mandira-mārjanādiṣu*  
*śrutiṁ cakārācyuta-sat-kathodaye*

*mukunda-liṅgālaya-darśane dṛśau*  
*tad-bhṛtya-gātra-sparśe ‘ṅga-saṅgamam*  
*ghrāṇaṁ ca tat-pāda-saroja-saurabhe*  
*śrīmat-tulasyā rasanāṁ tad-arpite*

*pādau hareḥ kṣetra-padānusarpaṇe*  
*śiro hṛṣīkeśa-padābhivandane*  
*kāmaṁ ca dāsye na tu kāma-kāmyayā*  
*yathottama-śloka-janāśrayā ratiḥ*

**“King Ambarīṣa fixed his mind on the lotus feet of Lord Kṛṣṇa, engaged his words in describing the abode of the Lord, his hands in cleansing the temple of the Lord, his ears in hearing the pastimes of the Lord, his eyes in seeing the form of the Lord, his body in touching the body of the devotee, his nostrils in smelling the flavor of the flowers offered to the lotus feet of the Lord, his tongue in tasting the Tulasī leaves offered to Him, his legs in traveling to the holy place where His temple is situated, his head in offering obeisances unto the Lord, and his desires in fulfilling the desires of the Lord...and all these qualifications made him fit to become a** ***mat-para*** **devotee of the Lord.”**

Improving our attitude of surrender and service to you seems harder as time passes. As neophytes, without your physical presence we feel desperate. On this most wonderful day we request you to remove all hindrances to our remembering you in all that we do every day.Sometimes we may get carried away and somehow think that we can imitate your activities as the *ācārya* of ISKCON. This brings to mind the following verses from *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (10.33.30–31):

*naitat samācarej jātu manasāpi hy anīśvaraḥ*  
*vinaśyaty ācaran mauḍhyād yathā ’rudro ’bdhi-jaṁ viṣam*

*īśvarāṇāṁ vacaḥ satyaṁ tathaivācaritaṁ kvacit*  
*teṣāṁ yat sva-vaco-yuktaṁ buddhimāṁs tat samācaret*

**“One should simply follow the instructions of the Lord and His empowered servants. Their instructions are all good for us, and any intelligent person will perform them as instructed. However, one should guard against trying to imitate their actions. One should not try to drink the ocean of poison in imitation of Lord Śiva.”**

When will we ever learn the lesson that the only way to serve you properly is with humility? This basic principle seems to elude us all the time. Still, with all our shortcomings, because your mercy is unfathomable we have hope that we can come to the perfect stage.

You considered ISKCON Juhu your office. All the devotees here at your office beg to become your loyal staff members. Please bless us that we may take the responsibility of maintaining your office seriously.

Some devotees visited Juhu recently and stayed for a few days. These devotees were amazed at the amount of preaching that is going on here at your office. I would just like to list some of the programs that are going on for your pleasure.

Recently almost all the tenants have been settled elsewhere, and so now we can fully engage all of Hare Krishna Land in the service to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Rasabihārī.

Every month the devotees arrange a yoga retreat that is attended by all types of people.

Every Saturday night there is a follow-up program for those who have attended the yoga retreat.

Every Friday night there is class for students.

Sunday there are over eight different classes held at different times during the day.

House programs are a regular feature of congregational preaching.

Govinda’s restaurant is doing very well, with people giving wonderful appreciations in the guest book about the *prasādam* and service. Also, many favorable articles about Govinda’s have appeared in various newspapers and magazines in Mumbai.

ISKCON Juhu was the number-one book distribution temple in the world for 1999.

Thousands of people visit the temple every day and appreciate the beauty of your Deities.

The temple sweet shop is selling so many wonderful sweets that people travel for miles to come taste the *prasādam*.

Every day 250 schoolchildren from our own school and other schools chant Hare Kṛṣṇa in the temple room.

Your BBT here at Juhu has become one of the largest in the world, with all of your book titles in print.

These are just some of the things going on at your office to enhance the preaching of your glories. Please bless your office staff that we may continue to serve and hopefully please you in some small way and thus make our lives perfect.

Your servants at your office in Juhu, Mumbai, India.

## Mumbai (Mira Road)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

We will be always grateful to you because you came all the way from the spiritual world and undertook lots of trouble and austerity in this material world to help us get out of the miserable material world and go back home, back to Godhead.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you personally guided a few fortunate souls, wrote many transcendental scriptures, and established temples (Vaikuṇṭhas) in the material world. By reading your books we come to know about the reality of spirit, matter, and the controller of both; we get a glimpse of the spiritual world, by which our consciousness changes from dirty, impure, material consciousness to ever-fresh, pure, spiritual consciousness; our desire to serve Kṛṣṇa increases; our love for Kṛṣṇa increases; and our desire to go back home, back to Godhead, increases. Before coming to ISKCON, we found every day tough and survival very difficult. Our relationships with people were difficult. Our lives would have been miserable had you not started ISKCON. Now, after coming to ISKCON, we find that every day is fresh, promising us more and more life and nectar. Our personal relationships are becoming sweeter and sweeter because we know that everybody is part and parcel of Kṛṣṇa. Our lives have been saved by ISKCON.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, all this is possible only because of you. What a lovely system you have given us! Just by chanting, dancing, feasting, and philosophizing we can go back home, back to Godhead.

Now that by your mercy we have tasted a little bit of the nectar of ISKCON, we would like to distribute that nectar to others by distributing your books and *prasādam*, conducting many preaching programs, etc. To achieve this, we beg at your lotus feet that you will continue to guide us. Śrīla Prabhupāda, all we want is to please you. Śrīla Prabhupāda, there is nothing else to live for. Please bless us and give us the strength to follow your orders.

Your servants at the ISKCON temple on Mira Road, Mumbai, India.

## Murāri Sevaka

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please allow your words to become our very life. Without those words we are lost in confusion and we have no direction. Please allow your will to become our will and your desire to become our desire. You desired that we establish working models of *varṇāśrama* for all the world to see and emulate, self-sufficient communities where all forms of cottage industry thrive together in harmony. We tried to do this, as you desired, and we failed you. Reflecting on our mistakes, we tried again and yet again, and still we failed you. For this we beg your unlimited tolerance, patience, and forgiveness.

We are like crippled men carrying the enormous weight of your instruction, taking one step toward pleasing you and then falling down, and still we struggle to take yet another step in the face of our failure. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for allowing us the opportunity to try again, to start over and over and over if necessary. This is the proof of your causeless unlimited mercy, without which we can find no salvation. It is in this repeated trying that we can achieve a drop of your causeless mercy, and with that one drop we can conquer the whole world for your glory.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for not discarding us and casting us out, even though we deserve no place at your lotus feet. In our lame efforts to serve you may we achieve even the smallest measure of your wisdom, for with that wisdom we can understand how to accomplish your wishes.

Dearest father, Śrīla Prabhupāda, kindly work your will through us and allow us to be an instrument in your hands. Guide us in establishing this dream of a working model of *varṇāśrama,* a self-sufficient community in this place you have named Murāri Sevaka. We must not settle for anything less than this. We have cried out to you in our darkest hour, and in response you have sent an army of Vaiṣṇavas under the care or your renowned and capable servant Prithu dāsa Prabhu, just to carry out your mission here at Murāri Sevaka. May there be glorious success in their future here, and may our community rally together like never before in a new and vigorous effort. Kindly empower us to establish a self-sufficient working model of *varṇāśrama* for all to see, and in so doing reveal to the world your plan for the ideal society. Please allow us to be a small room in the house you have built. With your love as the shining light filling up this room and spilling out of the windows, Murāri Sevaka will only then become the beacon light of the world, calling out to everyone to come home to Murāri. Please use us as you see fit in fulfilling your purpose in this land known as Murāri Sevaka.

Your devotees at Murāri Sevaka, ISKCON’s farm community in Tennessee, USA.

## Nagpur

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this auspicious day of your appearance in this world, the devotees at the ISKCON temple here offer their most humble obeisance at your lotus feet.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, the Nagpur temple is giving devotional association to thousands of people living outside. This association is given through Sunday feasts, festivals, *harināmas,* home programs, youth forums, *prasādam* distribution, Food for Life, and classes in the *japa* chanting of the *mahā-mantra,* Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, all your ISKCON centers are trying to give more association to these materialistic people. In this way the demoniac mentality can be vanquished.

*satāṁ prasaṅgān mama vīrya-saṁvido*  
*bhavanti hṛt-karṇa-rasāyanāḥ kathāḥ*  
*taj-joṣaṇād āśv apavarga-vartmani*  
*śraddhā ratir bhaktir anukramiṣyati*

**“In the association of pure devotees, discussion of the pastimes and activities of the Supreme Personality of Godhead is very pleasing and satisfying to the ear and the heart. By cultivating such knowledge one gradually becomes advanced on the path of liberation, and thereafter he is freed, and his attraction becomes fixed. Then real devotion and devotional service begin.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **3.25.25)**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, because of you, our future here in Nagpur is bright. You are our real father and well-wisher. A real father wants to see his son in a happy and secure position. “Even a little advancement on this path protects one from the most fearful type of danger.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are our real protector.

Today the entire world is placed in an awkward position because the demons have become more and more powerful. The demons are not interested in the welfare of the world. They sometimes make a show of religion, but their aim is material prosperity.

Those who are *munayaḥ,* saintly persons following in the footsteps of Nārada Muni, simply engage in the transcendental loving service of the Lord without any personal desire for sense gratification. *Anyābhilāṣitā-śūnyaṁ jñāna-karmādy-anāvṛtam*. Either in this life or in the next, the only concern of such saintly devotees is to serve the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Thus they are also absolute because they have no other desires. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 8.12.6 purport\]

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please help us become *munayaḥ*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please show your mercy to the Nagpur *yātrā* so that it can proceed on the right path to fulfill Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s desire.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please help us free the Nagpur *yātrā* from the reactions to past sins so that there will be no hindrance to its progress.

By surrendering to the Lord and praying for His causeless mercy, the devotee can progress on the path of complete self-realization. The Lord is addressed as fire because He can burn anything into ashes, including the sins of the surrendered soul. \[*Īśopaniṣad* 18, purport\]

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please help us become surrendered souls.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Appearance Day *kī jaya*! Nagpur *yātrā kī jaya*!

Your servants in Nagpur, India.  
(written by Rasamaṇḍala dāsa)

## New Badarikāśrama

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you on your blessed Vyāsa-pūjā day. All glories to your *saṅkīrtana* movement! It is said that Lord Anantadeva has been extolling the transcendental qualities of the Supreme Lord with His numberless tongues and that for incalculable eons He has been baffled in His attempts to fully encompass all of Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s attributes. The same is true of your unlimited glories. So, with only one tongue, what can I say?

Although you have already fully equipped us to cross over this world of *duḥkhālayam aśāśvatam,* you are still extending more and more of your causeless mercy without stint. Just as Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja describes Lord Caitanya as a gardener, so we can immediately recognize Your Divine Grace as the representative of that same transcendental gardener coming down in *paramparā*. Not only are you constantly harvesting an abundance of fruits in the form of varieties of *kṛṣṇa-prema,* but you are also freely distributing these sweet, nectarean fruits, and when the poor, hungry people eat, you smile. Upon tasting such spotless fruits, the *jīvas’* cheeks swell with a semblance of their original, authentic smiles and their eyes sparkle with soulful satisfaction.

Because you have so nicely presented the mature fruit of the desire tree of Vedic literatures, *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* it has become even more relishable for everyone. Your direct words, your books, your letters, and your perfect example of pure devotional service and surrender to guru and Kṛṣṇa are some of the fruits you are freely bestowing intact to all sincere souls.

Just as a blade of grass cannot move without the sanction of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, so we cannot move without your order. You have salvaged us from the dry, desolate wasteland of impersonalism and voidism and transported us into the brave, new world of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Gorgeous Deities, *sādhu-saṅga,* indispensable *prasādam,* self-purifying service to Kṛṣṇa and so much more—what more can a soul desire?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have molded us into missionary soldiers armed with the necessary ammunition, transcendental bombs, and devotional strength to wage war against Māyā’s forces and to protect ourselves from our own lusty desires. How can we ever repay you for saving us? We cannot, but the least we can do is try to rededicate our lives to you and try to intensify our service to become more in tune with your instructions. We want to become tiny, transparent representatives of Your Divine Grace, constantly assessing ourselves according to the criteria you set for purity, humility, sincerity, and so on. Thank you for opening our eyes with the torch of spiritual knowledge and engaging us in assisting others as well. May we become worthy recipients of your mercy.

Your humble servants at New Badarikāśrama, Denver, Colorado, USA.

## New Delhi

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept our most humble obeisances unto your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this most auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā, we are feeling a little despondent because you are not presiding over this occasion in your *vapu* form. But we feel a little solace because we know you are always present in your *vāṇī* form through your books, lectures, tapes, conversations, etc.

You are the *sena-pati* devotee of Lord Caitanya who advented on this earth to propagate pure love of Kṛṣṇa. *Sena-pati* means a “leader of the army,” and you are the true leader of Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* army.

By adjusting but not diluting the spiritual principles for the general masses, you have expertly devised the perfect means for making devotees of Lord Kṛṣṇa these days. In the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (2.4.18), it is stated:

*kirāta-hūṇāndhra-pulinda-pulkaśā*  
*ābhīra-śumbhā yavanāḥ khasādayaḥ*  
*ye ’nye ca pāpā yad-apāśrayāśrayāḥ*  
*śudhyanti tasmai prabhaviṣṇave namaḥ*

**“Kirātas, Hūṇas, Āndhras, Pulindas, Pulkaśas, Ābhīras, Śumbhas, Yavanas and members of the Khasa races, and even others addicted to sinful acts can be purified by taking shelter of the devotees of the Lord, for He is the supreme power. I beg to offer my respectful obeisances unto Him.”**

You have proven this *śloka* true by turning countless souls in every walk of life into Lord Kṛṣṇa’s devotees. You are the real purifier of *mlecchas,* Yavanas, Mongolians, etc.

On your Centennial Vyāsa-pūjā we inaugurated a very prominent landmark in New Delhi, the beautiful and majestic home for Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Pārtha-sārathi and Glory of India Vedic Cultural Centre. We have worked unitedly to create these offerings for you. About the need for unity you once said, “Your love for me will be shown by how much you cooperate to keep this institution together when I am gone.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give us strength to cooperate with one another so that we can prove our love for you. You also said, “If you want to please me, distribute my books.” We have purchased two new *saṅkīrtana* vehicles to distribute your books. Many foreign devotees joined the temple to help increase the distribution of your books, especially those from Russia. Furthermore, we are also constructing a big shop within the temple compound for book distribution.

You also said that nobody should go hungry within ten miles of your temples, and for that reason we have launched a massive Food for Life program for the poor. We have also opened a five-star restaurant, Govinda’s, for your pleasure. You also wanted us to preach vigorously. We are doing this by conducting many home programs, college preaching programs, and several yearly Ratha-yātrās, along with regular *harināma-saṅkīrtana* around the greater Delhi area. Our temple is attracting thousands of people every day, and within a year, by your transcendental blessings, we will be able to complete the Glory of India Experience-Museum. For the purity of our devotees here in the New Delhi temple, we are putting lots of emphasis on our *sādhana,* especially the morning program, including chanting sixteen good rounds, hearing *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and *Bhagavad-gītā,* and full attendance at your *guru-pūjā*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we pray at the dust of your lotus feet to please give us the strength to unite all your disciples and grand-disciples so that we can learn to love and trust one another. This alone will give us a very joyful, harmonious spiritual life. Actually, every single spirit soul is craving to love and be loved. Please give us the strength so that we can develop among ourselves the spiritual atmosphere of Goloka Vṛndāvana. In Goloka Vṛndāvana everybody works together, even if there are differences. In the spiritual world there are sometimes different parties among the *gopīs,* but their aim is one, to please Kṛṣṇa.

Once you said that one party could be right and another party could be wrong or vice versa, but that does not matter much. The principle of cooperation is even higher than who is right and who is wrong.

So please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, ask Kṛṣṇa to give us the strength to fulfill your most cherished desire, which is to reawaken our love, trust, and cooperation among ourselves. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give us the strength to keep on serving you life after life.

Your servants at Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Pārtha-sārathi Mandir, New Delhi, India.

## New Dvārakā

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our prostrated obeisances. All glories to you!

On Sunday evenings Nirantara dāsa begins his feast program announcements by welcoming one and all to New Dvārakā, “home of the world’s most famous, most beautiful Deities, Śrī Śrī Rukmiṇī-Dvārakādhīśa.” Since you installed and named these Deities, it’s impossible to take Their *darśana* without thinking of you.

You mentioned New Dvārakā in a letter to Dayānanda dāsawritten in May of 1969:

You have not mentioned when you are returning to Los Angeles. The idea of New Dvaraka in Los Angeles is developing there, and Tamala Krishna has already informed Mr. Leo Brown, the real estate man, to find out a bigger place than the present one. So when you return you try to complete it. I thank you very much for your nice surrendering attitude by placing yourself at my disposal.

Dayānanda Prabhu wrote about the circumstances surrounding your first visit to Los Angeles.

I do remember the first time Prabhupāda visited. We (Uddhava—shortly after replaced by Aniruddha—Nandarani and I) opened the first temple in August 1967. In early 1968 Prabhupāda returned from India to come to our fledgling center on Pico Blvd. He came via San Francisco Shyamsundar, Mukunda, and Murari came first to pave the way for him. Shyamsundar and Mukunda found an apartment, then Shyamsundar organized us into a painting crew, and the four of us stayed up all night painting the entire place white.

A couple of events stick clearly in my mind. When Prabhupāda arrived, many devotees came from San Francisco and other places to accompany him. One morning we were all sitting in Prabhupāda's apartment. Someone (maybe Janaki) asked him how he felt. Remember that he had been in India recuperating from his heart attack and had just arrived back in the U.S. Prabhupāda replied that he had a headache. He said that there was pressure in his ears and that it had bothered him in the night. I was very impressed, because when I got a headache that was severe enough to bother me at night, I was deeply affected by the symptoms. I had been sitting there in front of Prabhupāda watching him until he spoke about his headache and had not suspected that he was uncomfortable. I could understand that he had mastered tolerance of his body. I was deeply impressed by that.

I was in such awe of him. While I was driving him back to his apartment during the evening of the first day of his first visit, it was raining a little, a heavy California drizzle. I was so stunned with his presence next to me that I was struggling to see out of the window through the rain in the dark. I had forgotten all about the windshield wipers.  
I also remember when Prabhupāda moved in to his quarters on Watseka. A few of us had prepared his apartment. Tulasi das and I had built his bathroom. We moved him from his apartment, a place he called “the imitation Beverly Hills” because it was expensive ( 600 a month). A small crew worked to prepare the temple for general habitation. Sometimes Prabhupāda would inspect our progress. Later Karandhar and I jackhammered up the pavement for a small garden for Prabhupāda.

At one period in 1971 Karandhar and I were in charge of the temple. Prabhupāda had just given sannyasa initiation to Vishnu-jana, et al, and “reformed” ISKCON, instituting mandatory mangal aratrik attendance, etc. Prabhupāda was seriously considering going to India to start the preaching there. Rupanuga and Bhagavan had come to L.A. Prabhupāda had a meeting in his garden (Rupanuga, Bhagavan, Karandhar, and I) where he asked us our opinion of whether he should go to India. I remember Rupanuga strongly felt the movement in the U.S. needed Prabhupāda’s guidance. We were all deeply affected by the fact that the movement had gone somewhat astray. Someone else supported Prabhupāda’s interest in going to India. Prabhupāda asked each of us our opinion. Being somewhat overwhelmed by all that was going on at the time, I made a feeble attempt at diplomacy, saying, “I think you should do whatever is your desire, Śrīla Prabhupāda.” Prabhupāda shot back at me, “My desire is to spread this sankirtana movement all over the world.” I was stunned. This event made a deep impression on my psyche. I am still learning things from this one “simple” exchange.

In a 1975 arrival address here in New Dvārakā you emphasized the necessity of simply following spiritual predecessors…

**Prabhupāda:** (leads *prema-dhvanī*)...Gaṅgā-mayī *kī jaya*! New Dvārakā Dhāma *kī jaya*! New Dvārakā Dhāma *kī jaya*! *Samavetā bhakta-vṛnda kī jaya*! All glories to the assembled devotees! All glories to the assembled devotees! All glories to the assembled devotees! Thank you very much. \[devotees offer obeisances\] So I shall go now? Speaking something? Our speaking is the same, *harer nāma harer nāma harer nāmaiva kevalam*.  
**Devotees:** Haribol! Prabhupāda!  
**Prabhupāda:** We have no new discovery. We don’t manufacture. This is our process. We simply follow the predecessor’s instruction. That’s all. Our movement is very easy because we haven’t got to manufacture something. We simply repeat the words and the instruction given by the predecessor. Kṛṣṇa instructed Brahmā, Brahmā instructed Nārada, Nārada instructed Vyāsadeva, Vyāsadeva instructed Madhvācārya, and, in this way, then Mādhavendra Purī, Īśvara Purī, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, then the Six Gosvāmīs, then Śrīnivāsa Ācārya, Kavirāja Gosvāmī, Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura, Viśvanātha Cakravartī, Jagannātha dāsa Bābājī, Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, Gaurakiśora dāsa Bābājī, Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī, and then we are doing the same thing. There is no difference. That is the specific procedure of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. You are singing daily, *guru-mukha-padma-vākya, cittete koriyā aikya, ār nā koriho mane āśā*. Very simple thing. We are receiving the transcendental knowledge through *guru-paramparā* succession. So we have to simply take instruction from guru, and if we execute that to our heart and soul, that is success. That is practical.

Not only did you convince us that Kṛṣṇa is God on the basis of the above mentioned *paramparā* presentation, you also convinced us that service to Him is for our benefit. This you clearly stated in the following excerpt from your *Teachings of Queen Kuntī:*

Why then are we worshiping Kṛṣṇa in the temple by offering Kṛṣṇa so many comforts, dressing Him, decorating Him, and giving Him nice food? We should try to understand that Kṛṣṇa does not need our offerings of nice garments, flowers, or food, but if we give such offerings to Kṛṣṇa, we shall benefit. Thus it is Kṛṣṇa’s favor that He accepts such offerings. If one decorates oneself, one’s reflection in a mirror will also appear decorated. Similarly, since we are reflections of Kṛṣṇa, if we decorate Kṛṣṇa we also shall be decorated. In the Bible it is said that man was made in the image of God, and this means that we are reflections of God’s image. It is not that we invent or imagine some form of God according to our own form. Those who adhere to the Māyāvāda philosophy of anthropomorphism say, “The Absolute Truth is impersonal, but because we are persons we imagine that the Absolute Truth is also a person.” This is a mistake, and in fact just the opposite is true. We have two hands, two legs, and a head because God Himself has these same features. We have personal forms because we are reflections of God. Furthermore, we should philosophically understand that if the original person benefits, the reflection also benefits. So if we decorate Kṛṣṇa, we also shall be decorated. If we satisfy Kṛṣṇa, we shall become satisfied. If we offer nice food to Kṛṣṇa, we shall also eat the same food. Those who live outside the temples of Kṛṣṇa consciousness may never have imagined such palatable food as the food we are offering to Kṛṣṇa, but because it is being offered to Kṛṣṇa, we also have the opportunity to eat it. So we should try to satisfy Kṛṣṇa in all respects, and then we shall be satisfied in all respects.  
Kṛṣṇa does not need our service, but He kindly accepts it. When Kṛṣṇa asks us to surrender unto Him (*sarva-dharmān parityajya mām ekaṁ śaraṇaṁ vraja*), this does not mean that Kṛṣṇa is lacking servants and that if we surrender He will profit. Kṛṣṇa can create millions of servants by His mere desire. So that is not the point. But if we surrender to Kṛṣṇa, we shall be saved, for Kṛṣṇa says, *ahaṁ tvāṁ sarva-pāpebhyo mokṣayiṣyāmi*: “I shall free you from all sinful reactions.” We are suffering here in this material world without any shelter.

As always, your seemingly extemporaneous remarks are strictly and directly based on the statements of *śāstra*. The following verse from the seventh canto of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (7.9.11) illustrates this fact:

*naivātmanaḥ prabhur ayaṁ nija-lābha-pūrṇo*  
*mānaṁ janād aviduṣaḥ karuṇo vṛṇīte*  
*yad yaj jano bhagavate vidadhīta mānaṁ*  
*tac cātmane prati-mukhasya yathā mukha-śrīḥ*

**“The Supreme Lord, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, is always fully satisfied in Himself. Therefore when something is offered to Him, the offering, by the Lord’s mercy, is for the benefit of the devotee, for the Lord does not need service from anyone. To give an example, if one’s face is decorated, the reflection of one’s face in a mirror is also seen to be decorated.”**

Therefore, dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is ultimately for our own benefit that in 1999 the fortunate residents of New Dvārakā Dhāma, on your order and for your pleasure, distributed 149,503 pieces of literature. The New Dvārakā gift shop, Govinda’s Imports, increased its sales of devotional paraphernalia and accessories by 132%, and Govinda’s Buffet increased its *prasādam* sales by 28%.

We hope that you are pleased by these offerings, and we sincerely pray that you will allow us to continue to try to please you eternally.

Your disciples and grand-disciples in New Dvārakā Dhāma, Los Angeles, California, USA.

## New Gokula

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances on this auspicious day, when we celebrate your first Vyāsa-pūjā in the new millennium by gathering together at the lotus feet of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gokulānanda to extol your virtues, enunciate expressions of our gratitude to you, and once again pledge to serve Their Supreme Lordships under your blessed guidance. We are blind, and it is only you who can light up our paths with your shining torchlight of knowledge.

You came to Australia on a number of occasions. Your desire was to fan the spark of devotional service that you had ignited into a spiritual brush fire for the inhabitants of this country. This flame is continuously swelling and abating, but because you were so diligent in explaining *bhakti* to the parched ears of the fallen souls, that fire is still burning. Our commitment to you is to keep it burning, and we will continue to fan it so as to provide all the conditioned souls with an opportunity to relish service at Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet.

You transformed our existence from one of abject misery, hopelessness, and despair to a life with a spiritual future full of happiness and fulfillment. Only you and no one else could create such a transformation in the fallen souls who have taken birth in this Age of Kali.

May we serve you by following the instructions you wrote in your wonderful books. We’d like to thank you for providing shelter for us on this small but happy and peaceful farming community, and we’d like to join with our Godbrothers and -sisters in helping you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to save the world.

Your fallen servants at the New Gokula farm community near Sydney, Australia.  
(written by Vasuśreṣṭha dāsa)

## New Goloka

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our obeisances on this most auspicious day of your appearance. We are very grateful that Kṛṣṇa has given us this opportunity to glorify His dearmost servant.

You came alone to a foreign country and spread the confidential message of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Thank you for giving us this knowledge and for giving us the opportunity to give it to others.

You have kindly sent us Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Golokānanda to worship and give us inspiration. Our humble attempt is to follow your instructions purely. The most important thing is to remain under the shelter and guidance of your lotus feet. All that we need can be found in your books, lectures, and tapes. By following your program for *sādhana,* beginning with the morning program, we see how nicely both junior and senior devotees are advancing.

Deity worship, *prasādam* and book distribution, *harināma,* and college preaching are all going on as an offering of gratitude to you. We are happy to see the results of more and more souls coming to learn about Kṛṣṇa. Our temple no longer consists solely of a handful of devotees but extends to a congregation across the state. The stability of the temple has increased as devotees take up different occupations and contribute their talents and finances to the temple. We hope to provide a proper environment for the children to grow in, both materially and spiritually. We know how much care and concern you have for these Vaikuṇṭha children, and we pray to be able to train them to be first-class devotees of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Where would we be without you, Śrīla Prabhupāda? You have given us everything we know about Kṛṣṇa, and on this day we would like to rededicate ourselves to your transcendental mission. We pray for your blessings so that we may continue to serve you and remain faithful to your instructions.

Your servants at New Goloka, Hillsborough, North Carolina, USA.

## New Govardhana

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, any honest person who studies your life will conclude that you were not an ordinary conditioned soul governed by the harsh laws of material nature. It is evident from a careful study of your life that you perfectly displayed the twenty-six qualities of a pure devotee of Kṛṣṇa listed in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*. Such a great and magnanimous soul as you is rarely found within this material realm, and due to your unparalleled purity and exalted status as an intimate associate of the Supreme Lord, you are worshipable by the entire world.

You exhibited your **mercy** by opening hundreds of temples, printing millions of books, and sending disciples all over the world to teach Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Your mercy fell everywhere, like pleasing rain upon the burning hearts of this age. Here in San Diego we are forever grateful for your mercy. We are continuing with college preaching, we have ecstatic Sunday Love Feasts, and we have a wonderful dinner program every evening with *Bhagavad-gītā* classes.

You always spoke boldly and **truthfully**, never altering the pure message to attract followers, fame, and money. You presented a simple and easy process of spiritual life handed down through the disciplic succession. There are many editions of *Bhagavad-gītā,* but yours is *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* because you only repeated the exact message spoken by the Supreme Lord Himself.

You have given us wonderful philosophy to live by, knowledge of our true spiritual identities, beautiful Deities (like Rādhā-Giridhārī here in San Diego) to worship, the all-auspicious holy name of Kṛṣṇa, delicious *kṛṣṇa-prasādam*, and wonderful devotee communities for our shelter all over the world. You selflessly gave of yourself at every step, always exhibiting the **magnanimous** nature of a pure devotee of Kṛṣṇa.

I remember one lecture you gave in Los Angeles, stating that you were “horrified at the thought of taking *sannyāsa,*” but you surrendered to the calling of your beloved Gurudeva. You lived very simply in a tiny room at the Rādhā-Dāmodara temple, preparing for your eventual journey to the West. After preaching vigorously in the West for several years you had access to much opulence, yet **you never claimed any of it as your own**. You used every penny in the service of Kṛṣṇa. We are forever grateful to you.

Desires are eternally flowing like a river from our hearts, but this process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness that you have given us can change the quality of those desires from material to spiritual. **You are desireless**. Your only interest lies in satisfying the transcendental senses of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

You once said that if you had simply sat beneath a tree and preached, very few people would have come to listen. Therefore, to effectively deliver the message of Lord Caitanya, you worked tirelessly to construct beautiful ornate temples, like the one here in San Diego. You never settled comfortably to enjoy these facilities. You chose to follow a rigorous schedule of travel and preaching that even the youngest and healthiest of your disciples could not keep pace with. You were totally **indifferent to material acquisition.**

When you were instructed by Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness in English, you took this order as your life and soul and **fixed** your determination upon carrying it out diligently. Alone, you undertook the treacherous sea journey to America at the age of seventy, coming to this place of voidism and impersonalism, where madness prevails and the weather is cold! We are forever grateful to you.

You often displayed the mood of devout **humility.** You never took credit for your many successes but always preferred to acknowledge your beloved Gurudeva, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, and the assistance given to you by your disciples.

You were a most vibrant personality, abounding with laughter and spiritual enjoyment, yet you were always serious about Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You never forgot Lord Kṛṣṇa, even for a moment, and in this way you taught us the real meaning of **gravity.**

When you were a young man following Gandhi, you expressed that India first needed to be politically free before its spiritual message would be accepted abroad. Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta responded by using logic and scriptural evidence to prove that Kṛṣṇa consciousness alone could rid India as well as the rest of the world of all forms of material suffering. Because you were **not defiant**, you graciously surrendered to his conclusion and accepted him as your spiritual guide.

You were **respectful** to all living beings because you saw them as parts and parcels of the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa, with whom you were fully in love. You taught us to honor and respect all life, not only by promoting vegetarianism but by offering our simple food to Kṛṣṇa, to gain His mercy. We are forever grateful to you.

You saw the spiritual nature of all beings, regardless of their temporary material forms. You were always trying to **uplift everyone equally**, just as Lord Nityānanda did with Jagāi and Mādhāi. We are forever grateful to you.

You exhibited **cleanliness,** both internally and externally. Your heart and mind were constantly submerged in the purifying waters of love of God. Your cleanliness was so profound and far-reaching that you purified the lives of anyone who came in contact with you. Previously we had no concept of spiritual or bodily cleanliness. We are forever grateful to you.

You once described the mind of a pure devotee as being very peaceful, like a pond filled with crystal-clear water. Shouldering the immense pressure and responsibility of a worldwide spiritual family with its problems, you gracefully gave out love, guidance, and practical instructions with full composure, never losing touch with your **peaceful** Lord Kṛṣṇa.

In the material world, when a person attains a position of high authority, he often becomes influenced by false pride and loses the quality of gentleness in his behavior. You, however, although the world’s greatest spiritual authority, never displayed even a drop of false pride and always maintained a **mildness** around which everyone felt at ease.

You were **poetic**, not only in the literary sense, as evidenced by your beautiful poems glorifying your beloved Gurudeva and Lord Kṛṣṇa, but in every aspect of your personality as well. Your gestures, walking, talking, singing, smiling, dancing, and worship were all done with natural poetic grace, as befitting a swanlike pure devotee of the Lord.

You once said “Never was there a moment when I have not thought of Kṛṣṇa in this life.” Your unwavering remembrance of Kṛṣṇa provides the perfectly effulgent example of spiritual **sanity.** By following this example, we will become free from the clutches of the material illusion. We are forever grateful to you.

You were free from the four material defects, namely, the tendency to commit mistakes, to be in illusion, to cheat others, and to possess imperfect senses. Because you performed devotional activities in a mood of full surrender to Lord Kṛṣṇa, you were guided by the personal hand of the Lord and were therefore **faultless**.

Despite several heart attacks, your advanced age, and many other ordeals, you single-handedly planted the seed of devotional service in the hearts of thousands of fallen souls. You were able to do this because you were completely free from material attachment and **fully surrendered to Kṛṣṇa**.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, although you received the most opulent *prasādam,* **you only took modest portions** and distributed the remainder to the devotees. You took great pleasure in seeing all the devotees eating *kṛṣṇa-prasādam*. We are forever grateful to you.

**The six bad qualities were absent from your nature**. You were like a great gem moving in the brilliant light of pure devotional service. We too can become free of lust, anger, greed, illusion, madness, and envy by trying to please you and by enthusiastically serving your mission.

Recently a woman commented to me, “Your teacher has done more for this planet than anyone I know.” Your **welfare work** of spiritually uplifting the inhabitants of this planet will go on for the next ten thousand years. We are forever grateful to you.

You were so **expert** that you miraculously ignited a spiritual explosion that is resonating ever more powerfully throughout the world. You came to America with so little—one trunk of books and seven dollars—but you gave so much.

**Silence** means to engage the tongue in speaking only topics related to Kṛṣṇa. Your words are a river of nectar flowing mercifully into the hearts of the devotees, and in due course of time this mighty river will silence the nonsense of all souls suffering in this world.

You were **compassion** personified, dedicating every breath to spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness, resting only a few hours a night to keep body and soul together. We are forever grateful to you.
