This showed your great moral character, for you jeopardized your social future to stand up for your moral principles. You did not compromise with the modern world for success or comforts. You boldly stood and preached against the propaganda of godlessness manipulating everyone the world over.

We should be so fortunate to be even a little bit like you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Preaching so boldly like a boxer giving Kali-yuga a jab, a left hook, and finally a knockout punch. We don’t want to compromise with this demoniac civilization of abortion and animal slaughter. It is widespread evil. There are at least five billion animals slaughtered each year in America, and the abortions are in the millions and rising.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you saved us from this hell. You forced our eyes open so we could see the reality and ripped away the curtain of illusion. You revealed the ghastly form of Goddess Kālī torturing the conditioned souls with her trident. We’re suffering birth, disease, old age, and death but not considering it very serious, like fools. You revealed and introduced us to our long-lost dearmost friend, Bhagavān Śrī Kṛṣṇa. It was you who enabled us to finally find real love, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Thank you again and again and again to infinity.

We want to prove our thankfulness to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, by giving our measly lives in your service, and by being successful, just as you were successful.

We want to broadcast your name and fame in every town and village by book distribution.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you gave us the peace formula and showed us how to live it by your life’s example. Now you have left our vision and placed the burden on us—your disciples and grand-disciples and grand-grand-disciples—to carry on your mission without any compromise.

ISKCON is pushing to establish the ideal that you would be satisfied with. That ideal is not some “cosmic mystery,” but it is a reality that will naturally follow from our surrender to your humble request to us to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness in every town and village throughout the world. This is the “brass tacks” of your instructions. You would often tell us to use our brains nicely to organize the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Caitanya Mahāprabhu. In essence that means book publishing, book distribution, and opening preaching centers. You praised the *gṛhasthas* for being more successful in preaching work than your *sannyāsī* Godbrothers in England. Your instruction to preach worldwide is our permanent duty and does not change due to time or circumstances.

We admit to being very fallen, struggling to finish sixteen rounds daily. We know if we don’t chant sixteen rounds the lights go out on our spiritual progress. We become so proud in the assembly of devotees, thinking we are so advanced, and then we fight with one another over ridiculous details, like *kaniṣṭha-adhikārīs*.

We need to achieve your broad vision, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We heard that in a letter you said that all your disciples must go and distribute books at least one hour a day. Book distribution is the yoga of today. It is the perfect sacrificial action. The first thing we learn is humility, being rebuffed by the materialists we approach, who don’t care how “exalted” we are and who insult us. The expert preacher tolerates offenses like water rolling off a duck’s back. Then, when we sell a book, we know you are smiling, Śrīla Prabhupāda; we have seen it. Now we know Kṛṣṇa is pleased and the bliss comes rolling in like waves. Finally, we are blessed by the Goddess of Fortune herself, Lakṣmī, which we engage to increase the *saṅkīrtana* of Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Those collectors who refuse to distribute your books will never swim in the ocean of bliss but will remain on the beach in anxiety. But the *gṛhasthas* who support book distribution will also swim in that ocean of bliss, pleasing Caitanya Mahāprabhu. It all comes down to one thing, and one thing only. In our heart, do we really want to please our founder-*ācārya,* you, Śrīla Prabhupāda?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are feeling great despair due to separation from you, but the burden of love you gave us—to preach your mission, Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mission, without compromise—is giving us great bliss and association with you again and again. Thank you.

Your servants in Tampa Bay, Florida, USA.  
(written by Vivasvān dāsa)

## Tokyo

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most respectful obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace! All glories to your *saṅkīrtana* movement!

My first contact with you was at a train station. I was rushing back to my office when I received your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* from one of your *saṅkīrtana* devotees. It was so attractive to me, like Kṛṣṇa. But I did not know anything about the book. When I opened the book in my office I first appreciated your picture. It stunned me. “I’ve never seen this kind of saint before. He looks like so peaceful, grave, and merciful. He must be self-realized ” Those were my first thoughts. In fact, I joined the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement from seeing your picture rather than from reading the *Bhagavad-gītā*.

Some years ago I was on *saṅkīrtana* trying to distribute *Kṛṣṇa* book to a Japanese gentleman. As he leafed through the book quickly, he suddenly saw your picture and said to me, “I met this person in Chicago when I was a college student in the U.S.A. in the 70’s. He was surrounded by many young people. I was attracted to him, but I did not approach him.” So he desired to read your books, and he took your *Kṛṣṇa* book and *Bhagavad-gītā*.

When Caitanya Mahāprabhu traveled, sometimes thousands of people would follow Him. Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu attracted everybody, including animals. You are Gaura-priya-jana, a very, very dear devotee of Gaurāṅga Mahāprabhu. Indeed, you are like Gaurāṅga Mahāprabhu in that you also attract thousands and thousands people.

You are so attractive that your disciples left everything to serve your lotus feet. Your disciples always talk about you. They remember and cherish their personal association with you and accounts of your *līlā* when you were on this planet. Your disciples even dream about you.

When I joined the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement, you were not on this planet. You had already gone. When I saw your picture for the first time, I immediately asked myself, “Where and when can I see this person?” I had a great hope to see you. But then your devotees told me that you had left this planet physically.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, when will we meet you and serve you personally?

When will we get *ahaitukī-bhakti* to Gaura-Nityānanda and Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa?

When will your prophecy come true: “Japan will come last, but they will be the best”?

When will that day be ours?

On this very auspicious occasion of your divine appearance day, we pray for your causeless mercy and all your help in Japan.

Always desiring your association, we beg to remain,  
Your humble servants in Tokyo, Japan.  
(written by Jayānanda dāsa)

## Toronto

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

You taught us the true meaning of love, you showed us the real meaning of service by your perfect example, and you gave us, the most fallen souls, knowledge, joy, and purpose in our lives. We pray that you continue to shower us with your mercy and your wisdom, and we sincerely thank you for your infinite blessings.

You stated before leaving this world that our love for you would be evident in how well we worked as a society “to keep this institution together after I am gone.” As the new millennium begins, we are searching our hearts and souls trying to understand how to solve the problems of the present and plan effectively and judiciously for the ISKCON of the future. The responsibility you have given us is very great, but it is also glorious. Like growing children, we continue to need your guidance, your inspiration, and your kindness. We pray that you purify our minds and hearts so that we can proceed along the perfect path that you set for us. We pray that you inspire us spiritually so that we become truly humble and submissive to your divine will.

This Kṛṣṇa consciousness philosophy is perfect, the knowledge you gave us with such love is perfect, the spirit of Lord Caitanya is perfect. However, we are spiritually immature and still far from surrendered. Please help all of us, both your disciples and your grand-disciples, to utterly take shelter at your lotus feet, to embrace the real essence of spiritual life: selfless, humble, and ecstatic service to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. Purify our hearts and minds and empower us with the potency of Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement so that we can make your ISKCON strong and pure. *Then they will all come and we will be ready...“for your fame will spread all over the three worlds.”*

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!!

Your servants at ISKCON Toronto, Canada.

## Tsudanuma

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

You’re our sunshine, our holy master;  
if we don’t follow you, there’s only disaster.

Out on the sea, it sometimes gets rough;  
we have to hang on, we have to be tough.

Māyā is strong, testing our might;  
follow your instructions, we must stand and fight.

To follow your order, that’s our meditation;  
preach and chant Hare Kṛṣṇa, without deviation.

We must hold on tight to your lotus feet;  
demons are attacking, we must not meet defeat.

You left Rādhā-Dāmodar and came to New York;  
you didn’t even bring a knife and fork.

With *dhotī* and *tilak*—what a surprise!—  
you came to Butler, you didn’t compromise.

You sat down to chant the holy names of the Lord;  
then you gave us a book, the transcendental sword.

You cooked us a feast—what a taste sensation!  
“Please take some more, this is real meditation.”

You would go daily for a morning walk;  
we’d all gather round to hear you talk.

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda, all glories to you!  
Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda, the real Jagad-Guru!

Your servants in Tsudanuma, Chiba, Japan.

## Tucson

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

It’s said “Where ignorance is bliss it is folly to be wise,”  
But hearing from the Internet will surely be our demise.  
This Vaiṣṇava-kathā will not set our hearts free.  
The time for change is now, we all must agree.

What is that urge to know the inside scoop,  
All the detailed news about the latest bloop?  
Any gossip or unpleasant miscommunication  
Is turned into instant world-wide publication.

What axes do we have that we have to grind?  
And how many faults must we try to find?  
Situations and problems, judged and analyzed;  
Meanwhile the devotional creeper becomes paralyzed.

Who has got the time to spend at great length?  
Now is the hour to conserve all of our strength.  
Repairing and rebuilding should be our intent.  
Criticizing and accusing is the greatest lament.

Technology can be useful, an asset in our preaching.  
So let us all be careful about what we’re teaching.  
Simply following your instruction to cooperate  
Will bring about the most auspicious fate.

If we are absorbed in the preaching at hand,  
Studying your books and striving to understand,  
Distributing your *Gītās* in the lots at Wal-Mart,  
Strictly following your order with a purified heart,

Rising early to chant, sixteen rounds under our belt,  
This tendency to fault-find will hardly be felt.  
Instead of checking out who did what on the Web,  
After a full day’s service we’ll just drop into bed.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please forgive us when we stumble.  
Please correct us on this path so we can truly be humble.  
Let us offer glorification and put you in the center,  
For you are our savior, our master, our mentor.

ISKCON’s your child, an offering to your spiritual master.  
It’s our duty to protect it, not create a disaster.  
Let us not be divided, but strong and united,  
Meditating deeply on the words you confided.

“Your love for me will be shown by how you cooperate.”  
It is time to prove that love and no longer hesitate.  
Your work is “half-finished,” there’s still so much to do.  
“ISKCON, for all thy faults, I still love you.”

Your aspiring servants in Tucson, Arizona, USA.  
(written by Sandāminī-devī dāsī)

## Vancouver

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisance at your lotus feet again and again.

It is stated that only through the mercy of a pure devotee can one get Kṛṣṇa and thus liberation beyond the material world, which is temporary and full of miseries. But such pure devotees are very rare.

There are twenty-six qualities of a pure devotee and you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the most rare and purest of all devotees because you possess all twenty-six unlimitedly. To fully describe your unlimited qualities is an impossible task, even for Ananta Śeṣa, who with his thousands of mouths recites your glories continuously. Nonetheless, we would like to briefly mention one such quality that you display unlimitedly. Your quality of mercy.

It is inconceivable how you came to the West at such an advanced age to give Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the fallen souls. With the equivalent of only 8 in a foreign currency that you could not even spend, with no place to stay, no support from friends, relatives, or a mission behind you, seemingly all alone, you came to save the most fallen souls. Lord Caitanya delivers the most fallen souls first, so how befitting that as his primary representative, or, as the *Caitanya-maṅgala* states, “Lord Caitanya’s commander in chief to spread the *saṅkīrtana* mission throughout the whole world,” you came to the most degraded city to execute your mission.

After you gained a small foothold in this part of the world, you did not sit idle and consider your mission complete, but you continued to extend your mercy by spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness throughout the rest of the world.

You extended your mercy as far and wide as possible, constantly traveling, not staying in one place very long, always being subjected to time-zone changes and the demands of your disciples. In twelve years you circumambulated the globe twelve times. It was not the traveling of a tourist or vacationer, at a leisurely pace, but of a great general fighting the most powerful enemy, Māyā. You constantly fired up your troops to march the world back to Godhead. You never considered not extending your mercy; rather, when a preaching opportunity would arise, you always took it and put your own personal health and care aside.

And the amazing thing is that you are still spreading your mercy. Of course, without your personal presence it is sometimes difficult to continue to wage this war against the seemingly impervious enemy called Māyā, but that doesn’t mean your mercy isn’t still being extended or that you are not still present. You are still present, Śrīla Prabhupāda, through all that you left us: your books, your other instructions, the Deity worship you taught us, the “secret weapon” of *prasādam* you taught us how to prepare and offer to the Lord, the farm communities you desired, the schools you founded, and other avenues of preaching you showed us. You have given us so much mercy, and you are still giving it. Somehow or other we just have to try to become a little sincere and receive some of that unlimited mercy. It is readily available.

Anyone can easily see how so many have received your mercy and are still receiving it. There are so many sincere disciples that you have created, and so many grand-disciples as well. It is truly apparent that your mercy is still flowing and will continue to flow for the next ten thousand years and beyond. You will go down in history as the greatest *ācārya,* who saved the whole world and created a house in which everyone can live.

Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Your servants at ISKCON Vancouver, Canada.

## Villaggio Hare Kṛṣṇa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at the dust of your holy lotus feet. All glories unto you! All glories to the anniversary of your wonderful appearance in this world!

During a press conference in New York City in March of 1975, a reporter asked you, “At what age did you realize the highest truth?” At first you answered indirectly out of humility, giving credit to the *paramparā* and to your father, but the man persisted. Finally you said, “At the age of four or five years.” The man was astonished. But the more wonderful thing is that you are eternally self-realized, since from eternal time you have never forgotten the highest truth, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. All the devotees around you in New York exploded with *“Jaya! Haribol!”* and the beating of *mṛdaṅgas* out of their happiness and appreciation of your greatness.

This planet is very fortunate to have received the dust of your lotus feet. From the very beginning of your advent Kṛṣṇa watched over you and guided you, inspiring you directly to become the founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON.

You received the instructions from your spiritual master to spread the teachings of Lord Gaurāṅga (*gauravāṇī pracāriṇe*), and always in all details you have followed the instructions of Gaurāṅga, and you have guided your followers with great love. We can learn from you what the words “love” and “to be loving” mean. From the very beginning your disciples offered everything just to satisfy you because you were so loving. When the book distributors, the *saṅkīrtana* devotees, were flooding the entire planet with love for Kṛṣṇa, you greatly appreciated their effort, and you encouraged them with love, saying “Double it.” Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the most excellent *paramahaṁsa,* free from any material contamination, the most pure. In your pastimes you have always given the best example of how to control the mind by using it in the service of the spiritual master. This is the key to success: *vyavasāyātmikā buddhir ekeha,* to satisfy the spiritual master and not deviate.

I’m trying to follow the good example of your elevated disciples, such as His Holiness Hridāyananda Mahārāja, who by your mercy has completed the translation of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. There are not enough words in all the planets to glorify your great devotion. You have offered everyone the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.* Millions of thanks to you.

I desire to ask a benediction. Please help me develop more love for you, for the *mahā-mantra,* for ISKCON, and for your devoted followers.

Everything connected with you is extremely valuable. I especially appreciate your recorded lectures and *bhajans;* simply to hear your voice, so full of love for Kṛṣṇa, is of great benefit, destroying darkness. Your books are the only ones that deserve to be studied carefully and attentively.

I thank you millions and millions of times for coming down from the spiritual world and appearing on this planet. With great patience you waited for the right time to come to the West and deliver the message of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. All glories unto you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! I hope to become a chaste disciple serving you with more enthusiasm by performing the sacred and pure mission of distributing your wonderful books, which act like bombs to explode the fortress of illusion.

One last request: Please bless the devotees who were developing a taste for *saṅkīrtana* but who have left that service. Please inspire them to return to that service, because it is the most purifying. Distributing your books is all-purifying for the heart; there is no other *yajña* more powerful than spreading the holy name by distributing your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and other books by you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the great *mahā-bhāgavata*.

All glories to the assembled devotees! All glories unto you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your devotees at Villaggio Hare Krishna, Italy.  
(written by Durgama dāsa)

## Vṛndāvana

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Supreme Perfection in Vṛndāvana

Twenty-five years ago, on Rām Navamī day,  
You invoked the presence of Krishna-Balarām.  
Single-handedly you fulfilled your master’s order  
And returned a success to Śrī Vṛndāvan Dhām.

The supreme triumph in a life of devotion—  
You offered the first *ārati* to Their lotus feet.  
You smiled resplendent as you turned to the crowd;  
Ecstatic *kīrtan* made the occasion complete.

When you left Vṛndāvan alone, nine years before,  
You had only some books and a dream to achieve.  
By your determined devotion you created  
A temple that no one else could even conceive.

You transformed *mlecchas* into *brāhmaṇ pūjārīs,*  
Like an alchemist who turns iron into gold.  
Your touchstone was the transcendental holy name  
And the message of *Bhagavad-gītā* you told.

Just as you could invoke Their Lordships to appear  
In *mūrti* form, though They seemed to be made of stone.  
You somehow invoked devotional qualities  
In completely unqualified sinners to the bone.

By your sincere devotion Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa came  
And accepted all the offerings from these boys.  
What a miracle you displayed here in the Dhām!  
And on this Vyāsa-pūjā day we rejoice.

By your unlimited kindness you keep us here,  
A confirmation of your supreme perfection.  
Not just a passing fad whimsically accepted—  
No, twenty-five years of love and devotion.

By the thousands and lakhs they come to examine  
The international temple for everyone.  
We eternally aspire to serve you here—  
Prabhupāda’s supreme perfection in Vṛndāvan.

Your servants in Vṛndāvana, India.

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from the Publishers

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (North America)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

We all came to this movement to give up our worldly mess,  
To say “Good-by” to intoxication, meat-eating, and illicit sex.  
We vowed to you we’d never again pursue a mundane career,  
And accepted your books as the law books for the next ten thousand years.

You told us that we’d please you if we pleased the TP and GBC,  
Distributing your books, the holy name, and *prasād* for eternity,  
That we’d become the most fortunate and help others to become seers,  
That your books would be the law books for the next ten thousand years.

We want to push on your mission, to be good daughters and sons,  
We won’t rest till we repay our debt, a task that’ll never be done.  
We want that every man and woman on this planet will revere  
Your books, the law books for mankind for the next ten thousand years.

At times we may be rascals, at times we may act like fools,  
When we forget the vast distinction between us and you.  
But that dirt is easily removed and our hearts made pure and clear  
When we take shelter of your books, the law books for the next ten thousand years.

We’ve struggled since you left us, though your *vāṇī* still oils the gears.  
Some temples have closed up, and others seem quite a bit in arrears.  
But we know that we can please you, we know you’re with us here,  
When we profusely distribute your books, the law books for the next ten thousand years.

Someone must give *dīkṣā,* it says so in the CC,  
And everyone should give *śikṣā,* giving the holy name without a fee.  
But you give the real *śikṣā* to one who submissively hears  
Your books, the law books for mankind for the next ten thousand years.

Somehow the movement goes on and on, by the mercy of Kṛṣṇa and you,  
Though people come and people go and do what people do.  
Those who’ve stayed have cooperated, though sometimes shedding tears,  
To see that your books will be mankind’s law books for the next ten thousand years.

Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!

Your servants at the North American BBT offices, in Los Angeles, California, USA.

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from Other Sources

## Back to Godhead

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

As your aspiring servants, we naturally look to your example in all areas of devotional service. Today we are meditating on your quality of selfless giving. Inspired by the order of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, you always thought of how to give Kṛṣṇa consciousness to others. Despite the demands of household life, you launched *Back to Godhead* magazine in 1944, single-handedly writing, producing, and distributing it. So great was your desire to give the message of Godhead to others through BTG, that you would sometimes neglect eating to pay for printing.

The same spirit of giving guided you as you translated the *Bhāgavatam* and wrote your enlightening purports, first in the sacred shelter of Rādhā-Dāmodara, and later wherever in the world your mission of giving carried you.

When you landed in America in 1965, you seemed penniless, but you carried the greatest treasure, eager to find souls willing to receive it.

In your twelve years with us, you gave of yourself constantly. We who cherish our solitude can only marvel at how you made yourself accessible to thousands of people. We hear of the early days in places like New York, San Francisco, and Montreal, when your spiritual children would sometimes drop in unannounced. In their eagerness for your audience, they sometimes transgressed the etiquette of the guru-disciple relationship. But as their loving father, you gave them your time, inspiring them to give their lives for the service of Kṛṣṇa and humanity.

You could inspire dedication in others because your life was a perfect example of full dedication. Anyone who heard you speak in public can testify that your focus was on giving Kṛṣṇa. And those privileged souls who traveled with you and served you personally can testify that your giving didn’t stop when you were out of the public view. Sometimes, when relishing a point of Kṛṣṇa conscious philosophy, you would wake your staff in the middle of the night to share it with them. You couldn’t keep it to yourself; you had to give.

This gushing of *kṛṣṇa-kathā* is a sign of a pure devotee. You gave Kṛṣṇa not out of duty, but out of spontaneous love.

By your great mercy and wisdom, you have left us a storehouse of teachings that never stop giving, even in your physical absence. We pray for the desire to wholeheartedly accept your gifts and to awaken appreciation for them in the hearts of millions.

Your eternal servants, the Back to Godhead staff, Alachua, Florida, USA.

## Bhaktivedanta Archives

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances.

In the *Bhagavad-gītā* (2.54) Arjuna asks Kṛṣṇa:

*sthita-prajñasya kā bhāṣā samādhi-sthasya keśava*  
*sthita-dhīḥ kiṁ prabhāṣeta kim āsīta vrajeta kim*

**“O Kṛṣṇa, what are the symptoms of one whose consciousness is thus merged in transcendence? How does he speak, and what is his language? How does he sit, and how does he walk?”**

And Kṛṣṇa’s answer is:

*prajahāti yadā kāmān sarvān pārtha mano-gatān*  
*ātmany evātmanā tuṣṭaḥ sthita-prajñas tadocyate*

**“O Pārtha, when a man gives up all varieties of desire for sense gratification, which arise from mental concoction, and when his mind, thus purified, finds satisfaction in the self alone, then he is said to be in pure transcendental consciousness.” \[*****Bg*****. 2.55\]**

How have you attained this platform? In all the years of proofreading and preserving information at the Bhaktivedanta Archives, we have seen that the teachings of Your Divine Grace are absolutely consistent. There is never an occasion when you said something like “You know, I said this yesterday, but I’ve changed my mind about that; it’s really like this.” How could you be so consistent? The answer is simple: you immersed yourself in the *śāstra* and the instructions of your spiritual master. There is nothing in your thinking that deviates from these principles. Thus there are no desires for sense gratification, and this is the only way that there can be no mental concoction. And so you are fixed in the eternal consciousness, Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Pradyumna dāsa, your “*paṇḍitjī,*” was once asked what he considered to be your most important contribution or activity or attribute. His reply was breathtaking: “Śrīla Prabhupāda gave form to the spiritual energy.” After all, this is the most important aspect of Vaiṣṇava philosophy, that God has form and this form can be realized and understood even here in this material world. When you think about it, all the things that we commonly call “the Hare Krsna movement”—the simple living, the Deities, the dress, the lifestyle, the etiquette, the rituals, the *kīrtana,* dance, *prasādam* (the list is endless and variegated)—all are personified by your form and activities. You are the perfect manifestation of the answer to Arjuna’s question.

Earlier this year we were asked to speak to a group of devotees about the Bhaktivedanta Archives and its activities and duties, and this led us to compile a list of items that are being preserved and maintained in the Bhaktivedanta Archives. We are presenting this list to you and to all the devotees on this holiest of occasions so that you and the devotees may bless these humble efforts, and so that by your mercy it may continue. Because there are so many items and they are so diverse, even over twenty-three years there have not been the proper resources or manpower to catalog everything, so some of these figures are estimates:

Original materials:

2,000 hours of original audiotape; 400 pages of handwritten essays, manuscripts, and other documents; 6,250 letters or copies of letters by Śrīla Prabhupāda; 1,000 letters to Śrīla Prabhupāda; 10,000 pages of transcripts of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s translations and purports to the *Bhagavad-gītā*, *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, *Īśopaniṣad*, and other books; 18,000 color slides of Śrīla Prabhupāda; 3,000 color negatives of Śrīla Prabhupāda; 10,000 black and white negatives of Śrīla Prabhupāda; 75,000 color slides of ISKCON activities; 10,000 black and white negatives of ISKCON activities; 46 hours of film and video footage of Śrīla Prabhupāda in various formats.

Published works and microfiche and digital copies:

Bhaktivedanta VedaBase program and content files; 2,000 hours of digital audiotape masters (extra copies in Sweden, Australia, and underground storage); set of first editions of the English BBT publications; microfiche of 40,000 pages of transcripts of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s tapes and documents; 89 distribution masters for the CD Ministry (music and spoken word); 1,326 distribution masters for the Tape Ministry (music and spoken word); 1,500 high resolution images of BBT artworks on CD-ROM; 2,000 hi-res images of the best of the color slides of Śrīla Prabhupāda on CD-ROM; 2,000 hi-res images of black and white negatives of Śrīla Prabhupāda on CD-ROM; various published volumes of the letters, lectures, diaries, and conversations of Śrīla Prabhupāda, totaling more than 60 books; microfiche of over 300,000 pages of Matsya Project Vedic literatures, containing both first and second phases of the project.

In this material world there are always disasters and rumors of disasters, wars and rumors of wars. In the last year we underwent the Y2K rumors of disaster. Some were predicting the end of civilization as we know it, and there was considerable debate within the devotee community on the proper measures to prepare for this event. Fortunately, there was no such catastrophe, but Y2K served as a reminder to us to be ever diligent. We are mindful that you said on many occasions that the only true preparation for disaster is the chanting of Hare Kṛṣṇa, because in the end we all have our own personal disaster to face at the time of death, when we will have to depart these bodies. By your mercy we will continue this chanting.

At the Bhaktivedanta Archives we are privileged to look after the precious relics assigned to our care. We pray that you will continue to bless our efforts and keep us engaged in the service of your lotus feet.

Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Archives, Sandy Ridge, North Carolina, USA.

## Bhaktivedanta Center

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

As our spiritual preceptor, you’re eternally and lovingly destroying our illusion with the multi-million-watt candle-power of the torchlight of spiritual knowledge. The destruction of the illusion we so tightly grasp is critical to our hoped-for spiritual life.

Most people, believing they’re way too busy for spiritual life, wrongly feel that *bhakti-yoga* would be a drain on their time. They do find time, though, to work overtime—attempting to invent the next wheel or some new labor-saving device—which will ultimately occupy the time they’re supposedly going to be saving. Your admonition to use our time wisely and cultivate Kṛṣṇa consciousness will, if implemented, result in our attaining the perfection of life and ultimately going back home, back to Godhead.

When one does have some measure of leisure time, so many hypotheses on the human condition are proffered, and you so nicely point out their inaccuracies. Many *jīvas* are enamored of the materialistic so-called philosophers, who with big, big words simply juggle their mental conclusions. In speaking about the existentialist philosopher Camus, who “inspired” so many miserable people with his “I don’t care” attitude, you rendered his ersatz philosophical foundation nil with only four words: “But he took care.” Minimum words, maximum effect.

In our attempts at preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness, we see that some *jīvas* are siphoned away from the bona fide process by a plethora of quasi-spiritual groups—everything from encounter groups, sweat lodges, and walking labyrinths to the ever-popular psychospiritualists. Your clear and authoritative Bhaktivedanta purports allow us to see how bogus are these new varieties of spiritual farce, and like a flame touched to parchment they burn these straw men to ashes.

Western so-called civilization can be likened to a double-edged sword in that it cuts both ways. On the one hand we have an enormous potential for change, or material advancement. Materially, practically anything we want is here for the taking. The down side is that we become accustomed to immediate sense gratification. Thus anything requiring a little time to bring to fruition stands a good chance of being rejected in favor of something giving an immediate sensory response. It’s the age-old case of *preyas* vs. *śreyas*. Without spiritual life, as is so often the case, our ego keeps pace, stride for stride, with the material advancement, thus becoming quite inflated and well developed, and ultimately we lose sight of who or what we actually are. Your Divine Grace nicely pops this bubble, and in spite of abundant opposition, you are delivering Lord Kṛṣṇa’s message to anyone, anywhere, and at any time.

Your spiritual instructions and the society you envision that we, collectively, will continue and build upon will be, when your instructions are fully implemented, a transcendental finishing school where our spiritual understanding and etiquette, due entirely to your efforts, will prepare us for our next birth—hopefully a spiritual one. Who is giving such a legacy? Though we are unfortunate, still you’re giving us all good fortune—if we’ll only take up this process.

The expectation is that we will indeed take up this spiritual life in all earnestness, until we leave our bodies, with the hope of achieving the goal of life: revival of our eternal relationship with the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. You admonish us not to let our spiritual lives become a fashion, as we were trained up to do in Churchianity. Rather than opting for the safety of the *bhajanānandī* position and in seclusion wrapping up your exemplary spiritually saturated life in India, you most chastely and gloriously fulfilled your Guru Mahārāja’s request. For this incomparable effort and for your guidance we can only fall at your lotus feet repeatedly and eternally. The love you are showing the world makes everything else pale in comparison.

Desiring always to remain in your lotus shadow as your lowly servants, we remain  
Your aspiring devotees at the Bhaktivedanta Center, Charlottesville, Virginia, USA.  
(written by Amit Ācāra dāsa)

## Bhaktivedanta College of Education and Culture

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace! All glories to the most blessed event of your Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā!

In a letter dated 12 October 1974, you wrote,

But before saving others we must first see that we ourselves are protected from the dangerous effects of maya. Therefore my recommendation to you is that you should associate yourself with my disciples in one of our Krishna Consciousness centers, practice our regulative principles, and study my books very carefully.

And in a lecture given in February 1970 you emphasized that “in addition to the classes, study of our scriptures must be done very scrutinizingly.”

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for blessing the growth of the Bhaktivedanta College of Education and Culture (BCEC) as one of your many instruments to help us fulfill your desire. Since its beginning in Durban in July 1991, BCEC has expanded to Johannesburg, Pretoria, Cape Town, Mauritius, Nairobi, Māyāpur, Hong Kong, Manila, Cebu, Vancouver, Toronto, Washington, D.C., Houston, Trinidad, and Guyana. Over four thousand people, both seasoned devotees and beginners, have graduated from various courses offered by that BCEC.

We pray that you continue to bless us to study and share this sublime transcendental knowledge with others.

Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta College of Education and Culture, Durban, South Africa.  
(written by Mādrī-devī dāsī)

## Bhaktivedanta Gurukula and International School

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**A Gathering of Praise**

Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
with golden cymbals in hand  
you sing so sweetly.

In a foreign place  
you walk down the street with grace,  
with a steady pace.

Bold heroic man,  
gentle father leading all,  
Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Serious strong smile,  
messages that will not die,  
by Śrīla Prabhupāda.

When you look at the Deity  
of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara,  
tears pour from your eyes.

Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
you smile eternally.  
Bless us with that smile.

Hare Kṛṣṇa,  
Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
please accept us.

Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Gurukula and International School in Śrī Vṛndāvana Dhāma, India.  
(written by students in the 7th to 10th grades)

## Bhaktivedanta Institute (Berkeley and Mumbai)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Sometimes people say that Your Divine Grace died in November 1977. But then we can recollect your words here in Berkeley in response to a reporter who asked what would happen after your disappearance. Your response was that “I will live forever in my books, and you will utilize.” Of course, Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura made the same point in his oft-quoted verse about the Vaiṣṇavas:

He reasons ill who tells that Vaiṣṇavas die  
When thou art living still in sound!  
The Vaiṣṇavas die to live, and living try  
To spread the holy name around!

We can practically see that you are still very much with us, and we still associate with you by reading your transcendental books.

Whenever this Vyāsa-pūjā ceremony is performed, we feel obliged to bring Your Divine Grace some report of how we are trying to execute your many instructions for how to preach to scientists. Thus we would like to report that one of our members, Rasarāja dasa, published a paper entitled “Quantum Theory and the Observation Problem” in a reputable scientific journal, *The Journal of Consciousness Studies*. We are also continuing to offer a graduate program granting Masters and Ph.D. degrees through the authority of Birla Institute of Technology and Science at Pilani, one of India’s top technical schools.

In the course of our conversations with scientists, we have heard several reputable scientists say that a new science is coming. We are also trying very hard to bring in this new science. We believe that Lord Caitanya is making many arrangements in the scientific world to bring about a revolution in science. Therefore, the new science will be the science of the Golden Age of Lord Caitanya. Your transcendental books will give the basic conceptual outline of this science. O Śrīla Prabhupāda, please engage us in Lord Caitanya’s mission of revealing this new science. People are suffering under the philosophy that “consciousness is a product of matter.”

We are begging to remain engaged in your preaching mission.

Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Institute in Berkeley, California, USA, and Mumbai, India.

## Bhaktivedanta Society

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

This year, I feel, we can offer you something tangible here in Israel.

Although our external aspect is not so devotional, and instead of living in the traditional temple and following regulated devotional life most of us attend the university and have mostly student association, still, we learned how to develop loving relations within our community, we started to take seriously the cleaning of the *anarthas* from our hearts, and, most important, we began to learn how to approach all your (*karmī*) children with love and friendship. As a result, we are hardly considered a sect anymore, and the public has learned to appreciate our contribution of devotion and personalism to the local culture. We are even able to preach to religious people. They appreciate our philosophy and often find elements of it within their own culture. People are happy to receive your books willingly give contributions.

I think all this is due to your inconceivable guidance and inspiration. Please help us to become free from politics, to dive wholly and purely into preaching to the suffering souls, and to love the devotees.

Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Society, Israel.  
(written by Guṇa Avatāra dāsa)

## Cleveland Nāma Haṭṭa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

O dear Prabhupāda, we pledge our hearts to thee,  
For you have given us the key  
To life eternal, peace, joy, and freedom.  
And because of you, pure devotees we will be.

You walked this earth in absolute reality,  
A true personification of love and humility.  
You asked nothing for yourself but gave life to all else.  
O renowned one, esteemed one, renounced one.

So take our hearts and mold them to Kṛṣṇa’s will,  
And on them let them bear the seal  
of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, that’s love of God.  
All glories to Your Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Then tell us, Śrī Guru, what can we do for you?  
What can one give for the gift of life?  
Just let us always be surrendered at your lotus feet,  
And surely then Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s favor we will meet.

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. We are feeling so much separation from your lotus feet.

The above song, penned by Kṛṣṇanandiṇī-devī dāsī, who was initiated by Your Divine Grace in Dallas, Texas, in September of 1972, is a small seed of our desire to serve you. We are realizing your mercy daily as we endeavor to share Kṛṣṇa consciousness in Cleveland, Ohio, and surrounding towns. Thank you for the awesome legacy you have bequeathed to us in the form of your books, your example, your disciples, and all the other weapons you left with us (the holy names, *tilaka,* Deity service, *saṅkīrtana,* and the previous *ācāryas*). We pray that Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī and Lord Nityānanda will bestow Their mercy upon us as we try to reflect the deep and genuine love that you have given us.

Your servants of the Nāma Haṭṭa program in Cleveland, Ohio, USA.

## College Traveling Saṅkīrtana Party

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this day commemorating your divine appearance, we would like to thank you for engaging us in the service of Lord Caitanya by way of your unique process of preaching through book distribution.

Just like Vyāsa, you have taken the topmost conclusions of the *Vedas* and made them understandable to the common mass of people in this unfortunate age. Through wonderful analogies and practical instructions, you have enabled anyone who has a little faith or sufficient intelligence to begin the process of *kṛṣṇa-bhakti*. By hearing from you, taking to the chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra,* and taking the association of those devotees following in your footsteps, any conditioned soul can become fortunate and actually become a devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, by assisting you in your book distribution mission, we are witnessing you preaching to and affecting innumerable conditioned souls. We daily feel enlivened to see you perpetuating and expanding Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement. We are completely happy to be the “peon postmen” who carry the message you have so clearly written. Ending each day with new realizations and a lighter, cleaner heart, we know that you are actually delivering us to Kṛṣṇa consciousness through this service; not by our *sādhana* alone could we feel so connected to you and Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Knowing that we can never repay you for all that you have given us, we bow down at your lotus feet and beg you for your mercy so that we may eternally have this confidential service of assisting you in distributing Lord Caitanya’s mercy to others. Although wandering here and there on your behalf, we feel happily at home in the shade of your lotus feet.

Your servants doing College TSKP in the USA.

## Food for Life Global

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On behalf of all the Food for Life devotees, please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to your service! All glories to Śrī Kṛṣṇa *prasādam*!

One never gets bored with Kṛṣṇa *prasādam*. In fact, once you have experienced the Lord’s mercy you can never forget it, what to speak of tiring of it. Like the Lord, it is ever fresh, pure, energizing, satisfying, and calming. It soothes our disturbed minds, calms our passions, uplifts our souls, and helps us cope with the many complex and distracting material affairs we encounter daily. *Prasādam* is the true elixir of material life. Without *prasādam* our bodies are simply dead and worthless. With *prasādam* our bodies become transformed into vital emissaries of good will and productivity.

A devotee does not “eat” *prasādam* but rather “accepts” the Lord’s mercy. When we accept *prasādam* we in fact serve the Lord and invite Him into our hearts. As the Lord sits upon the throne of our hearts, He blesses us with transcendental wisdom and purifies our senses, beginning with the tongue. “Spiritual life begins with the tongue,” you told us, Śrīla Prabhupāda. How deeply profound that statement is, and yet how simplistic and deceiving as well. Such is the mystery of devotional service. Such is the mystery of *prasādam*!

Tonight we heard a nice lecture by Nityodita Swami from New Vṛndāvana. He spoke about the essence of devotional service as illustrated in chanting, dancing, and feasting and encouraged us to develop affection for Kṛṣṇa though these activities. He reminded us of the time when you would cut one apple into small pieces after your lecture in New York so that every guest in the room was able to get at least one morsel of *prasādam*. Such was your faith in the importance of *prasādam*!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we owe you so much for introducing us to the wonders of Kṛṣṇa *prasādam*. Without it, I shudder to think where most of us would be right now. I was certainly saved by Kṛṣṇa *prasādam*. It maintained my devotional creeper just like a baby is maintained by its mother’s breast milk. Early on in my devotional life I caught the importance of Kṛṣṇa *prasādam* and wanted to share it with others. In fact, my first service was serving *prasādam* and cleaning the restaurant. However, my attachment came much earlier than that. I still remember attending the Sunday Feasts and taking home four to five large plastic containers of *prasādam* “leftovers” to have another feast with my friends when I returned! Oh, how sweet those memories are.

More important, though, is that your “*prasādam* legacy” has lived on and expanded immensely through Hare Kṛṣṇa Food for Life. Now more than ever in the history of the world, people from all walks of life are getting a chance to experience Kṛṣṇa *prasādam*. From one perspective this is a glorious act of humanitarianism, especially when we see your devotees risking their lives to serve *prasādam* to flood victims, as they did in Orissa and Mozambique. And from another perspective we can see the wonder of Kṛṣṇa’s hand blessing His children with the seed of love of God and thus preparing them for further devotional service. Indeed Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu asked us to help Him distribute the fruits of love of God, and so surely Food for Life is an expression of that desire.

Having said that, we must also acknowledge that you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are also Kṛṣṇa *prasādam* and that all the glory that comes with *prasādam* can be equally ascribed to you. For you too are ever fresh, pure, uplifting, satisfying, and calming to be with. You soothe our disturbed minds with your presence, calm our passions with your merciful glance, uplift our souls with your purity, and help us cope with the many complex and distracting material affairs with your transcendental wisdom.

You are the true elixir of our life. Without you, our bodies are simply dead and worthless. With you our bodies become transformed into vital emissaries of good will and productivity. You are the personification of Lord Caitanya’s fruit of love of God.

Thank you for being who you are.

Your prasādam distributors throughout the world.  
(written by Priyavrata dāsa)

## Hare Krishna World

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace on the anniversary of your merciful appearance on earth!

Having entered a new millennium, we are looking beyond the year 2000 to what we want this project to be. To draw inspiration, we remember your instructions on publishing a newspaper, as *Hare Krishna World* founder Mukunda Goswami recalled them:

In 1970 Śrīla Prabhupāda asked his followers to print a daily newspaper—half Hare Kṛṣṇa events, and the other half news of the world, laced with ISKCON analysis and commentary.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, as a most pragmatic preacher, you understood the economic, political, and cultural forces that sway people’s consciousness and behavior. You have generously delivered transcendental knowledge to help people of all backgrounds connect with their true spiritual nature. You are also expert at teaching us how to avoid the pitfalls of this material world, including lust, greed, and ignorance.

Today communication is sped up tremendously by computers. Sometimes harmful rumors spread faster than wildfire. Economic disparity among the populations of the world is widening. Large international corporations have the power to exploit whole nations as never before. Species of animals and plants are disappearing, and the air, water, and soil are becoming increasingly toxic. Children’s minds are being twisted by sex and violence in the media, in communities, and even in the home. Genetically modified and hybrid foods are being forced upon farmers, and no one knows the long-range effects.

To each of these ills, there is a Kṛṣṇa conscious solution. And you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have addressed them all. That is what we want to continue to do with your newspaper. We want it to tackle the important issues that affect everyday lives so that our readers can equip themselves mentally and spiritually to find the Kṛṣṇa conscious response for themselves and their loved ones.

All around the world, your followers are busily engaged in serving Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement. By publishing their stories, we want to encourage devotees everywhere to find joy and courage in the execution of their spiritual activities, even when faced with great obstacles.

Since the newspaper began in 1981, our society has grown closer to maturity, and sometimes at great cost. In 1990 ISKCON’s Governing Body Commission (GBC) officially censured us for reporting on a controversial public debate in San Diego (filmed by ISKCON Television) between ritvikism proponents and ISKCON. We came within a hair’s breadth of being shut down. But we felt that the openness of the discussion was a sign that we were growing up as a society. This cropped up again when we covered problems at New Vṛndāvan and child abuse in ISKCON schools.

We felt the stakes were too high to completely gloss over these problems. We didn’t think ignoring them would make them go away. And we felt that you would want us to be forthright with our readers.

The ritvikism report and a few other articles tumbled us into the arena of internal debate. The timeless struggle between conservative and liberal had begun. We saw it as “creative tension,” a healthy but challenging development that spoke of unity in diversity. All this led to a more penetrating outlook, not only on ourselves but on the world around us.

As we enter the new millennium, internal differences are still with us. Some will be resolved and others will continue. But we feel that these issues are subordinate to a greater struggle: Godliness versus atheism and ultimate good against ultimate evil.

Our newspaper detailed a decade-long struggle at England’s Bhaktivedanta Manor that resulted in approval for public worship. For fourteen years we wrote about events leading up to the greatly reduced final settlement of a multi-million dollar judgment against ISKCON, alleging that we hid a teenage girl from her parents. From the newspaper’s inception in 1981 to the imprisoned Soviet devotees’ release in 1987, the editors had rallied support for worldwide protests against the Soviet government’s harassment. These three campaigns—among many others—are but a part of the greater story of how ISKCON asserts its mission to establish and propagate God consciousness in a godless world.

For a mountain climber, each successive step is a small triumph. And a big victory is waiting at the top. Of course, there are always other mountains. Your spiritual master said that the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement would be successful when the high-court judges wear *tilaka*. This is a lofty and difficult goal, but well worth striving for. Acceptance of such missions impossible is the basis and driving force behind *Hare Krishna World*.

Our clarion call for a Kṛṣṇa conscious world of the future now resounds with the determination and patience of maturing missionaries. We hope some day we can cover this and more—through a daily newspaper reaching the homes and hearts of millions. We know the odds are against us. But our faith to steadily endeavor with confidence comes from you. And with your mercy, we know that it is possible.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Hare Kṛṣṇa!

Your servants, the staff at Hare Krishna World, Alachua, Florida, USA.  
(written by Sarva-satya dāsa)

## Institute for Applied Spiritual Technology

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On behalf of the Institute for Applied Spiritual Technology and our spiritual master, His Holiness Bhakti-tīrtha Swami, we offer our most humble obeisances.

Although we are second-generation ISKCON devotees, we feel we know you very well. We see you in your pictures. We hear you in your books. We speak to you as we worship your *mūrti*. Our relationship is not bound by material restrictions, being based on the transcendental principles you have brought to us. How can one read the *Bhagavad-gītā* or *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and not feel one knows you, Śrīla Prabhupāda? Your unwavering focus, gentle compassion, and boundless humility are clearly seen in your books, your other instructions, and your wondrous accomplishments. And we see you very clearly in your son, our beloved Gurudeva. He is a living, dynamic manifestation of your causeless mercy and loving potency.

We offer you our humble obeisances and awe-inspired appreciation for the courage and extraordinary devotion you displayed in delivering to us the matchless gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Young children are totally dependent on their father and mother, but as they mature they appreciate the great sacrifices their grandfather undertook to make all they know and love available.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your servants at the Institute for Applied Spiritual Technology, Silver Spring, Maryland, USA.

## International Society for Cow Protection

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Follow the cow path.  
It is well worn, well traveled.  
Edged into the hillside  
By the passage of heavy hoofs.

Where will the cow path lead you?

If you have lost your way,  
Follow the cow path.  
The cows know the way home.

If you are lonely,  
Follow the cow path.  
The cows are not far ahead.

If you are thirsty,  
Follow the cow path.  
The cows know where the river flows.

If you are hungry,  
Follow the cow path.  
The cows know where the apple trees grow.

Follow the cow path.  
It is well worn, well traveled.  
Edged into the hillside  
By the passage of heavy hoofs.

Where will the cow path lead you?

If you are looking for life’s meaning,  
Follow the cow’s footprints on the cow path.  
Their four legs are the four pillars of religious life.

If you are looking for Lord Kṛṣṇa,  
Follow His footprints on the cow path.  
Follow His example of cowherd boy.

If you are seeking to please Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Follow the cow path, the path of cow *seva*.  
You will meet Lord Kṛṣṇa and His cows at the path’s end.

If you are feeling separation from Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Follow the cow path behind him, our eternal guide.  
By his merciful guidance you will not fall on the way.

When Lord Kṛṣṇa appeared, He purposefully became a cowherd boy and showed personally how to give protection to the cows and calves. Similarly, He showed respect to Sudāmā Vipra, a real *brāhmaṇa*. From the Lord’s personal activities, human society should learn how to give protection specifically to the *brāhmaṇas* and cows. Then the protection of religious principles, fulfillment of the aim of life, and protection of Vedic knowledge can be achieved. Without protection of cows, brahminical culture cannot be maintained; and without brahminical culture, the aim of life cannot be fulfilled. The Lord, therefore, is described as *go-brāhmaṇa-hitāya* because His incarnation is only for the protection of the cows and *brāhmaṇas*. Unfortunately, because in Kali-yuga there is no protection of the cows and brahminical culture, everything is in a precarious position. If human society wants to be exalted, the leaders of society must follow the instructions of *Bhagavad-gītā* and give protection to the cows, the *brāhmaṇas,* and brahminical culture. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 8.24.5, purport\]

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s servants at the International Society for Cow Protection and the ISKCON Ministry of Cow Protection and Agriculture.

## ISKCON Education of North Carolina, Inc.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

The vision you gave us for a perfect society has yet to be realized. As part of accomplishing the realization of that vision, we need the children who are being raised in Kṛṣṇa consciousness to have a firm and deep understanding of the philosophy and lifestyle you have taught us. Further, they need to be prepared to live in the modern world as Vaiṣṇavas with exemplary character and behavior.

Please help us to assist you. Surely Lord Kṛṣṇa has already accomplished His mission, and we can simply hope to be His instruments. But why should He accept us who are so fallen? Only by your intercession and intervention will Kṛṣṇa use us to further the glorious work of Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Please let our *gurukula* become both academically and spiritually successful even beyond what we can imagine. May our students become an example to the world of perfect Kṛṣṇa conscious ladies and gentlemen.

Your servants at ISKCON School in Hillsborough, North Carolina, USA.

## ISKCON Moscow Press Center

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances into the dust of your glorious lotus feet!

One more difficult year has passed since your last Vyāsa-pūjā. Now I can see more distinctly that if you hadn’t been a loving grandfather, the loss of my spiritual father would have been fatal to my wretched self. But in spite of the calamity, I’m still capable of serving your eternal mission. By your divine grace you sent me such able assistants, and they’re doing so nicely!

Russians are still troubled by political and economic instability. They need your wonderful books even more than others do. Nonetheless, many reject them just as a small, diseased child rejects the medicine that can save him.

The ISKCON Moscow Press Center is trying to build a bridge between the ignorant public and your books. This is why we regularly issue a media bulletin called “Hare Kṛṣṇa Today,” write articles for the Russian press neutralizing derogatory publications, send news releases with the Vedic view on current events, and cultivate journalists. This is also why we have prepared the first issue of an externally mundane Russian-Indian full-color mass-market quarterly magazine called *Parallels,* which shows many cultural, spiritual, linguistic, and socio-economic parallels between the Russians and Indians. By your divine grace, an old friend of ISKCON who is a professional designer and artist has joined us and made the magazine look very attractive.

Please, by your divine grace, send us a sponsor to print the first issue of *Parallels* so we can spread your glory all over the place! Please continue to shower your mercy upon your still immature grandchildren at the ISKCON Moscow Press Center.

Your servants at the ISKCON Moscow Press Center, Russia.  
(written by Vipina-purandara dāsa)

## ISKCON Television (ITV)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

In the material world, the farther away from an object one is, the smaller it appears. However, just the opposite is true in your case. As the years separate us from your physical presence with ever more distance, inconceivably, your stature continues to grow. Although you were shorter than most of your disciples, even when your *vapu* was present you always appeared to tower over us. You had an aristocratic manner, a self-confidence, a peacefulness, and an omniscience that we all looked up to. Twenty-three years later, you loom larger than life itself. I can understand that the more we see the way *māyā* tricks us to choose profit and adoration in the name of devotional service, the more we can see how humble you remained despite all the opulences offered to Your Divine Grace. Nobody ever felt that you had some ulterior motive or political agenda in any of your relationships. Your intentions were always pure—and always perceived to be pure. None of the devotees questioned your sincerity. Now your lofty example lives as the Mt. Meru of *bhakti-yoga*.

Since last year’s auspicious celebration of your transcendental appearance, ITV has completed *The Disciple of My Disciple,* which presents our understanding of your desire for your disciples to continue the tradition of the *paramparā*. The interviews took me face to face with some of your most intimate disciples, and I could see them sincerely trying to carry out your desire. Of course, nobody can act as you did, but by your mercy the process of initiations is going on. Their memories of those final days with you were more than just touching; they illuminated to me the consistency of your teachings. They recalled how even as your body deteriorated you maintained your unwavering composure in continuing to share your Kṛṣṇa consciousness with all who were present. We feel privileged to share this glimpse of your greatness with those who were not there.

The years ravage my body,  
But unfulfilled desires  
Pulse through my veins and  
I attempt to satisfy my senses  
Again and again.

Hope, the drug against the pains,  
Sees no horizon,  
So I continue  
My futile attempts for gratification.  
I find no fertile ground,  
I find no peace of mind  
In the material merry-go-round,  
Although my search for answers  
Has been satisfied by you  
Again and again.

Perhaps with the passage of time  
And your inconceivable mercy  
I will, at last, surrender to your  
Transcendental instructions,  
Endure the discipline of attentive hearing,  
Relinquish the notion  
Of being the enjoyer  
Again and again.

We were fortunate to have been donated six unpublished photos of you, which we have given to the Bhaktivedanta Archives library. From the pictures, you are head and shoulders above anyone else around you.

We beg to remain  
Your aspiring servants at ITV, Los Angeles, California, USA.  
(written by Nṛsiṁhānanda dāsa)

## Krishna Fest

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble and respectful obeisances to your divine lotus feet.

How can we possibly properly glorify your transcendental qualities and the activities you performed while personally present in this world? You came here out of your infinite mercy toward us conditioned souls, who from time immemorial have had the tendency to enjoy independently from Kṛṣṇa. Being covered by the three modes of material nature, we have forgotten our eternal relationship with the Supreme Lord.

Who can properly glorify the great effort and sacrifices you made when you left Vṛndāvana and came to the West? You associated with people whose only interest was sense gratification and elevated them to the platform of devotional service.

Your ability to use everything in Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s service by employing your immaculate intelligence has taught us to direct our lives to the Supreme Lord. How can we possibly adequately glorify your mercy in teaching us that? Whoever thinks he can do so is a clown. The only thing we can do is try to glorify your name and set a good example so others will also glorify your name.

Once you said that whoever wanted to know you could know you through your books. We didn’t have the chance to meet you in person, but we have had the chance of knowing you through your books. And we see how as the years go by more and more persons are glorifying you because of contact with your books.

The members of our small Krishna Fest party pray: Please give us your mercy and the strength to take it, so we can continue in this great family you have created, thus helping you in your great mission of taking everyone back home, back to Godhead.

Aspiring to be the servants of your servants, we remain

The Krishna Fest traveling saṅkīrtana party in Argentina.

## Māyāpur Institute of Higher Education

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances on this most auspicious day of your appearance. All glories to you, your transcendental books, and your transcendental movement!

Shortly after starting ISKCON you made it clear to your followers that you wanted Māyāpur to be established as an educational center where devotees from around the world could come to receive training in all aspects of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Then in January 1976 you wrote in a letter:

Bhakti-sastri, Bhakti-vaibhava, Bhaktivedanta, and Bhakti-sarvabhauma. All our brahmanas and anyone wanting to become brahmana, will have to sit for examination once a year at Mayapur.

Clearly the fulfillment of this vision will provide a tremendous facility to keep your movement strong and pure.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are greatly inspired by this vision and are eager to be your instruments in executing your will. Please mercifully and intensely continue to engage us in this ecstatic service to ISKCON devotees from around the world.

Thank you.

Your servants at the Māyāpur Institute of Higher Education.

## Ministry of Education Development

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**“I was born in the darkness of ignorance, and my spiritual master opened my eyes with the torch of knowledge. I offer my respectful obeisances unto him.”**

This *maṅgalācaraṇam* verse succinctly captures the purpose and intent of the educational process. Education is meant to bring one to a higher state of awareness, to liberate one from misconceptions, illusions, and doubts. Most enlightened thinkers throughout the ages have equated education with notions of enlightenment and liberation. While there appears to be much common ground among educators concerning the purpose of education, the means for approaching this destination, and the criteria for assessing whether we have achieved it, open the field for vast interpretation.

Sometimes, in a vain attempt to classify and categorize your instructions on education in general and Gurukula in particular, we find ourselves wedging deep divisions between various camps of opinion. In an attempt to bring some sense of rationality and method to our discussions, we have formulated twelve guiding principles from your collected teachings and your personal example. This is a work in progress, but here we would like to present these principles with the intention of providing a framework for common reference. Education requires such a demanding complexity of considerations that any reckless attempt to discuss it invariably produces confusion and bewilderment.

ISKCON education is governed by:

1\. Śrīla Prabhupāda’s teachings.

2\. A conducive environment

3\. Respect for the individual

4\. Character formation

5\. Qualified Personnel

6\. Qualified students

7\. Long-term vision

8\. Clarity of purpose

9\. Consideration of time, place, and circumstances

10\. Appropriate utilization of resources

11\. The dormant Kṛṣṇa consciousness of the *jīva*

12\. Attachment to Kṛṣṇa and detachment from *māyā*

Based on the definition of a principle being an axiomatic truth and, as you have explained in the introduction to *Śrī Īśopaniṣad*, universally applicable, we have attempted to bring the discussion of our educational endeavors within the realm of a manageable framework. Of course, since each of these categories is in itself open to unlimited interpretation, we might not expect much in the way of conclusive solutions. Still, as you so ably demonstrated in your personal efforts to establish this society, guiding principles are indispensable, and therefore we offer these as a starting point for rational dialogue among thoughtful devotees.

Providing an education consistent with our overriding goal of *prema-pum-artha* is no small feat. How to instill within our children the desire to willfully perform devotional service, to develop Vaiṣṇava character, to be respectful, responsible, and righteous, to act with integrity and discrimination, to be attached to Kṛṣṇa and detached from *māyā—*this is a challenge we collectively bear as guardians of your legacy. As an exemplar of Vaiṣṇava character, you lived these principles. You taught by example, by your personal responses, by your total commitment and absorption in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Education and empowerment were for you synonymous.

In appreciating the empowering aspect of the spiritual master who reveals the truth to the disciple, we do so with the understanding that this revelation necessitates a commitment and dedication to remaining on this level of awareness. In an attempt to develop educational systems that are empowering, we need to continually reevaluate and check ourselves to make sure that we are in line with these principles and that all concerned parties are progressing to higher stages of awareness. You captured the essence of education as a systematic and deliberate attempt to train the individual to become a competent thinker.

In a 1973 conversation with Bhakti Svarūpa Dāmodara Mahārāja, you emphatically proclaimed that the sole purpose for sending a child to school is to teach him how to think, to become inquisitive, to ask the “Why” question. “This,” you said, “is education,” this is the human form of life. Until we are trained to think, we remain on the animal platform. In the *Bhagavad-gītā,* while describing the quality of peacefulness, you conclude that our whole quest for sanity revolves around the thinking process.

The power of thought should be developed in association with persons who are authorities in the scriptures, saintly persons and spiritual masters, and those whose thinking is highly developed. \[*Bhagavad-gītā* 10.5, purport\]

Education as enlightenment, then, may be equated with a systematic and deliberate approach to teaching our children how to think. Not indoctrinating them, not isolating them, but empowering them to think for themselves, to evaluate, to analyze and to become creative, dynamic young Vaiṣṇavas dedicated to reasonableness and clarity of thought.

Please bless us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that we can inspire our dependents to become empowered representatives of your legacy. That they may develop the fine qualities of discrimination you unceasingly exemplified throughout your life as a preacher. That they may act with integrity and be proud of their Vaiṣṇava inheritance. Finally, we pray that in our attempts to impart these qualities to our children we may develop them in ourselves, for possessing them is an unquestionable prerequisite for imparting them to others.

Your aspiring servants at the Ministry of Education Development, in Vṛndāvana, India.  
(written by Prāṇa dasa)

## The Oxford Project

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet and our words of homage to Your Divine Grace.

His Holiness Mukunda Goswami informs us that on the very day you arrived in England for the first time in 1969, with your first words to your eagerly waiting disciples you expressed your desire that a center be opened in Oxford. Later you visited here and gave what Mukunda Mahārāja calls a “stirring program” in the Town Hall. You also brought devotees to Oxford to investigate a “redundant” church that was on the market, and Mukunda Mahārāja acted under your order to pursue renting or purchasing it as a temple. Several years have passed, and now a center is under way by the strength of your desire and the determination of devotees to see your Society represented in this important seat of Western learning.

We call your center “the Oxford Project” because we see it truly as a “project” in the dictionary sense of “a supplementary long-term assignment given by a teacher to students,” as well as “a large or important undertaking.” We see our mission here mainly in terms of study and teaching as important elements of “preaching.” You sometimes referred to your disciples as your students, suggesting the Western paradigm of teacher-student relationship, with its encouragement of critical inquiry, and indeed *Teachings of Lord Caitanya* is the name you gave to your summary study of the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta.* Universities pride themselves on safeguarding the spirit of inquiry, and we want to, with your blessings, develop this embryonic Project in such a way that it may gain solid footing and enduring presence in this world-renowned higher educational institution.

We can report to you that in the twenty-three years since your physical departure from us, a gradual positive shift in attitude among academics indicates a growing acceptance of scholars representing varied faith traditions, including Vaiṣṇava scholars. We realize increasingly the necessity of becoming mature in all aspects of our acting and speaking in order to properly represent you and all the Vaiṣṇava *ācāryas,* a necessity which involves very judicious presentation of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in this arena of critical intellectual scrutiny.

All of us present here—your disciples Mañjarī-devī dāsī, Rāsamaṇḍala dāsa, and Kṛṣṇa-kṣetra dāsa, your grand-disciples Śaunaka Ṛṣi dāsa, Hari-devī dāsī, Īśvara-Kṛṣṇa dāsa, Rādhā-Mādhava-Sakhī-devī dāsī, Bhakta-vatsala dāsa, and Rādhikā-ramaṇa dāsa, and your followers Dave and Ronnie and several congregational members—all pray for your kind blessings to make our presence here to your liking and satisfaction as we individually and collectively work to imbibe Lord Caitanya’s teachings and make them available to others, “giving everyone the opportunity to hear about Kṛṣṇa.”

*Your eternal servants at the Oxford Project, Oxford, UK.*  
(written by Kṛṣṇa-kṣetra dāsa)

## Padayātrā India

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Last year was very successful for Padayātrā India. On Rādhāṣṭamī we celebrated fifteen continuous years on the roads of Bhāratavarṣa. Walking from village to village, town to town, we have been distributing your books at a steadily increasing rate, from 5,000 to 10,000 small books and 2,000 to 4,000 big books per month. From the sales of books we are becoming more and more financially self-sufficient.

Now we are looking to establish a permanent Padayātrā *āśrama* at the ISKCON temple in Pandharpur, Maharashtra. This would be a place where *padayātrīs* could rest, study, and recuperate from time to time. It would also serve as a place for care and eventual retirement of our hard-working bullocks.

Padayātrā is a wonderful program for new devotees to receive training. The devotees follow a regulated schedule of book distribution, reading, *kīrtana,* preaching lectures in the evenings, *prasādam* distribution, and school preaching, all in the course of one day, not to mention following your prescribed routine of *maṅgalārati, japa,* class, *guru-pūjā,* and *sandhyā-ārati.* Padayātrā is a paradise for *brahmacārīs*.

This past year His Holiness Lokanāth Swami visited Padayātrā many times, and also His Grace Jaya Vijaya, ex-Padayātrā manager of eleven years. They provided much inspiration for us in our services.
