We pray that we will continue to be blessed by their association and that of other senior devotees who are able to walk with us.

We thank them and we thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for being with us each day, not only in your *mūrti* form on the bullock cart, but by our side helping us overcome our difficulties in Kṛṣṇa consciousness so we can remain strong to continue the preaching work on your behalf.

Finally, we would like to report that we have inaugurated a new Padayātrā cart for Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Gaurasundar and Your Divine Grace to ride on. Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Gaurasundar are fulfilling Their own prediction of bringing the chanting of the holy name, the *yuga-dharma,* to every town and village.

Please bless us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and give us the strength and intelligence to become better instruments in Their hands.

Your spiritual grandchildren and servants on Padayātrā in India.

## Prahlada and Kunti Devi Hostels

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

We know that you always desired that Kṛṣṇa consciousness be preached among the student community. It is to carry out this desire of yours that we have established the first two Kṛṣṇa conscious student hostels in the world, namely Prahlada Asrama and Kunti Devi Asrama, in the Indonesian city of Bandar Lampung.

During your days as a student at Scottish Churches’ College in Calcutta, you never forgot Kṛṣṇa even for a moment, nor did you forget Him at any time during your life. However, most students of today hardly remember Kṛṣṇa at all; indeed, they remember and contemplate everything *except* Kṛṣṇa. As stated in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* traditionally it has been most difficult to teach mundane students Kṛṣṇa consciousness. The same is certainly true today, even in India.

However, we know that your mission is to give Kṛṣṇa consciousness to everyone, regardless of their position. Therefore, we are thankful that you have inspired some of your disciples and grand-disciples to establish these two hostels where we now reside, so that as students we can complete our education and at the same time become fixed in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Surely without this mercy and kindness of yours we would also surely be drowning in the waves of *māyā,* following the general populace.

You have kindly taught us that no education is complete without this *rāja-vidyā,* the king of education, Kṛṣṇa consciousness. And you are surely the greatest professor of this supreme science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

We pray to always be engaged in the service of your servants.

Your spiritual grandchildren at Prahlada and Kunti Devi Hostels, Bandar Lampung, Indonesia.

## Radio Krishna Centrale

My beloved spiritual master Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble and respectful obeisances at your divine lotus feet.

This earth planet is glorious because you have walked on her surface. Today is Your Divine Grace’s appearance day, and it is a joyful occasion.

Still, “appearance” reminds me of “disappearance,” and my mind wonders...where are you now? Then my vision broadens and I see you.

You are here in the pages of your books,  
in your bedroom, lighted all night long.

You are here in the words of every lecture,  
where it’s clear what’s right and what is wrong.

You are here with your ISKCON and *prasādam,*  
when we preach to the lost and fallen souls.

You are here when we meet any devotee  
and welcome him by chanting *haribol*!

You are here on the *vyāsāsana* in every temple,  
be it *mahā,* large, medium, or just small.

You are here while performing early duties  
with the Deities, Tulasī-devī, et al.

You are here on the Internet and Com,  
even when some discussion gets hot.

You are here in your teachings and instructions,  
which are what we’re all made of and all we’ve got.

You are here when I’m dreaming of you walking,  
then you stop by and touch me on my head.

You are here in the strength of my recallings  
and every time I hear “Prabhupāda said...”

You are here in your photos gently smiling,  
with your pure love that captured our hearts.

You are here even now while I am writing,  
and I know that you never did depart.

You are here. All that I’ve said is surely true...  
nonetheless this fallen soul is missing you.

Your humble servants at Radio Krishna Centrale, in Terni and Florence, Italy.  
(written by Tridaṇḍi dāsa)

## Rūpānuga Vedic College

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances on this most auspicious day of your appearance.

Once you were asked if you could show some miracles. In reply you gestured toward your disciples and said, “This is my miracle, to turn *mlecchas* into Vaiṣṇavas.” Recently some stories illustrating your “miracle” came to our attention and we would like to present them before you.

A man who had been searching for God without success happened to be visiting a friend of his in a Colorado mountain cabin. His friend had to leave. Left alone and looking over the books in the library, he was drawn to your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is*. While reading, he became transcendentally enlivened and could not stop until he had finished the book. From that point on, his life changed. Reading that eating *bhoga* was sin, he reduced his eating to only two slices of bread and one small cup of rice, thinking that if he must eat sin, better to eat as little as possible. He resolved to surrender at your lotus feet and is presently at the Rūpānuga Vedic College, fully engaged in devotional service. The change in his person is apparent. He has become a happy dancing devotee, even mentioning that he never danced before coming to your shelter but now he just cannot stop.

Another young man was engaged in a very lucrative business and enjoyed life with many friends. One day a *saṅkīrtana* devotee gave him one of your books on the street. Upon reading that book, he immediately gave up meat-eating, intoxication, gambling, and illicit sex. Presently he is at the Rūpānuga Vedic College happily engaged in devotional service.

Some time ago one of your book distributors was standing in a parking lot. A gentleman drove up with his family and, pointing to the book in the devotee’s hand, asked, “Is that *The Nectar of Devotion*?” After purchasing the NOD and speaking with the devotee for a short time, he confided, “I have never met devotees or been to a temple, but I just love reading the swami’s books.” He purchased another book and promised to visit the temple soon.

Recently we received a phone call from a seventy-eight-year-old gentleman, Willie, who revealed that he was dying of cancer in a nearby hospital. He had seen your ISKCON devotees chanting *harināma* in public. He was very inquisitive as to his next destination after death. At the end of the discussion he asked very seriously, “Now that I am dying, what should I do?” We answered that he should simply chant the holy name. On the phone we instructed him how to chant the *mahā-mantra,* and he very sincerely learned. A few days later we received a call from his son. It turns out that Willie’s last words were Hare Kṛṣṇa. He had requested his son to inform us of that fact.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your books are the ultimate source of all fulfillment, just like streams of nectar from the Lord’s lotus feet. Those who associated with you in your *vapu* form were spiritually enlivened. Association with you in your book form has the same effect. Conditioned souls are still taking advantage of your association, giving up material life and developing pure love of Kṛṣṇa. You are still turning *mlecchas* into Vaiṣṇavas.

We are eternally grateful to you. Our debt to you is immeasurable, and although we are helplessly harassed by the modes of material nature and plagued by so many *anarthas,* still we are trying to offer whatever little service we can.

We humbly beg that you accept us as your servants and always keep us fully engaged in your “miraculous” mission.

Your servants at the Rūpānuga Vedic College, in Kansas City, Kansas, USA.

## The Saraswati Charitable Trust

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to your unceasing mercy upon the fallen, deluded souls suffering in this temporary world—namely, ourselves!

Having just returned from the Gaura Pūrṇimā festivals in Śrīdhām Māyāpur and Śrī Vṛndāvana *dhāma,* I can say that your mercy is indeed still pouring down without limit. This is especially evident from those wonderful devotees who are simply trying to follow your instructions with devotion and with determination. Through them, I can feel your presence.

For example, as we circumambulated around the Māyāpur Deity altars one morning before greeting of the Deities, Jananivāsa Prabhu very sweetly reminded me of a few services I had done in the past in Śrīdhām Māyāpur (I used to dress the Deities with him in the early 1970’s). Śrīla Prabhupāda, he told me that oftentimes, when you would take your morning walks, I used to sing the “Udila Aruṇa” *bhajana* over the temple PA system with one Bengali devotee, and how that *bhajana* would drift over the rice-paddy fields where you would hear it. He told me how much you appreciated that. Then he reminded me of how I had introduced the chanting of the *Puruṣa Sūkta* while he would bathe *śālagrāma-śilā* Gadādhara (the movement’s first *śālagrāma-śilā*). And how they still do that today. He gently offered these remembrances to me with a very warm appreciative smile and with much kindness.

Until Jananivāsa Prabhu jogged my memory, I had completely forgotten these little events of twenty-five years ago. After all, they were really pretty insignificant. Or rather, utterly insignificant. Every single one of us has done so many kinds of devotional services over the years. Preaching, book distribution, *prasadam* distribution, chanting, dancing, touching our heads to the temple floor. But in the broadest sense, compared with what you have accomplished, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I think it would be fair to say that whatever we may have accomplished, no matter how we may look at it, is all ultimately insignificant. Insignificant with your mercy! Of course, if we were to humbly, purely, and completely surrender to your mercy, things could be much different for ourselves and for everyone living in this world.

So of what real lasting importance could there be in these very simple services done twenty-five years ago? Activities so insignificant that I had completely forgotten about them?

Well, what I learned from Jananivāsa Prabhu that day in Māyāpur was that although our actions and attempts to serve may be utterly insignificant, they are never forgotten. Not only are they never forgotten, but they are actually appreciated with an abundance of love and sweet causeless mercy. And that is the inconceivable blessing that you brought to us, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You gave to us a process of approaching the Supreme Lord that is so wonderful and so intimate that our many childlike, stumbling devotional efforts can somehow actually reach Him and please Him.

Each and every one of us should be encouraged to discover a way to express our personal devotional service to the Lord. Our own personal expression. Regardless of anything that may be happening around us! Fortunately, none of our insignificant services will ever be forgotten. Following your example, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we must deepen our desires to lovingly serve and plant the seeds of Kṛṣṇa consciousness everywhere with greater and greater determination. And following your instruction, we must keep visiting the holy *dhāmas*. By doing so we will become spiritually nourished, refreshed, and blessed with renewed determination by associating directly with the spiritual world, the Lord, and His wonderful devotees, of whom you are the very best of all! All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Your insignificant servants at The Saraswati Charitable Trust (TSCT)..*  
(written by Satyanārāyaṇa dāsa)

## Society for Cow Protection

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to you and to this glorious day of your appearance!

We never met personally, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but I will always remember the day when you came into my life through your books and your instructions. Many years have gone by since then, and your association has become more and more important for me from day to day, year to year. Now I can say that you are the most important person in my little, insignificant life. I cannot do anything else but love you with all my heart and desire your association more and more, since you have been always here for me, guiding me, instructing me, and helping me whenever I turned to you in need. I have learned to associate with you and to live with you. You have become so important in my life. You are my support, adviser, well-wisher, and lord.

You are present in my life not only through your instructions, but also through your representatives—my spiritual master and your sons and daughters and grandsons and granddaughters. Thanks to you, I have the association of highly elevated devotees, who help me serve you and be part of your mission. I do little things, but I feel I am part of your mission and a member of your ISKCON family. I live as you wished your disciples to live. I have left the association of *karmīs,* become a faithful wife to my husband, and a loving and surrendered mother to my children. Thanks to your wish and instructions that we should protect cows, I haven’t sold my cow but take care of her and her offspring with the help of your dedicated disciples and representatives from ISCOWP. In this way we lead this little Society for Cow Protection, called GOPALA. Thanks to you, my sons and I earn our living by distributing *prasādam,* and I can only say thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for all of that. I thank you for making it possible for us to live such a life full of meaning, purification, and hope. What do we hope for? That the day will come when we shall be prepared to leave everything in this material world and return back to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa and our eternal service.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I cannot glorify you with great words or great deeds, but only with my insignificant life, which became bright and nice when you appeared in it. I hope, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that you are satisfied with me, and I shall sincerely try to serve and satisfy you more and more. You know that I am prepared to do whatever you and your representatives wish from me. I live for serving you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and ask you to always engage me in your service. I am yours and live for serving you and your servants lifetime after lifetime.

Whoever has met you has seen what a great personality, what a great *ācārya,* you are. You have saved and changed the whole universe by your presence, as you have saved and changed the little one I am in. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for everything. May everyone hear your glories and be saved by you.

Your servants at the Society for Cow Protection in Novi Sad, Serbia.  
(written by Parameśvarī-devī dāsī)

## Tauranga

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances on this sublime day. All glories to you!

February 1961. Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Vyasa-puja offering to Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī (excerpts):

Even now, my Godbrothers, you return here on the order of our  
master, and together we engage in this *pūjā*.

But simply a festival of flowers and fruits does not constitute  
worship. The one who serves the message of the guru really worships him....

Oh shame! My dear brothers, aren’t you embarrassed?...  
But just take a look at the terrible situation that has arisen....

From the seas, across the earth, penetrate the universal shell;  
come together and preach this Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

August 2000. Vyāsa-pūjā offering to Śrīla Prabhupāda.

ISKCON devotee (a bit embarrassed):

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I would like to express myself honestly to you today, and quite honestly...

ISKCON’s *full* of problems,  
It’s very plain to see.  
Someone should really *do* something—  
The leaders I mean...not me.

After all, what can I do?  
I’m only little me.  
I’m broke and sick and a woman to boot,  
and pushing forty-three.

Śrīla Prabhupāda (humbly):

The Gauḍīya Maṭh was at war,  
It was plain for all to see.  
But someone had to fulfill the wish of  
Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī.

I thought, “Now what can I do—  
a *jīva* small and tiny,  
forty rupees and two heart attacks,  
a lone old Indian swami?”

Devotee:

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.  
Your example...well, it’s plain to see.  
The future of your ISKCON  
Is up to all us leaders, including me.

Your servants in Tauranga, New Zealand.

## The Vaiṣṇava Institute of Higher Education of North America

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, in your teachings you have provided the world with the seeds for a spiritual and cultural revolution. Of course, such a revolution is not one of fanaticism and mob rule. Rather, it is one of individual people gaining the inner inspiration to turn to Kṛṣṇa in devotion, then helping to inspire others to do the same. Witnessing this, we feel that it is important to note that no matter how powerful a philosophy is, it is practically impossible for people to give their lives to it unless they have a living example and relationship with those who embody it. Therefore, in this offering, we would like to offer you our gratitude for two main reasons. First of all, you have mercifully carried to us the incomparable devotional philosophy of Lord Caitanya and the *ācāryas*. Moreover, you have lovingly drawn us to adopt this path by your personal example of uncompromising saintliness, as well as your loving reciprocation with us.

You have said in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* that “Everyone has dormant love for Kṛṣṇa, and by culture and education that has to be awakened. That is the purpose of this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement.” We beg you to bless us with the intelligence to understand this deep message of *bhāgavata* philosophy, and more importantly, with the conviction to live it. Then, with both knowledge and proper conduct, we may act as living testaments to your glory.

Your indebted servants at the VIHE of North America.

## Vṛndāvana Institute of Higher Education (VIHE)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet.

Recently I heard a tape of you discussing standards for your Gurukula system. Your voice was relaxed and matter-of-fact as you outlined how the children should eat, how long their classes should be, and so on. You sounded caring and concerned, but you didn’t sound like you were giving us orders etched in stone for the next ten thousand years. Yet we often take your statements in a fanatical and superficial way—as in the past case of Gurukula and many other things—and reap adverse results.

On the other hand, we sometimes would like to see your core teachings adjusted. Perhaps they are no longer applicable or practical. After all, times have changed. Or because so-and-so has said something different and more attractive, we are now following him rather than you.

Both approaches, in my estimation, are to be avoided.

I don’t want to minimize your teachings, but neither do I want to make a mockery of them by following them in a foolish manner.

This seems to be our big challenge in trying to serve you properly. My understanding, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is that if we change a principle you and the *śāstra* have established, we are finished. But I also understand that if we don’t adjust a detail when needed, we are also in trouble—we lose our pertinence, become open to *niyamāgraha,* and often treat fellow devotees harshly as we stick your quotes in front of each other’s faces in indignation.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I want to serve you, follow you, please you, and feel your presence. These days devotees go here and there and preach this and that. I hope they are happy and achieve success. For me, I want your smile, your glance, your blessings—*bās*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please guide me through these rough times. Enable me to stick to your service, avoid *aparādhas,* and get real about Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Please help me be chaste to the principles that you have taught, yet grant me the intelligence to adjust details, only when necessary, in a dynamic way to serve you and spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Your servants at the Vṛndāvana Institute of Higher Education (VIHE).  
(written by Braja Bihārī dāsa)

## Women’s Ministry

My dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble and fallen obeisances at your lotus feet.

The good fortune of coming into association with you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the most transcendental personality in the universe, is an unexplained phenomenon—truly a blessing sent from Kṛṣṇa. You have the power, either through your words or your presence, to spiritualize everyone with whom you come into contact, even the most misdirected, materialistic individuals. Thus, by your divine association you have miraculously transformed unsaintly characters into transcendental messengers.

You picked up souls lost in the depths of Māyā’s ocean and gave us *kṛṣṇa-kathā* and *prasādam*. We were your audience, and though we had deafened ears, you persisted in preaching and cracked our hardened hearts. Then, after purifying our polluted minds with transcendental knowledge, you sent us off to preach, extending your potency to reach the entire world, every town and village. You personally took us to Kṛṣṇa’s abode, allowed us to become a part of your transcendental pastimes, and endeared yourself to our hearts. You taught us that faith in Kṛṣṇa is all we need to conquer the world. You gave us a reason to live, a lifestyle to live by, and communities to live in. “Chant and be happy!” you instructed, and offered us Kṛṣṇa’s protection, free of charge. Your legacy continues to live on through your dedicated disciples, and your mercy continues to rain upon lost souls, providing a spiritual haven from the miseries of life.

As my protector, well-wisher, and beloved spiritual master, you have led me through the trials and tribulations of this world, even when I turned away. Māyā’s illusions are there to distract us; feeling confident, I took her hand and walked with her down the trodden path. But I have learned that Māyā’s path can be very treacherous, and without your mercy there is no shelter from the harsh realities of material life. Bewildered by this world, I am hopeless; however, your blessings give me strength to carry on, and from within me you direct my confused mind, always bringing me back to Kṛṣṇa for shelter. Your mercy is available for the taking at every minute of every day, and it’s up to each and every one of us to accept or reject the shelter you provide.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, may I be blessed to have the association of your devotees, to be engaged in your transcendental service, to distribute Kṛṣṇa philosophy to the unsuspecting, and to remain in the soothing shelter of your lotus feet.

Your unworthy servants at the Women’s Ministry.  
(written by Citralekhā-devī dāsī)

## Śrī Gurv-aṣṭaka

*by Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura*

saṁsāra-dāvānala-līḍha-loka  
trāṇāya kāruṇya-ghanāghanatvam  
prāptasya kalyāṇa guṇārṇavasya  
vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam (1)

The spiritual master is receiving benediction from the ocean of mercy. Just as a cloud pours water on a forest fire to extinguish it, so the spiritual master delivers the materially afflicted world by extinguishing the blazing fire of material existence. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master, who is an ocean of auspicious qualities.

mahāprabhoḥ kīrtana-nṛtya-gīta-  
vāditra-mādyan-manaso rasena  
romāñca-kampāśru-taraṅga-bhājo  
vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam (2)

Chanting the holy name, dancing in ecstasy, singing, and playing musical instruments, the spiritual master is always gladdened by the saṅkīrtana movement of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Because he is relishing the mellows of pure devotion within his mind, sometimes his bodily hairs stand on end, he feels quivering in his body, and tears flow from his eyes like waves. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master.

śrī-vigrahārādhana-nitya-nānā-  
śṛṅgāra-tan-mandira-mārjanādau  
yuktasya bhaktāṁś ca niyuñjato ’pi  
vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam (3)

The spiritual master is always engaged in the temple worship of Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. He also engages his disciples in such worship. They dress the Deities in beautiful clothes and ornaments, clean Their temple, and perform other, similar worship of the Lord. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master.

catur-vidha-śrī-bhagavat-prasāda-  
svādv-anna-tṛptān hari-bhakta-saṅghān  
kṛtvaiva tṛptiṁ bhajataḥ sadaiva  
vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam (4)

The spiritual master is always offering Kṛṣṇa four kinds of delicious food \[analyzed as that which is licked, chewed, drunk and sucked\]. When the spiritual master sees that the devotees are satisfied by eating bhagavat-prasādam, he is satisfied. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master.

śrī-rādhikā-mādhavayor apāra-  
mādhurya-līlā-guṇa-rūpa-nāmnām  
prati-kṣaṇāsvādana-lolupasya  
vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam (5)

The spiritual master is always eager to hear and chant about the unlimited conjugal pastimes of Śrī Śrī Rādhikā and Mādhava, and about Their qualities, names and forms. The spiritual master aspires to relish these at every moment. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master.

nikuñja-yūno rati-keli-siddhyai  
yā yālibhir yuktir apekṣaṇīyā  
tatrāti-dākṣyād ati-vallabhasya  
vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam (6)

The spiritual master is very dear, because he is expert in assisting the gopīs, who at different times make different tasteful arrangements for the perfection of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa’s conjugal loving affairs within the groves of Vṛndāvana. I offer my most humble obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master.

sākṣād-dharitvena samasta-śāstrair  
uktas tathā bhāvyata eva sadbhiḥ  
kintu prabhor yaḥ priya eva tasya  
vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam (7)

The spiritual master is to be honored as much as the Supreme Lord because he is the most confidential servitor of the Lord. This is acknowledged in all revealed scriptures and followed by all authorities. Therefore I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master, who is a bona fide representative of Śrī Hari \[Kṛṣṇa\].

yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo  
yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ’pi  
dhyāyan stuvaṁs tasya yaśas tri-sandhyaṁ  
vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam (8)

By the mercy of the spiritual master one receives the benediction of Kṛṣṇa. Without the grace of the spiritual master, one cannot make any advancement. Therefore, I should always remember and praise the spiritual master. At least three times a day I should offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of my spiritual master.
